\title{\textsc{Prime DNA}\\[1ex]
\textbf{The Floor of the Weil Form}\\[1ex]
\large The edge of the minimizer, the echo lattice,\\ and one inequality for the Riemann Hypothesis\\[1ex]
\normalsize Revised after independent review}
\author{Christophe Michaels\\ Independent Researcher\\ Houston, Texas}
\date{October 1, 2026}

\begin{document}
\maketitle

\begin{abstract}
Let $\lambda(a)$ be the floor of the odd-sector Weil form over test functions supported in $[-a,a]$. Weil's criterion, in Yoshida's odd-test form, says that $\lambda(a)\ge 0$ for every $a$ is equivalent to the Riemann Hypothesis; the floor is certified positive to $a=0.8$ (Zhu) and numerically decays doubly exponentially in $a$. We study the floor as a function of the window. Two results are exact. First, the archimedean part of the form on a window is one half of the Dirichlet form of the logarithmic Laplacian plus a bounded form, with an explicit confining wall $\tfrac12\log(1/(a-|y|))$ at the edge; by the boundary theory of the logarithmic Laplacian, a bounded minimizer is bounded by $C_0(\log(a/\delta))^{-1/2}$ at distance $\delta$ from the edge, and numerically it behaves like $C(a)(\log(a/\delta)+\beta)^{-1/2}$ with an amplitude $C(a)$ that we measure on meshes graded to $10^{-12}$ of the edge. Consequently the entry of a prime power into the window produces no kink in $\lambda$ but a soft kink, $\lambda'$ continuous with an increment of order $C^2/\log(1/\varepsilon)$ at scale $\varepsilon$, which we verify over four decades. Second, between entries the floor obeys the sliding-window identity
\[
a\,\lambda'(a) \;=\; -\Big[\,D_\infty(f)+D_P(f)+2P(f)^2+2P(f)M(f)\Big],
\]
proved for the $K$-mode form and, for the exact form, as a two-sided Dini-derivative statement holding at every point of differentiability, where $f$ is the minimizer, $D_\infty$ is the archimedean dilation form, whose symbol tends to $1$, $D_P(f)=-\sum_n 2\Lambda(n)n^{-1/2}\log n\; g_f'(\log n)$ is the Euler product differentiated against the derivative of the autocorrelation of $f$ at the prime-power positions, and $P,M$ are the polar moments. We evaluate its three terms in ball arithmetic on the $K$-mode minimizer and confirm the identity independently on the Fourier side; three order-one quantities cancel to the size of $\lambda$. The floor is continuous and non-increasing, and under a bounded-variation hypothesis on near-minimizers it is locally Lipschitz; every minimizer computed, on meshes graded to $10^{-12}$ of the edge and with up to $160$ modes, has total variation exactly $4\sup|f|$, being unimodal on each half window, so the regularity clause of the conjecture below reduces to that hypothesis. A third statement is numerical: on seven supports, with and without primes, $\lambda'(a)=-2C(a)^2$ to a part in a thousand (an eighth, at an entry, to $1.7\%$), the Hadamard formula one expects for this window problem, so that $\Phi'=2C^2/\lambda$ with $\Phi=-\log\lambda$. We prove this boundary law under the edge law with two derivatives, as a Pohozaev identity whose boundary term is the overlap of the leak of the dilation generator with the singular force outside the edge; and for the archimedean form, without primes, we prove the edge law itself from an exact Wiener--Hopf representation of the critical point: the edge of its transform is $A_0/(t\sigma_-(t))$ with $\sigma_-$ the outer factor of the symbol, the profile is $|A_0|\,(\Psi_\infty(1/\delta)-\lambda-\gamma)^{-1/2}$ with Euler's constant $\gamma$, verified to a part in a thousand with nothing fitted, and the law $\Phi'=\pi T_{\mathrm{eff}}$ stated below is exact for that form; with the prime $2$ inside the window the constants are unchanged, the edge is accompanied by its echo, and the law of the zeros is a theorem under RH for $a_2<a<a_3$. At every support below $a_\infty=0.843$, where the walks that the entries generate inside the window are confined, the echoes form a finite tree, the symbol of the edge is the Schur complement of the tree's adjacency, a finite continued fraction along each chain, the constants of the edge are unchanged and the law of the zeros is a theorem under RH; beyond $a_\infty$ the echo set is dense, the representation with the window's symbol remains exact, and the band that governs the onset of the law is the one the edge sees, the spectral measure of the confined echo lattice at the edge's coupling, whose mass lies within a few horizons at every support computed while its support reaches the spectral radius of the edge's component, several horizons at $a\ge2$ and growing: the law holds from five horizons on inside the region where the symbol of the window still changes sign. At every support the edge law and the boundary law are theorems under the boundedness hypothesis: above its periodic band the window's symbol factors expand in the lattice of the entries with coefficients indexed by the walks of the echo tree, a walk of $k$ steps cannot return to within $\tfrac12e^{-2ak}$ of its start, so the echoes that accumulate at an edge are long chains of small total weight, and the chains from the far edge across the window are the leading correction to the edge amplitude, found in the data at their predicted frequencies; under that hypothesis the Hypothesis reduces to the single inequality below. By the explicit formula, the defect of the zeros above a height against their mean density is the prime sum at the entries, the oscillating part of the symbol, equal to twice the square of the echoes' amplitudes averaged over the tail and verified on the zeros to a few parts in a million; so the two sampling hypotheses reduce to one statement about the minimizer alone, that the sharp tail law holds at a bounded multiple of the horizon. The derivative of the floor is a property of the ground state alone: the pencil $(-Q',Q)$ has eigenvalues of size $6\times10^{10}\,\Tst$ at $a=1$ while the minimizer's direction gives $3.7\,\Tst$, so no inequality between the two forms can hold. With the horizon $\Tst(a)=2\pi e^{2a}$, finite-mode upper bounds on the floor from height $17$ to height $130$ (down to $\lambda\approx10^{-93}$, with up to $230$ modes and rigorous enclosures of the truncated matrix's lowest eigenvalue beyond $a=1$) give $\Phi'/\Tst$ rising from $3.2$ to $3.9$ on coarse grids, and the Hellmann--Feynman derivative resolves a sawtooth at every prime-power entry: a rise into the entry, of $10$ to $45\%$ over the last $0.003$, as the form without the entering prime heads toward indefiniteness, a drop across it, and a relaxation after, with peaks $3.3$ at the entry of $2$ and $4.1$--$4.3$ at the entries of $3,4,5,7$. We state the bounded-relay conjecture, $\lambda$ locally absolutely continuous with $\lambda'\ge-c\,\Tst\lambda$ almost everywhere, which implies the Riemann Hypothesis by Gr\"onwall's inequality from Zhu's certified support, and the decay law $\Phi'/\Tst\to c_0$; the data exclude the raw phase-space count, disfavour the logarithmic law and the derivative of Zhu's even-sector Landau--Widom law, and favour a constant near $3.8$. Evaluated on the first $6000$ zeros of $\zeta$, the sum of $|F(\gamma)|^2$ over the zeros returns the floor of the $K$-mode minimizer to $3\times10^{-4}$ or better at $a=0.6,0.8,1.0$, down to $\lambda=10^{-26}$, and the zero-side dilation sum returns its derivative to $3\times10^{-3}$ or better: the explicit formula closes on the actual zeros for both, a third and independent measurement of the identity after the matrices and the Fourier symbol. The zeros below the horizon carry at most $10^{-5}$ of the floor, the first zero $10^{-24}$ of it at $a=1$, and the floor is made by the zeros between one and a half and three horizons. The fraction of the floor carried by the zeros above height $T$ is $\Phi'/(\pi T)$ from a few horizons on, on every minimizer computed (edge-FEM to $1\%$ over two decades of height, $K$-mode to $1$--$3\%$ down to $\lambda=10^{-93}$): the rate of decay of the floor is $\pi$ times the effective height of the zeros that carry it, $\Phi'=\pi T_{\mathrm{eff}}$, and the two conjectures say that this height is tied to the horizon. Under the Riemann Hypothesis the floor is a sampling constant for the zeros above the horizon, and a prolate-type count bounds $\Phi$ below by about half of what is observed. Nothing in this paper proves the Riemann Hypothesis. The last section states what is missing as one inequality: under the boundedness hypothesis, with the boundary law and the analytic tail law now theorems below $a_\infty$ and no zeros involved in either, the Hypothesis follows from $2|A_0(a)|^2\le c\,\Tst(a)\lambda(a)$ for all $a$, the square of the edge amplitude of the minimizer against its energy times the horizon; the inequality contains the Hypothesis, since it fails wherever the floor is negative, and in prime-side form it is a statement about the weighted multiplicative relations among the prime powers below $e^{2a}$, the moments of the spectral measure of the echo lattice at the edge. Taken on the plane, the zero side is the quadratic functional $4S(z)^2$ of the minimizer's transform at every zero wherever it lies, and the exponential rate of the negative part of the floor is the zeros' maximal distance from the line: the edge of the minimizer reads the plane, growing exponentially if the Hypothesis fails and decaying faster than any exponential if it holds with the conjecture, with no third behaviour. The main result is a theorem: under the boundedness hypothesis the Riemann Hypothesis follows from that one inequality, every other link being proved; the inequality is where the paper rests, its remainder in the dilation identity, the prime part, having no fixed sign. Theorems, computations with their precision, and conjectures are distinguished throughout.
\end{abstract}

\noindent\emph{Standalone research paper. Companion to} Primes, Folds, and the One Dot \emph{and to} Theta Kernels, Weil Positivity, and the Mathematical Theory Atlas. \emph{Revised after an independent referee report; the report's corrections are incorporated and acknowledged in Section~\ref{sec:lit}.}

\section*{Status conventions}
A \textbf{Theorem}, \textbf{Proposition} or \textbf{Lemma} without citation is proved in the text. A statement with a citation is taken from the literature and its hypotheses are stated. A \textbf{Computation} is a numerical result with its method, precision and cross-checks; a finite-mode minimum is a minimum over a subspace and hence an upper bound on the floor, and every such value is labelled by its mode count $K$; derivatives of finite-mode floors are Hellmann--Feynman derivatives of the $K$-mode form and are called so, never ``exact''. A \textbf{Conjecture} is a precise statement with the evidence for it. A \textbf{Heuristic} or \textbf{Remark} makes no mathematical claim. The hypothesis that a minimizer is bounded, needed for the edge law, is written (H$_\infty$); the hypothesis that near-minimizers have bounded sup norm times total variation, which gives Lipschitz continuity of the floor, is written (H$_{\mathrm{BV}}$); both are carried explicitly wherever they are used. A step of a proof marked \emph{sketch density} is written at the density of a research sketch, with its estimates stated but not isolated as lemmas; the verification notes at the end of Section~\ref{sec:everysupport} list every such step.


\section*{Main theorem}
\noindent\textbf{Theorem (the reduction).} \emph{Assume the boundedness hypothesis} (H$_\infty$) \emph{of Section~\ref{sec:edge}, that the minimizer of the odd Weil form on the window $[-a,a]$ is bounded. Let $\lambda(a)$ be the floor of the form, $\Tst(a)=2\pi e^{2a}$ the horizon, $A_0(a)$ the edge amplitude of the minimizer, and $a_Z=0.8$ the support to which the floor is certified positive. If for some constant $c$ and some locally integrable $e\ge0$}
\[
2\,|A_0(a)|^2\ \le\ \big(c\,\Tst(a)+e(a)\big)\,\lambda(a)\qquad\text{for almost every }a\ge a_Z ,
\]
\emph{then $\lambda(a)>0$ for every $a>0$ and the Riemann Hypothesis holds} (Theorem~\ref{thm:reduction}, Corollary~\ref{cor:unconditional}, Proposition~\ref{prop:late}).

\noindent Every other link is a theorem: the edge law of the minimizer and the boundary law $\lambda'=-2|A_0|^2$ at every support that is not an entry (Theorem~\ref{thm:everysupport}), the analytic tail law, the Wiener--Hopf representation of the transform with the window's symbol, the echo tree and its band. The inequality itself is not proved. It contains the Hypothesis, since it fails wherever the floor is negative; in the dilation identity $a\lambda'=-[D_\infty+D_P+2P^2+2PM]$ it asks for a sign of the remainder against $\Tst\lambda$, and the obstruction is the prime part $D_P$, which has no fixed sign. This is where the paper rests. In prime-side form the inequality is a statement about the weighted multiplicative relations among the prime powers below $e^{2a}$, the moments of the spectral measure of the echo lattice at the edge (Proposition~\ref{prop:edgeband}); and since the window reads an off-line zero only at second order in its distance from the line (Theorem~\ref{thm:blind}), the Hypothesis is a matter of structure, not of detection at any finite scale.

\section{The form, the floor and the horizon}\label{sec:form}

For $f\in L^2(\R)$ real with compact support write $F=\hat f$, $F(t)=\int f(x)e^{itx}\,dx$, and $g=f\star\tilde f$, $g(u)=\int f(x)f(x-u)\,dx$, so that $\hat g=|F|^2$. The Weil form in the normalization used throughout is
\begin{equation}\label{eq:Q}
Q(f) \;=\; W_\infty(g)\;-\;\sum_{n\ge2}\Lambda(n)n^{-1/2}\big(g(\log n)+g(-\log n)\big)\;+\;\big(\hat g(i/2)+\hat g(-i/2)\big),
\end{equation}
\begin{equation}\label{eq:Winf}
W_\infty(g) \;=\; -(\gamma+\log\pi)\,g(0)\;+\;2\int_0^\infty \frac{g(0)e^{-2x}-g(x)e^{-x/2}}{1-e^{-2x}}\,dx .
\end{equation}
By the explicit formula \cite{weil}, $Q(f)=\sum_\rho \hat g\big((\rho-\tfrac12)/i\big)$ over the nontrivial zeros; on the geometric side, in Fourier form,
\begin{equation}\label{eq:symbol}
Q(f)=\frac1{2\pi}\int_\R |F(t)|^2\,\Psi(t)\,dt \;+\;\text{polar},\qquad
\Psi(t)=\Ree\psi\big(\tfrac14+\tfrac{it}{2}\big)-\log\pi-\sum_{n}\frac{2\Lambda(n)}{\sqrt n}\cos(t\log n),
\end{equation}
where the polar term is $2F(i/2)^2$ for even $f$ and $-2P(f)^2$, $P(f)=\int f(x)\sinh(x/2)\,dx$, for odd $f$; the multiplier $\Ree\psi(\tfrac14+\tfrac{it}2)-\log\pi$ is that of $W_\infty$ by the integral representation of $\psi$. This is the normalization of Zhu \cite{zhu} and of Checkpoints 15--20 of \cite{michaels-atlas}; Computation~\ref{comp:zhu} confirms the identification numerically.

\begin{definition}
For $a>0$ the \emph{odd floor} is
\[
\lambda(a)=\inf\{\,Q(f):\ f \text{ odd},\ \operatorname{supp}f\subset[-a,a],\ \|f\|_2=1,\ Q(f)<\infty\,\},
\]
$\Phi(a)=-\log\lambda(a)$ wherever $\lambda(a)>0$, and the \emph{horizon} is $\Tst(a)=2\pi e^{2a}$. $N(T)$ denotes the number of zeros with $0<\gamma\le T$.
\end{definition}

Since $g$ is supported in $[-2a,2a]$, only the prime powers with $\log n<2a$ enter $Q(f)$; the \emph{entries} are $a_n=\tfrac12\log n$. The form $Q$ itself does not depend on $a$, so $\lambda$ is non-increasing.

\begin{theorem}[Weil \cite{weil}; Yoshida \cite{yoshida}, Prop.\ 1, as recorded in Suzuki \cite{suzuki-screw}]\label{thm:weil}
The Riemann Hypothesis holds if and only if $Q(f)\ge0$ for every compactly supported smooth $f$; and $Q(f)\ge0$ for every odd such $f$ already implies it. Hence $\mathrm{RH}\iff \lambda(a)\ge0$ for all $a>0$.
\end{theorem}

\begin{theorem}[Zhu \cite{zhu}, Theorem 6.2]\label{thm:zhu}
For $a=0.8$, in the odd sector, $8.206\times10^{-15}\le\lambda(0.8)\le 2.347\times10^{-14}$, certified by interval arithmetic (and in the even sector $8.9\times10^{-18}\le\lambda^{\mathrm{even}}(0.8)\le2.523\times10^{-16}$). In particular $\lambda(a)>0$ for $0<a\le0.8$.
\end{theorem}

The horizon is the height at which the zeros become too dense for the window. A function of exponential type $a$ has zeros of upper density at most $a/\pi$ (Jensen's formula; see Boas \cite{boas}); the zeros of $\zeta$ have density $(2\pi)^{-1}\log(t/2\pi)$ at height $t$, which equals $a/\pi$ exactly at $t=\Tst(a)$. Zhu's resolution threshold, $2\pi e^{(4+o(1))e^{a}}$ for a one-stroke positivity certificate at half-width $a$ \cite{zhu}, is a different and far larger scale. Everything below is organized around the pair $(\lambda(a),\Tst(a))$.

\begin{lemma}[existence, monotonicity, continuity, differentiability a.e.]\label{lem:exist}
For every $a>0$ the infimum defining $\lambda(a)$ is attained, the infimum over odd $C_c^\infty(-a,a)$ functions is the same number, $\lambda$ is non-increasing and continuous, and $\lambda$ is differentiable at almost every $a$.
\end{lemma}
\begin{proof}[Proof sketch]
By Proposition~\ref{prop:wall} and the discussion after it, $Q$ on $L^2_{\mathrm{odd}}(-a,a)$ is one half of the Dirichlet form of $L_\Delta$ plus a form bounded on $L^2$; the form domain $\{f:\int\log(2+|t|)|F|^2<\infty\}$ embeds compactly into $L^2(-a,a)$ \cite[Thm.\ 2.1 ff.]{chen-weth}, so $Q$ is bounded below and lower semicontinuous on the unit sphere and a minimizing sequence has a convergent subsequence. Density of $C_c^\infty$ in the form domain gives the second claim. Monotonicity is inclusion of the admissible sets, and a monotone function is differentiable almost everywhere. Continuity: for $a<a'$ and a minimizer $f'$ at $a'$, the dilate $f'_{a/a'}$ (notation of Section~\ref{sec:identity}) is admissible at $a$, so $0\le\lambda(a)-\lambda(a')\le Q(f'_{a/a'})-Q(f')$. Minimizers at supports in $[a,a+1]$ have form norm bounded in terms of $a$ and $\lambda(a)$ alone (the wall is positive, the prime and polar terms are bounded on the unit sphere), and on a form-bounded set $Q(f_b)-Q(f)\to0$ uniformly as $b\to1$: in the archimedean term because $|\Psi_\infty(s/b)-\Psi_\infty(s)|\le w_*|\log b|$ with $w_*=\sup_t|t\Psi_\infty'(t)|<\infty$, in the prime terms because $|g_f(u)-g_f(u')|\le\|f\|\,\|f(\cdot-u)-f(\cdot-u')\|$ and $\|f(\cdot-h)-f\|^2\le h^2T^2\|f\|^2+4\int_{|t|>T}|F|^2$ is uniformly small on a form-bounded set, in the polar term because $P$ is a bounded functional. Letting $a'\downarrow a$ gives right continuity; left continuity is $\lambda(a')\le Q(f_{a'/a})\to Q(f)=\lambda(a)$ as $a'\uparrow a$, for a minimizer $f$ at $a$. In particular the floor cannot jump.
\end{proof}

\section{Three engines}\label{sec:engines}

All computations use the orthonormal odd Legendre basis on the window, $f(y)=a^{-1/2}\sum_{i<K}c_iN_iP_{2i+1}(y/a)$, $N_i=\sqrt{(4i+3)/2}$, in which the matrix $Q_K(a)$ of \eqref{eq:Q} is assembled from the exact cross-correlations $g_{ij}(x)=\int\phi_i(u)\phi_j(u-x)\,du$ by Gauss--Legendre rules exact for the polynomial products. The \emph{$K$-mode floor} $\lambda_K(a)$ is the lowest eigenvalue of $Q_K(a)$; it is an upper bound on $\lambda(a)$, and $\lambda_1$ below denotes the second eigenvalue. Three implementations are used, all in the repository accompanying this paper \cite{michaels-repo}.

\begin{enumerate}[leftmargin=2em]
\item \texttt{rh\_weil\_odd.py}: mpmath, 50--60 digits, $K\le72$, $80$ outer quadrature nodes. Reproduces the floors and the failure points of \cite{michaels-folds} to 4--6 digits. Its outer rule is exact only to polynomial degree $159$, which costs $10^{-4}$ in relative accuracy at $K=72$.
\item \texttt{rh\_weil\_arb.py}: ball arithmetic (python-flint 0.9.0 over FLINT/Arb), $K\le230$, 500--800 bits, with certified Gauss--Legendre rules, $2K+8$ inner and $2K+100$ outer nodes, and a rigorous enclosure of the lowest eigenvalue of the assembled matrix. The Legendre recurrence is evaluated with a midpoint reset at each degree, since its ball version doubles the radius per degree; the enclosure therefore covers the assembly and the eigenvalue computation but not the polynomial evaluations, which are 500-bit floating point, nor the truncation of the outer rule. It reproduces the mpmath floors to twenty digits where both are converged and runs a $180$-mode point in six minutes.
\item \texttt{rh\_edge\_fem.py}: odd-extended hat functions on a mesh of the half window graded geometrically to $10^{-12}a$ (350--490 nodes; the ``two-stage'' mesh has ratio $0.9$ from $0.05a$ to $10^{-4}a$ and $\tfrac12$ below), exact cross-correlations, closed-form archimedean tails, double precision. Built to read the minimizer at the edge, where a Legendre truncation resolves only $\delta\gtrsim a/K^2$. Its amplitudes carry a mesh bias of $10^{-3}$ (prime-free) to $10^{-2}$ (full form).
\end{enumerate}

\begin{computation}\label{comp:zhu}
$\lambda_{40}(0.8)=1.5935\times10^{-14}$ and $\lambda_{64}(0.8)=1.5659\times10^{-14}$, both inside Zhu's certified odd interval of Theorem~\ref{thm:zhu}, which fixes the normalization and shows the truncation error at that support. At $a=0.3$, where no prime is inside the window, $\lambda_{64}(0.3)=0.2225569$ and the edge-FEM floor $0.2225566$ agree to $1.3\times10^{-6}$; at $a_3=\tfrac12\log3$ the $64$-mode profile and the FEM profile agree to $10^{-4}$ for $|y|\le0.99a$.
\end{computation}

\section{The archimedean wall and the edge law}\label{sec:edge}

\begin{proposition}[decomposition on the window]\label{prop:wall}
Let $J_\Gamma(s)=e^{-|s|/2}/(1-e^{-2|s|})$, $\mathcal J(x)=\int_x^\infty J_\Gamma=\operatorname{artanh}(e^{-x/2})+\arctan(e^{-x/2})$, and $c_1=-(\gamma+\log\pi)-\tfrac{\pi}{2}-3\log2$. For $f$ supported in $[-a,a]$,
\[
W_\infty(g_f)=c_1\|f\|^2+\tfrac12\iint_{[-a,a]^2}J_\Gamma(u-v)|f(u)-f(v)|^2\,du\,dv+\int_{-a}^a V_a(y)|f(y)|^2\,dy,\qquad
V_a(y)=\mathcal J(a-y)+\mathcal J(a+y),
\]
and $V_a(y)=\tfrac12\log\frac1{a-|y|}+\kappa_a+o(1)$ as $|y|\to a$, with $\kappa_a=\tfrac12\log4+\tfrac\pi4+\mathcal J(2a)$.
\end{proposition}
\begin{proof}
With $g(0)-g(x)=\tfrac12\int|f(u)-f(u-x)|^2du$, the integral in \eqref{eq:Winf} is $\tfrac12\iint_{\R^2}J_\Gamma(u-v)|f(u)-f(v)|^2$, and $2\int_0^\infty(e^{-2x}-e^{-x/2})/(1-e^{-2x})\,dx=\psi(\tfrac14)+\gamma=-\pi/2-3\log2$ by the integral representation of $\psi$. Splitting the double integral according to whether $v$ lies in the window gives the three terms, the two cross-regions contributing $\tfrac12\int V_a|f|^2$ each. The substitution $w=e^{-s/2}$ gives $\int_x^\infty J_\Gamma=\int_0^{e^{-x/2}}2\,dw/(1-w^4)=\mathcal J(x)$, and $\operatorname{artanh}(e^{-x/2})=\tfrac12\log(4/x)+o(1)$ gives the constant; at $a=a_3$, $\kappa_a=2.660623$, and the direct evaluation of $V_a-\tfrac12\log(1/\delta)$ at $\delta=10^{-9}$ gives $2.6606231$.
\end{proof}

The symbol of the whole-line form $\tfrac12\iint J_\Gamma|f(u)-f(v)|^2$ is $\sigma(\xi)=\int J_\Gamma(s)(1-\cos s\xi)\,ds=\log|\xi|+O(1)$, so on the window the archimedean form is one half of the Dirichlet form $\int\log(\xi^2)|\hat f|^2$ of the logarithmic Laplacian $L_\Delta$ of Chen--Weth \cite{chen-weth} plus a bounded quadratic form; the prime terms are bounded translation operators and the polar term has rank one. Write $Q(f)=\tfrac12\mathcal E_L(f)+\ip{Bf}{f}$ with $B$ bounded and self-adjoint on $L^2(-a,a)$ (its kernel is bounded on the window). The weak Euler--Lagrange equation of a minimizer is then
\begin{equation}\label{eq:EL}
L_\Delta f = 2(\lambda-B)f\quad\text{in }(-a,a),\qquad f=0\ \text{outside},
\end{equation}
tested against $C_c^\infty(-a,a)$.

\medskip
\noindent\textbf{Hypothesis (H$_\infty$).} The minimizer $f$ is bounded. \emph{This is not known to follow from the form domain, which embeds in no $L^p$, $p>2$; an $L^\infty$ bound of De Giorgi--Moser type for $L_\Delta$ with a bounded lower-order perturbation would supply it. Numerically $\max|f|=1.37$ at $a_3$ and $1.61$ at $a=0.3$.}

\begin{theorem}[edge law; Chen--Weth \cite{chen-weth}, Thm.\ 1.11; Hern\'andez-Santamar\'ia--L\'opez R\'ios--Salda\~na \cite{hls}, Thm.\ 1.1]\label{thm:edge}
Assume (H$_\infty$). Then $f$ is continuous on $\R$ and there is $C_0$ with
\[
|f(y)|\le C_0\,\big(\log\tfrac1{a-|y|}\big)^{-1/2}\qquad(|y|<a,\ a-|y|<0.1).
\]
In particular $f(\pm a)=0$.
\end{theorem}
\begin{proof}[Proof sketch]
By \eqref{eq:EL} and (H$_\infty$), $f\in H(\Omega)\cap L^\infty(\R)$ is a weak solution of $L_\Delta f=h$ with $h=2(\lambda-B)f\in L^\infty(\Omega)$, $\Omega=(-a,a)$, which is a Lipschitz domain with the uniform exterior sphere condition. \cite[Thm.\ 1.11]{chen-weth} gives continuity and the rate $(\log(1/\delta))^{-\tau}$ for every $\tau<\tfrac12$; \cite[Thm.\ 1.1]{hls} gives $\tau=\tfrac12$, with $\ell(r)=-1/\ln\min\{0.1,r\}$.
\end{proof}

The exponent $\tfrac12$ is sharp for the torsion function of a small interval \cite[Thm.\ 1.2]{hls}, and a Hopf-type lemma gives the rate from below for nonnegative supersolutions \cite[Thm.\ 1.4]{hls}; the minimizer here is odd, so the lemma does not apply to it directly and the lower rate is a numerical statement (Computation~\ref{comp:edge}).

\begin{heuristic}\label{heur:edge}
In $t=\log(1/\delta)$, $\delta=a-y$, equation \eqref{eq:EL} near the edge has the shape $t\,h(t)-\tfrac12\int_0^t h(\tau)\,d\tau=O(h)+O(1)$, the coefficient $t$ being $\tfrac12$ from the wall and $\tfrac12$ from the interior kernel, and the nonlocal term coming from the long-range part of the kernel, which in the variable $t$ is the constant $\tfrac12$ on one side; its solutions are $h=Ct^{-1/2}(1+O(1/t))$. We have not derived the equation in this form.
\end{heuristic}

Checkpoint 17 of \cite{michaels-atlas} applied Theorem~\ref{thm:edge} to the forced solutions of the same operator and left open the existence of the normalized trace $\lim\sqrt{\log(1/\delta)}\,f(a-\delta)$; the following computation is the numerical answer for the ground state.

\begin{computation}[the edge amplitude]\label{comp:edge}
On every mesh and with or without primes, $1/f(a-\delta)^2$ is linear in $t=\log(a/\delta)$ with relative residual below $10^{-4}$ over $14\le t\le20$; the local exponent $-d\log f/d\log t$ descends toward $\tfrac12$ exactly as $\tfrac12 t/(t+\beta)$ does. Writing $f(a-\delta)=C\,(t+\beta)^{-1/2}(1+o(1))$ on the two-stage mesh:
\begin{center}
\begin{tabular}{@{}lccccc@{}}
\toprule
form & $a$ & $\lambda$ (FEM) & $C$ & $\beta$ & mesh dependence of $C$\\
\midrule
prime-free & $0.3$ & $0.2225566$ & $1.3089$ & $-1.49$ & $10^{-3}$\\
full & $a_3=0.5493$ & $1.4914\times10^{-5}$ & $0.02412$ & $-1.87$ & $10^{-2}$\\
\bottomrule
\end{tabular}
\end{center}
The six $K$-mode endpoint values of \cite{michaels-folds} at $a_3$ ($f_K(a_3)=0.009471,\dots,0.008207$ for $K=24,\dots,64$) are this law read at the truncation's resolution: with the fitted $C,\beta$, the effective $t_K=C^2/f_K^2-\beta$ equals $2\log K+2.0$ within $0.3$ for all six, with and without primes, so a degree-$2K$ expansion resolves $\delta\approx e^{-2}a/K^2$ at the edge and $f_K(a)^2=C^2/(2\log K+\beta')$ with $\beta'=2+\beta$.
\end{computation}

\section{The relay is a soft kink}\label{sec:kink}

At an entry $a_n$ the new term $-2\Lambda(n)n^{-1/2}g(\log n)$ vanishes and grows with $a$.

\begin{proposition}[$K$-mode kink]\label{prop:kink}
For the $K$-mode form, $\lambda_K$ is continuous at $a_n$ and $\lambda_K'(a_n^+)-\lambda_K'(a_n^-)=4\Lambda(n)n^{-1/2}f_K(a_n)^2$, where $f_K$ is the normalized $K$-mode minimizer at entry.
\end{proposition}
\begin{proof}
For $a=a_n+\varepsilon$ and odd $f$, $g(\log n)=\int_{a-2\varepsilon}^{a}f(y)f(y-\log n)\,dy=-\int_0^{2\varepsilon}f(a-\delta)f(a-2\varepsilon+\delta)\,d\delta$, which is $-2\varepsilon f(a)^2+O(\varepsilon^2)$ for a function continuous at the edge; the $K$-mode minimizer is a polynomial. Hellmann--Feynman with the minimizer frozen gives the jump.
\end{proof}

\begin{computation}[the $K$-mode kink]\label{comp:kink}
With $24$ modes at the entry of $3$, the measured jump is $0.948$ of the prediction $2.276\times10^{-4}$ at step $5\times10^{-4}$ and $0.9998$ at step $10^{-5}$ (\texttt{rh\_kink\_steps.py} in \cite{michaels-repo}); the deficit at coarser steps is the reorganization of the minimizer within the scale $\text{gap}/\|Q_K'\|\approx3\times10^{-5}$, the ratio of the spectral gap $\lambda_1-\lambda_K$ to the norm of the entering term's derivative.
\end{computation}

For the exact form, under (H$_\infty$), Theorem~\ref{thm:edge} gives $f(a_n)=0$ and the proposition's jump is a quantity of the truncation. What replaces it follows from the fitted profile of Computation~\ref{comp:edge}: with $f(a-\delta)=C(\log(a/\delta)+\beta)^{-1/2}$, the product integral is
\begin{equation}\label{eq:soft}
-2\Lambda(n)n^{-1/2}g(\log n)=\frac{4\Lambda(n)C^2}{\sqrt n}\,\frac{\varepsilon}{\log(a/\varepsilon)+\beta+1-\log2}\,\Big(1+O\big(\log^{-2}(1/\varepsilon)\big)\Big),
\end{equation}
so $\lambda'$ is continuous across the entry and its increment at scale $\varepsilon$ is $4\Lambda(n)n^{-1/2}C^2/(\log(a/\varepsilon)+\beta+1-\log2)$: a soft kink, vanishing only logarithmically. (An earlier version omitted the constant $1-\log2$ in the denominator, which is an $8\%$ effect at $\varepsilon=10^{-3}$.)

\begin{computation}[the soft kink]\label{comp:soft}
On the edge FEM (134-node mesh, for which $C=0.0241$, $\beta=-1.93$) at the entry of $3$, with $D(\varepsilon)=[\lambda(a_3+\varepsilon)-2\lambda(a_3)+\lambda(a_3-\varepsilon)]/\varepsilon$, all entries $\times10^{-4}$:
\begin{center}
\begin{tabular}{@{}lccccc@{}}
\toprule
$\varepsilon$ & $10^{-3}$ & $10^{-4}$ & $10^{-5}$ & $10^{-6}$ & $10^{-7}$\\
\midrule
measured $D(\varepsilon)$ & $2.45$ & $1.90$ & $1.48$ & $1.21$ & $1.01$\\
first-order energy of the entering term on the $a_3$ minimizer & $3.42$ & $2.13$ & $1.59$ & $1.27$ & $1.06$\\
law \eqref{eq:soft} & $3.15$ & $2.11$ & $1.59$ & $1.27$ & $1.06$\\
\bottomrule
\end{tabular}
\end{center}
The law reproduces the first-order energy from $\varepsilon=10^{-4}$ down, as it must, since it is that quantity evaluated on the fitted profile; the measured increment sits $3$--$5\%$ below both, which is the second-order reorganization of the minimizer, and at $10^{-3}$ the smooth curvature $\lambda''\varepsilon\approx0.9\times10^{-4}$ is still inside $D$. The increment falls by about $22\%$ per decade, as the law does. The $24$-mode form gives the constant $2.28\times10^{-4}$.
\end{computation}

At the resolution of the grids of Section~\ref{sec:grid} (steps $0.004$ to $0.025$) the entries beyond $n=3$ leave no visible trace in $\lambda_K'$. The Hellmann--Feynman derivative resolves what those steps average over.

\begin{computation}[the relay resolved]\label{comp:relay}
\texttt{rh\_dilation.py} gives $\lambda_K'(a)=\ip{c}{Q_K'(a)c}$ at $a_n+\text{offset}$ for the first five entries ($K=48,48,64,80,96$ for $n=2,3,4,5,7$; ball arithmetic, central differences of the matrix at step $10^{-30}$). Entries are $\Phi'/\Tst$; data in \texttt{relay\_scan.csv}.
\begin{center}
\begin{tabular}{@{}rrrrrrrrr@{}}
\toprule
$n$ & $a_n$ & $-0.01$ & $-0.003$ & $-0.001$ & $-0.0001$ & $+0.0001$ & $+0.001$ & $+0.01$\\
\midrule
$2$ & $0.3466$ & $2.42$ & $2.96$ & $3.16$ & $3.27$ & $2.80$ & $2.59$ & $2.30$\\
$3$ & $0.5493$ & $2.31$ & $3.02$ & $3.65$ & $4.08$ & $3.41$ & $3.17$ & $3.08$\\
$4$ & $0.6931$ & $3.61$ & $3.32$ & $3.70$ & $4.08$ & $3.73$ & $3.63$ & $3.51$\\
$5$ & $0.8047$ & $3.96$ & $2.98$ & $3.32$ & $4.32$ & $3.63$ & $3.48$ & $3.51$\\
$7$ & $0.9730$ & $3.18$ & $3.66$ & $3.06$ & $4.12$ & $3.59$ & $3.54$ & $3.84$\\
\bottomrule
\end{tabular}
\end{center}
Over the last $0.003$ before each entry the ratio rises by $10\%,\,35\%,\,23\%,\,45\%,\,13\%$ ($n=2,3,4,5,7$), monotonically for $n=2,3$ and after a dip at $-0.003$ for $n=4,5,7$; across the entry, between offsets $\mp0.0001$, it drops by $14\%,\,16\%,\,9\%,\,16\%,\,13\%$, which at this scale is the $K$-mode kink of Proposition~\ref{prop:kink} and the soft kink \eqref{eq:soft} nearly coincide, since $1/K^2\approx10^{-4}$; then it relaxes. The peaks at the entries are $3.27,\,4.08,\,4.08,\,4.32,\,4.12$ and the dips between them reach $2.3$. The values are stable in $K$: at $a_3-0.001$, $\Phi'/\Tst=3.6404,\,3.6485,\,3.6505$ for $K=32,48,64$; at $a_5-0.003$, $3.001$ at $K=64$ against $2.98$ at $K=80$. The rise is the form without the entering prime power heading toward its failure point $a_{\mathrm{fail}}$, the deleted-form failure of \cite{michaels-folds} ($0.5574$ for $n=3$): $\lambda_K$ falls roughly linearly there ($\lambda_K'\approx-1.14\times10^{-3}$ at $a=0.549$, reaching zero at $0.562$ by linear extrapolation) while $\lambda_K'$ barely moves, so $\Phi'=-\lambda_K'/\lambda_K$ grows like $1/(a_{\mathrm{fail}}-a)$ until the entry rescues the floor. Each value near $a_3$ was checked against a finite difference of $\lambda_K$ with step $2\times10^{-4}$ to four digits. The coarse grids average this sawtooth into the plateau of Computation~\ref{comp:grid}.
\end{computation}

The relay is therefore the sawtooth described in \cite{michaels-folds}, measured on the $K$-mode form: not a kick at the entry but a rise into it, cut off by the entering prime power, whose first-order action at the entry is the soft kink.

\section{The sliding-window identity}\label{sec:identity}

For $f$ supported in $[-a,a]$ and $b>0$ let $f_b(x)=b^{-1/2}f(x/b)$, supported in $[-ab,ab]$ with $\|f_b\|=\|f\|$. Define, with $F=\hat f$ and $g=g_f$,
\[
D_\infty(f)=\frac1{2\pi}\int|F(t)|^2\,t\,\partial_t\Ree\psi\big(\tfrac14+\tfrac{it}2\big)\,dt,\qquad
D_P(f)=-\sum_{\log n<2a}\frac{2\Lambda(n)}{\sqrt n}\,\log n\;g'(\log n),
\]
$P(f)=\int f(x)\sinh(x/2)\,dx$, $M(f)=\int f(x)\,x\cosh(x/2)\,dx$, and
\begin{equation}\label{eq:Dtilde}
\mathcal D(f)=D_\infty(f)+D_P(f)+2P(f)^2+2P(f)M(f).
\end{equation}

\begin{lemma}[dilation derivative of the form]\label{lem:dil}
Let $a$ lie strictly between two consecutive entries and let $f$ be supported in $[-a,a]$ with $Q(f)<\infty$ and $g_f$ differentiable at every $\log n<2a$. Then $b\mapsto Q(f_b)$ is differentiable at $b=1$ and $\frac{d}{db}Q(f_b)\big|_{b=1}=-\mathcal D(f)$.
\end{lemma}
\begin{proof}
$\widehat{f_b}(t)=\sqrt b\,F(bt)$ exactly, jumps included. In \eqref{eq:Q}, $g_{f_b}(u)=g_f(u/b)$, so the prime terms are $-2\Lambda(n)n^{-1/2}g_f(\log n/b)$, differentiable at $b=1$ with derivative $2\Lambda(n)n^{-1/2}\log n\,g_f'(\log n)=$ the $n$-th term of $-D_P(f)$; for $b$ near $1$ no new prime enters. The archimedean term is $\frac1{2\pi}\int b|F(bt)|^2\Psi_\infty(t)\,dt=\frac1{2\pi}\int|F(s)|^2\Psi_\infty(s/b)\,ds$ with $\Psi_\infty=\Ree\psi(\tfrac14+\tfrac{it}2)-\log\pi$; since $t\Psi_\infty'(t)$ is bounded on $\R$, differentiation under the integral is justified by dominated convergence and gives $-\frac1{2\pi}\int|F|^2\,t\Psi_\infty'(t)\,dt=-D_\infty(f)$ at $b=1$. For the polar term, $P(f_b)=\sqrt b\int f(y)\sinh(by/2)\,dy$ gives $\frac{d}{db}P(f_b)|_{b=1}=\tfrac12P(f)+\tfrac12M(f)$, so $\frac{d}{db}(-2P(f_b)^2)|_{b=1}=-2P^2-2PM$.
\end{proof}

\begin{theorem}[sliding-window identity, $K$-mode form]\label{thm:identity}
Let $a$ lie strictly between two consecutive entries and let $\lambda_K(a)$ be simple with normalized eigenvector $c$, $f$ the corresponding function. Then $\lambda_K$ is differentiable at $a$ and
\begin{equation}\label{eq:identity}
a\,\lambda_K'(a)=-\mathcal D(f).
\end{equation}
\end{theorem}
\begin{proof}
The basis functions are a dilation family, $\phi_i^{(ab)}=(\phi_i^{(a)})_b$, so $Q_K(ab)=[Q((\phi_i^{(a)})_b,(\phi_j^{(a)})_b)]_{ij}$ is real-analytic in $b$ near $1$ (each entry is a finite sum of terms as in Lemma~\ref{lem:dil}, with $g_{ij}$ polynomial), Hellmann--Feynman for a simple eigenvalue gives $\frac{d}{db}\lambda_K(ab)|_{b=1}=\ip{c}{\frac{d}{db}Q_K(ab)|_{b=1}c}=\frac{d}{db}Q(f_b)|_{b=1}$ by bilinearity, and Lemma~\ref{lem:dil} evaluates it.
\end{proof}

\begin{proposition}[exact form: two-sided Dini derivatives]\label{prop:dini}
Let $a$ lie strictly between two consecutive entries and let $f$ be a minimizer at support $a$ (Lemma~\ref{lem:exist}) with $g_f$ differentiable at every $\log n<2a$. Then
\[
a\,\Dp\lambda(a)\;\le\;-\mathcal D(f)\;\le\;a\,\Dm\lambda(a),
\]
where $\Dp,\Dm$ are the upper-right and lower-left Dini derivatives; hence $a\lambda'(a)=-\mathcal D(f)$ at every point of differentiability of $\lambda$, which by Lemma~\ref{lem:exist} is almost every $a$.
\end{proposition}
\begin{proof}
For every $b>0$, $f_b$ is admissible at support $ab$, so $\lambda(ab)\le Q(f_b)$, with equality at $b=1$. By Lemma~\ref{lem:dil}, $Q(f_b)=\lambda(a)-(b-1)\mathcal D(f)+o(b-1)$. For $b>1$, $(\lambda(ab)-\lambda(a))/(b-1)\le-\mathcal D(f)+o(1)$; for $b<1$ the division reverses the inequality. Since $\frac{d}{db}=a\frac{d}{da}$ at $b=1$, the two bounds follow.
\end{proof}

\begin{remark}
The hypothesis on $g_f'$ is implied by $f'\in L^1$, which the edge law $C(\log(1/\delta))^{-1/2}$ suggests but which is not proved; it is the only condition beyond existence. The form-domain condition $\int\log(2+|t|)|F|^2<\infty$ does not give $\int|t||F|^2<\infty$, so the prime part of $\mathcal D$ is to be read in $x$-space, as defined.
\end{remark}

The hypothesis is natural in the class of functions of bounded variation, and in that class the dilation derivative is bounded, which makes the floor Lipschitz.

\begin{lemma}[dilation of a function of bounded variation]\label{lem:bv}
Let $f\in L^\infty(\R)$ have bounded variation $\operatorname{Var}f$ and support in $[-a,a]$. Then $g_f$ is Lipschitz with constant $\|f\|_\infty\operatorname{Var}f$; the map $b\mapsto Q(f_b)$ is locally Lipschitz on $(0,\infty)$, entries included, with $b\,\frac{d}{db}Q(f_b)=-\mathcal D(f_b)$ at every $b$ at which $g_f$ is differentiable at the points $(\log n)/b$; and for every $b$
\[
|\mathcal D(f_b)|\;\le\;w_*\|f\|^2+\frac{\|f\|_\infty\operatorname{Var}f}{b}\sum_{\log n<2ab}\frac{2\Lambda(n)\log n}{\sqrt n}+\big(2\sigma(ab)^2+2\sigma(ab)\mu(ab)\big)\|f\|^2,
\]
where $w_*=\sup_t t\,\partial_t\Ree\psi(\tfrac14+\tfrac{it}2)=2.0807$, attained at $t=0.520$, $\sigma(a)^2=\int_{-a}^a\sinh^2(x/2)\,dx$ and $\mu(a)^2=\int_{-a}^ax^2\cosh^2(x/2)\,dx$. If $f$ is continuous, $g_f\in C^1$ and $b\mapsto Q(f_b)$ is $C^1$.
\end{lemma}
\begin{proof}
For $f$ of bounded variation, $\int|f(y)-f(y-h)|\,dy\le|h|\operatorname{Var}f$, so $|g_f(u)-g_f(u')|\le\|f\|_\infty\int|f(x-u)-f(x-u')|\,dx\le\|f\|_\infty\operatorname{Var}f\,|u-u'|$. The prime terms of $Q(f_b)$ are $-2\Lambda(n)n^{-1/2}g_f(\log n/b)$, locally Lipschitz in $b$, with $b\frac{d}{db}g_f(\log n/b)=-(\log n/b)\,g_f'(\log n/b)=-\log n\;g_{f_b}'(\log n)$ wherever $g_f$ is differentiable at $\log n/b$; a term with $\log n=2ab$ enters with the value $g_f(2a)=0$ and continuously, since $g_f$ vanishes on $[2a,\infty)$. The archimedean and polar terms are $C^1$ in $b$ for every $b$, with the derivatives computed in Lemma~\ref{lem:dil}. The bound uses $|t\,\partial_t\Ree\psi|\le w_*$, $|g_{f_b}'|\le\|f_b\|_\infty\operatorname{Var}f_b=b^{-1}\|f\|_\infty\operatorname{Var}f$, and $|P(f_b)|\le\sigma(ab)\|f\|$, $|M(f_b)|\le\mu(ab)\|f\|$ by Cauchy--Schwarz. If $f$ is continuous, $g_f'(u)=-\int f(z+u)\,d(Df)(z)$ with $Df$ the finite derivative measure of $f$, which is continuous in $u$ by dominated convergence.
\end{proof}

\begin{proposition}[the floor is Lipschitz under a variation bound]\label{prop:lip}
Let $0<a_0<a_1$ and $M>0$, and assume
\begin{itemize}
\item[(H$_{\mathrm{BV}}$)] for every $a\in[a_0,a_1]$ and $\varepsilon>0$ there is $f\in L^\infty$ of bounded variation, supported in $[-a,a]$, with $\|f\|=1$, $Q(f)\le\lambda(a)+\varepsilon$ and $\|f\|_\infty\operatorname{Var}f\le M$.
\end{itemize}
Then for $a_0\le a<a'\le a_1$,
\[
0\;\le\;\lambda(a)-\lambda(a')\;\le\;L\log\frac{a'}{a}\;\le\;\frac{L}{a_0}\,(a'-a),\qquad
L=w_*+\frac{a_1}{a_0}\,M\sum_{\log n<2a_1}\frac{2\Lambda(n)\log n}{\sqrt n}+2\sigma(a_1)^2+2\sigma(a_1)\mu(a_1).
\]
In particular $\lambda$ is Lipschitz on $[a_0,a_1]$, hence absolutely continuous there, with $\lambda'\ge-L/a_0$ almost everywhere.
\end{proposition}
\begin{proof}
The left inequality is Lemma~\ref{lem:exist}. For the right one take $f'$ as in (H$_{\mathrm{BV}}$) at $a'$ and put $b_0=a/a'$. The dilate $f'_{b_0}$ is supported in $[-a,a]$ with unit norm, so $\lambda(a)\le Q(f'_{b_0})$. By Lemma~\ref{lem:bv}, $b\mapsto Q(f'_b)$ is Lipschitz on $[b_0,1]$ with $|b\frac{d}{db}Q(f'_b)|\le L$ almost everywhere (the prime sum runs over $\log n<2a'b\le2a_1$, and $b^{-1}\le a_1/a_0$), so $Q(f'_{b_0})\le Q(f')+L\log(1/b_0)\le\lambda(a')+\varepsilon+L\log(a'/a)$. Let $\varepsilon\to0$.
\end{proof}

\begin{remark}\label{rem:bv}
(H$_{\mathrm{BV}}$) concerns near-minimizers, not the minimizer, and it follows from a bound on the $K$-mode minimizers uniform in $K$: $\lambda(a)=\inf_K\lambda_K(a)$ by Lemma~\ref{lem:exist} (the odd Legendre truncations of an odd $C_c^\infty(-a,a)$ function converge to it in the form norm, their jumps at $\pm a$ being of vanishing size and costing $O(\mathrm{jump}^2)$ in a norm that tolerates jumps), so the $K$-mode minimizers are near-minimizers of the exact problem, and they are polynomials, hence of bounded variation. Under (H$_{\mathrm{BV}}$) on every compact subinterval of $[a_0,\infty)$ the regularity clause of Conjecture~\ref{conj:A} holds and the conjecture is the inequality $\lambda'\ge-c\Tst\lambda$ a.e.\ alone. The trivial bound $\lambda'\ge-L/a$ also shows what the conjecture asks: on $[0.6,1]$ with $M=9$ (Computation~\ref{comp:bv}) it gives $L=126$ and $\lambda'\ge-211$, while $\lambda'$ is $-4\times10^{-5}$ at $a=0.6$ and $-3\times10^{-24}$ at $a=1$; the conjecture is that $\mathcal D(f)$, whose three terms are each of order one, cancels to $O(\Tst\lambda)$ on the minimizer, as in \eqref{eq:cancel}.
\end{remark}

($^{\dagger}$: $K=64$.) At $a=1.0$ ($600$ bits): $\sup|f_K|=1.392228,\,1.399706,\,1.399722,\,1.399716,\,1.399714$ and $\operatorname{Var}f_K=5.568912,\,5.598825,\,5.598888,\,5.598865,\,5.598858$ for $K=24,48,80,120,160$. In every case $\operatorname{Var}f_K/(4\sup|f_K|)=1.0000$, and $\|f_K\|_\infty\operatorname{Var}f_K$ converges, to $8.55$, $8.09$, $7.84$ at $a=0.6,0.8,1.0$: (H$_{\mathrm{BV}}$) holds on these supports with $M=9$, in the form of Remark~\ref{rem:bv}.
\end{computation}

Since $t\,\partial_t\Ree\psi(\tfrac14+\tfrac{it}2)\to1$, $D_\infty(f)=\|f\|^2+O(\int|F|^2t^{-2})$; on a minimizer $\|f\|=1$ while $\lambda$ is $10^{-90}$ at $a=1.5$, so \eqref{eq:identity} forces
\begin{equation}\label{eq:cancel}
D_P(f)=-D_\infty(f)-2P^2-2PM+O(a\Tst\lambda):
\end{equation}
the Euler product, differentiated and applied to the derivative of the minimizer's autocorrelation at the prime-power positions, cancels the archimedean dilation form to ninety digits. Together with the explicit formula on the same function, $\sum 2\Lambda(n)n^{-1/2}g(\log n)=W_\infty(g)-2P^2-\lambda$, both the values and the log-weighted derivatives of $g_f$ at the prime powers are pinned by the archimedean side.

\begin{computation}[the identity on the $K$-mode minimizer]\label{comp:identity}
\texttt{rh\_dilation.py} differentiates the archimedean matrix $A$, the prime matrices $\Pi_n$ and the polar matrix $ss^{\mathsf T}$ of $Q_K(a)$ by central differences with step $10^{-30}$ in ball arithmetic and contracts them with the eigenvector; every entry is a ball with the radius shown, and the last column is the sum, which is $a\lambda_K'$ by Theorem~\ref{thm:identity}.
\begin{center}
\begin{tabular}{@{}rrcrrrcc@{}}
\toprule
$a$ & $K$ & $\lambda_K$ & $a\ip{c}{A'c}$ & $a\ip{c}{\Pi'c}$ & $-2a\ip{c}{(ss^{\mathsf T})'c}$ & sum $=a\lambda_K'$ & radius\\
\midrule
$0.6$ & $48$ & $5.9659\times10^{-7}$ & $-1.014313$ & $+1.086974$ & $-0.072687$ & $-2.493408\times10^{-5}$ & $4\times10^{-20}$\\
$0.8$ & $64$ & $1.5659\times10^{-14}$ & $-1.016605$ & $+1.103966$ & $-0.087361$ & $-1.295854\times10^{-12}$ & $4\times10^{-27}$\\
$1.0$ & $80$ & $1.4820\times10^{-26}$ & $-1.018057$ & $+1.114966$ & $-0.096909$ & $-2.516852\times10^{-24}$ & $3\times10^{-39}$\\
$1.25$ & $140$ & $3.4161\times10^{-51}$ & $-1.019178$ & $+1.123585$ & $-0.104407$ & $-1.233712\times10^{-48}$ & $2\times10^{-63}$\\
\bottomrule
\end{tabular}
\end{center}
The three columns are $-D_\infty$, $-D_P$ and $-(2P^2+2PM)$ evaluated through the matrices; that their sum is $a\lambda_K'$ is Theorem~\ref{thm:identity}, not an independent check. The independent check is on the Fourier side: at $K=12$, $a=0.6$ (\texttt{rh\_fourier\_check.py}), with the same coefficient vector, $D_\infty$ evaluated as $(1/2\pi)\int|F|^2\,t\,\partial_t\Re\psi(\tfrac14+\tfrac{it}2)\,dt$ with $F$ in closed form through spherical Bessel functions and the Parseval tail, $D_P$ from $g'(\log2)$ and $g'(\log3)$ of the exact autocorrelation, and $P$, $M$ by quadrature, gives $D_\infty=1.01430470102641$, $D_P=-1.08691690953774$, $2P^2+2PM=0.0726373685293583$ and $-\mathcal D(f)=-2.51600180293\times10^{-5}$, against $a\ip{c}{Q_K'c}=-2.51600180295\times10^{-5}$ from the matrices: twelve digits. (The eigenvector enters through one step of inverse iteration from a shift at relative distance $2^{-20}$ below $\lambda_K$, which fixes $a\ip{c}{Q_K'c}$ to about seven digits: at $K=12$ a second step moves it by $1\times10^{-7}$ relatively, to $-2.51600203711\times10^{-5}$, which is the central difference of $\lambda_K$ itself to twelve digits. The ball radii in the table bound the arithmetic only.) The archimedean dilation form sits at $1.014$--$1.019$, the excess over $\|f\|^2$ being the finite-$t$ correction; the prime dilation form grows with the primes present and cancels it; the polar term grows from $7\%$ to $10\%$. The implied $\Phi'/\Tst$ are $3.339,\,3.324,\,3.658,\,3.774$; at $a=0.8$ this lies inside the sawtooth of the entry of $5$ (Computation~\ref{comp:relay}).
\end{computation}

\subsection{The derivative is a property of the ground state}\label{sec:pencil}

Let $Q_K'(a)$ be the $a$-derivative of the $K$-mode matrix, a symmetric form on the $K$-mode space. Is there an inequality $-Q_K'\le c\,\Tst\,Q_K$ between the two forms, which would give the derivative bound for every $f$ at once? Since a form inequality on the exact form would restrict to every $K$-mode subspace, the pencil $(-Q_K',Q_K)$ answers this for the exact form too.

\begin{computation}[the pencil]\label{comp:pencil}
Largest and smallest eigenvalues of $Q_K^{-1}(-Q_K')$ divided by $\Tst$, computed from the midpoint matrices with an approximate QR eigenvalue routine at $500$ bits ($Q_K$ has condition number up to $10^{26}$ here, leaving over a hundred digits):
\begin{center}
\begin{tabular}{@{}lcccc@{}}
\toprule
$a$ & minimizer direction & largest & second, third & smallest\\
\midrule
$0.6$ & $3.34$ & $74.7$ & $4.26,\ 3.69$ & $-70.5$\\
$0.8$ & $3.32$ & $1.46\times10^{5}$ & $332,\ 6.08$ & $-1.46\times10^{5}$\\
$1.0$ & $3.66$ & $6.2\times10^{10}$ & $3.1\times10^7,\ 8.2\times10^4$ & $-6.2\times10^{10}$\\
\bottomrule
\end{tabular}
\end{center}
\end{computation}

The extreme eigenvalues come in $\pm$ pairs and grow explosively. They are near-null directions of $Q_K$ whose dilation derivative is enormous relative to their value; heuristically they are the directions along which the minimizer rotates as $a$ moves, and their size is of the order of the inverse of the scale $\text{gap}/\|Q_K'\|$ of Computation~\ref{comp:kink}. The ground state is neither extreme; it is a special interior direction in which the dilation derivative is anomalously small, by ten orders at $a=1$. Hence the derivative bound of Section~\ref{sec:conj} is a statement about the ground-state eigenvector alone, $\lambda'=\ip{f}{Q'f}$, and cannot be an inequality between forms. A proof must pass through the Euler--Lagrange equation \eqref{eq:EL} of the minimizer.

\section{The floor to height 130}\label{sec:grid}

\begin{computation}[the band]\label{comp:grid}
Three data sets (\texttt{floor\_grid\_K40.csv}, \texttt{floor\_grid\_K72.csv}, \texttt{floor\_grid\_arb.csv}): $K=40$, step $0.0125$ on $[0.30,1.20]$ with the entries $a_n\pm0.004$ added; $K=72$, step $0.025$ on $[0.475,1.05]$; and the ball-arithmetic engine at pairs $a\pm0.005$ around the centres $1.06,1.10,1.15,1.25,1.33,1.40,1.45,1.50$ with $K=110,110,110,140,140,180,180,180$, plus $K=180$ at $1.25$ and $K=230$ at $1.5$. $\Phi'$ is a centered difference over the nearest admissible pair ($\pm0.025$ for $K=72$, $\pm0.005$ for arb), never across an entry; the pair at $1.10$ straddles $a_9=1.0986$ and is excluded. Every value is a $K$-mode upper bound on the floor. Convergence: $\lambda_{40}$ agrees with $\lambda_{72}$ to $5\%$ up to $a=0.925$ and $26\%$ at $1.0$; $\lambda_{140}$ with $\lambda_{180}$ to $2\%$ at $1.25$; at $1.5$, $\lambda_{180}$ and $\lambda_{230}$ differ by $39\%$ and the ratio by $4\%$, more modes raising it, so the last ratio is a lower bound. For $a>1$ each eigenvalue carries a rigorous enclosure of the assembled matrix's lowest eigenvalue: radius $2\times10^{-64}$ for the four points computed before the midpoint reset of Section~\ref{sec:engines}, $\approx10^{-143}$ at $500$ bits after it, and $\approx10^{-233}$ at $800$ bits; the enclosures do not cover the polynomial evaluations or the outer rule.

\midrule

For the $K=72$ rows $\lambda_K$ is the grid value at the centre; for the arb rows it is the geometric mean of the pair. Means of $\Phi'/\Tst$: $3.23$ on $[0.5,0.7]$ ($5$ grid points), $3.61$ on $[0.85,1.05]$ ($5$), $3.79$ on $[1.06,1.25]$ ($3$), $3.85$ on $[1.33,1.5]$ ($4$). One-parameter least-squares fits of $\Phi'/\Tst$ against $a$ on the nine tabulated rows with $a\ge0.85$, residual $=$ rms of the relative deviations: $\Phi'=c\Tst$ with $c=3.79$, $2.1\%$; $\Phi'=c\Tst\log\Tst$, $7.5\%$; the raw count $\Phi\propto N(\Tst)$, whose ratio is $\propto 4a-1$, $18.4\%$; the derivative of Zhu's even-sector law $\Phi\simeq C\,N(\Tst)/\log N(\Tst)$, whose ratio is $\propto 2a(L-1)/L^2$ with $L=\log N(\Tst)=2a+\log(2a-1)$ and tends to $C/\pi$, $8.1\%$. With Zhu's own $C=2\pi^2$ that derivative is $3.14$ at $a=1$ and $3.72$ at $a=1.5$, a $42\%$ rise against the observed $7\%$. The ratio $\lambda_1/\lambda_K$ stays above $5.7$ and grows overall to $10^6$ at $a=1$ (with local decreases of up to $18\%$), so the $K$-mode ground state is simple throughout.
\end{computation}

At step $0.004$ the entries beyond $n=3$ show no net drop in $\Phi'$, because the difference quotient across an entry averages the steep rise before it against the drop at it; Computation~\ref{comp:relay} resolves both. Over the fine scan, $\Phi'/\Tst$ lies in $[2.3,\,4.3]$, and the supremum over all measured points, the quantity Conjecture~\ref{conj:A} bounds, is $4.32$, at the entry of $5$.

\section{The conjectures}\label{sec:conj}

\begin{conjecture}[bounded relay]\label{conj:A}
There are $a_0>0$ and $c>0$ such that $\lambda$ is locally absolutely continuous on $[a_0,\infty)$ and
\[
\lambda'(a)\;\ge\;-c\,\Tst(a)\,\lambda(a)\qquad\text{for almost every }a\ge a_0 .
\]
\end{conjecture}

The statement is meaningful whatever the sign of $\lambda$; absolute continuity is part of the conjecture: Lemma~\ref{lem:exist} gives continuity and differentiability a.e., and Proposition~\ref{prop:lip} gives Lipschitz continuity under (H$_{\mathrm{BV}}$), a bound on the sup norm times the total variation of near-minimizers, which Computation~\ref{comp:bv} supports on every minimizer computed ($\operatorname{Var}f=4\sup|f|\approx6$, converged in the mesh and in $K$). Where $\lambda>0$ it reads $\Phi'\le c\,\Tst$.

\begin{proposition}\label{prop:AimpliesRH}
Conjecture~\ref{conj:A} with $a_0\le0.8$ implies the Riemann Hypothesis.
\end{proposition}
\begin{proof}
Put $u(a)=\lambda(a)\exp\big(\tfrac c2\Tst(a)\big)$, which is locally absolutely continuous with $u'=e^{c\Tst/2}(\lambda'+c\Tst\lambda)\ge0$ a.e., using $\frac{d}{da}\Tst=2\Tst$. Hence $u$ is non-decreasing and $\lambda(a)\ge\lambda(a_0)\exp\big(-\tfrac c2(\Tst(a)-\Tst(a_0))\big)$ for $a\ge a_0$. With $a_0=0.8$, Theorem~\ref{thm:zhu} gives $\lambda(0.8)\ge8.206\times10^{-15}>0$, so $\lambda>0$ on $[0.8,\infty)$, and $\lambda>0$ on $(0,0.8]$ by the same theorem. Theorem~\ref{thm:weil} concludes.
\end{proof}

\begin{conjecture}[decay law]\label{conj:B}
For $a\ge a_0$, $\lambda(a)>0$ and $\Phi'(a)/\Tst(a)$ is bounded above and below by positive constants. In its sharp form, $\Phi'(a)/\Tst(a)\to c_0$, i.e.\ $\lambda(a)=\exp\big(-(c_0/2+o(1))\,2\pi e^{2a}\big)$.
\end{conjecture}

Computation~\ref{comp:grid} supports the weak form from height $17$ to $130$ and, on the odd sector, favours a constant $c_0\approx3.8$ (residual $2.1\%$) over every law with a logarithmic factor; the derivative of Zhu's even-sector law, whose constant $C=2\pi^2$ would give the asymptote $2\pi$, predicts a rise six times larger than observed. Whether the ratio saturates or continues to creep up beyond height $130$ is not decidable from these data. Conjecture~\ref{conj:A} is the one that implies the Riemann Hypothesis; the constant is not its content.

By Theorem~\ref{thm:identity} and Proposition~\ref{prop:dini}, Conjecture~\ref{conj:A} is, at almost every $a$, the inequality
\begin{equation}\label{eq:Aform}
\mathcal D(f)\;\le\;c\,a\,\Tst(a)\,Q(f)\qquad\text{on the minimizer } f,
\end{equation}
and by Computation~\ref{comp:pencil} it holds only there.

\begin{remark}[what a proof cannot be]
Positivity of $Q_{24}$ on a window is destroyed by displacing $\log2$ by $3\times10^{-14}$ at $a=0.8$ and by displacements of order $\exp(-c\Tst(a))$ in general \cite{michaels-folds,michaels-repo}, so no statement about the primes that is invariant under such displacements can imply positivity; and Checkpoint 20 of \cite{michaels-atlas} shows that bounding the reflected prime terms separately from the polar term yields $-\infty$ on translated tests. A proof of Conjecture~\ref{conj:A} must therefore use the exact multiplicative structure of $\Lambda$, and by Computation~\ref{comp:pencil} it must use that $f$ minimizes $Q$.
\end{remark}

\subsection{Under the Riemann Hypothesis}\label{sec:rh}

Let $\PW_a$ be the Paley--Wiener space of transforms $F=\hat f$ of $f\in L^2(-a,a)$, here with $f$ odd. If all zeros are real, $Q(f)=\sum_\gamma|F(\gamma)|^2$ (the sum over $\gamma$ with multiplicity, of both signs; it converges when $Q(f)<\infty$), and the dilation computation of Lemma~\ref{lem:dil} on the zero side, $Q(f_b)=\sum_\gamma b|F(b\gamma)|^2$, gives at a point of differentiability
\[
a\lambda'=\lambda+\sum_\gamma\gamma\,\partial_t|F|^2(\gamma)
\]
whenever the last sum converges absolutely, so Conjecture~\ref{conj:A} reads $\sum_\gamma\gamma\,\partial_t|F|^2(\gamma)\ge-(1+ca\Tst)\sum_\gamma|F(\gamma)|^2$: the minimizer is small at the zeros to the same order in its derivative as in its value. And
\[
\lambda(a)=\inf\{\textstyle\sum_\gamma|F(\gamma)|^2:\ F\in\PW_a\ \text{odd},\ \|f\|_2=1\}
\]
is the lower sampling constant of the zeros for $\PW_a$. The zeros have density $(2\pi)^{-1}\log(t/2\pi)$, which exceeds $a/\pi$ exactly above $\Tst(a)$, so only the zeros above the horizon can sample $\PW_a$, and the floor measures how well a band-limited function can hide from them.

Both statements can be evaluated on the zeros themselves. The explicit formula is unconditional, and every zero used below lies on the line, so for the $K$-mode minimizer the sum over the zeros of $|F_K(\gamma)|^2$ must return $Q(f_K)=\lambda_K$ up to the contribution of the zeros above the last one used; this closes the loop on the object the form was built from.

\begin{computation}[the floor and the identity on the zeros]\label{comp:zeros}
\texttt{rh\_zero\_side.py}, with the first $6000$ zeros ($\gamma\le6365.85$; \texttt{data/zeros\_6000.txt}, from \texttt{mpmath.zetazero}, the first $400$ to $36$ digits and the rest to $21$). For the $K$-mode minimizers of Computation~\ref{comp:identity}, $F_K$ is evaluated in closed form through spherical Bessel functions at $35$ digits; the sums run over $\gamma>0$ with the factor $2$; the tail above $\gamma_{6000}$ is the Riemann--von Mangoldt density integrated against $|F_K|^2$, with $|F_K(t)|^2$ replaced by its mean $2f_K(a)^2/t^2$ beyond $\gamma_{6000}+60$, and the derivative tail is the corresponding $-2\times$ that; $a\lambda_K'$ is the matrix-side value of Computation~\ref{comp:identity}, the sums run over $\gamma>0$.
\begin{center}\footnotesize
\begin{tabular}{@{}cccccccc@{}}
\toprule
$a$ & $K$ & $\lambda_K$ & $2\sum|F_K(\gamma)|^2$+tail & ratio & $a\lambda_K'$ & $\lambda_K+2\sum\gamma\,\partial_t|F_K|^2$+tail & ratio\\
\midrule
$0.6$ & $48$ & $5.96594\times10^{-7}$ & $5.96554\times10^{-7}$ & $0.99993$ & $-2.49341\times10^{-5}$ & $-2.49310\times10^{-5}$ & $0.99988$\\
$0.8$ & $64$ & $1.56587\times10^{-14}$ & $1.56568\times10^{-14}$ & $0.99988$ & $-1.29585\times10^{-12}$ & $-1.29685\times10^{-12}$ & $1.00077$\\
$1.0$ & $80$ & $1.48201\times10^{-26}$ & $1.48157\times10^{-26}$ & $0.99971$ & $-2.51685\times10^{-24}$ & $-2.52433\times10^{-24}$ & $1.0030$\\
\bottomrule
\end{tabular}
\end{center}
At $a=0.6$ the value ratio with $N=10,\,30,\,100,\,300,\,1000,\,3000,\,6000$ zeros (tail included) is $0.977,\,0.952,\,0.973,\,0.989,\,0.9974,\,0.99967,\,0.99993$; the residual is the fluctuation of the zeros about their mean density, of the size of the tail. The derivative sum is first-order sensitive to the positions of the zeros, by $4\gamma|F_K'(\gamma)|^2\varepsilon$ per zero for an error $\varepsilon$ in $\gamma$, while the value sum is not; with all zeros at $21$ digits the derivative sum at $a=1$ came out $+7\times10^{-22}$ against $-2.5\times10^{-24}$, stable from $N=30$ on, and the $36$-digit zeros cure it. The residuals of the derivative sums, $1.2\times10^{-4}$, $8\times10^{-4}$, $3\times10^{-3}$, grow with $a$ as this sensitivity does (the zeros beyond the $400$th are still at $21$ digits); the value sums are insensitive, and their residuals are the fluctuation of the zeros about their mean density in the tail. The explicit formula, which built the form from the primes and the archimedean symbol, returns on the actual zeros both the floor and its rate of change.
\end{computation}

Where the floor sits among the zeros is the second reading of the same computation. At $a=0.6$ ($\Tst=20.9$) one zero lies below the horizon and carries $1.0\times10^{-5}$ of $2\sum|F_K(\gamma)|^2$; at $a=0.8$ ($\Tst=31.1$) four zeros lie below and carry $1.0\times10^{-7}$; at $a=1$ ($\Tst=46.4$) eight lie below and carry $1.6\times10^{-13}$, the first zero alone $9\times10^{-25}$. The largest single terms are at $\gamma_7=40.9$ ($15\%$ of the floor), $\gamma_{13}=59.3$ ($19\%$) and $\gamma_{27}=94.7$ ($8\%$), at $2.0$, $1.9$ and $2.0$ horizons, and at each support the mass sits between $1.5$ and $3$ horizons. So the minimizer hides from the zeros below the horizon to an accuracy far beyond the floor itself, and the floor is made by the zeros between the horizon and about three times the horizon, as the sampling picture says. This is the shadow in its most literal form: $2\sum_{\gamma>0}|F_K(\gamma)|^2=\lambda_K$ says that the worst test function is small at every zero, to $10^{-13}$ at $a=1$, and the derivative column says that it is small there in its dilation derivative too.

The distribution has a law. Write $\vartheta(T)$ for the fraction of $2\sum_{\gamma>0}|F(\gamma)|^2$ carried by the zeros above height $T$.

\begin{computation}[the tail law]\label{comp:tail}
\texttt{rh\_tail\_law.py}, same zeros. The FEM floor is recovered from the zeros: $2\sum_{\gamma\le\gamma_{6000}}|F(\gamma)|^2/\lambda_{\mathrm{FEM}}$ plus the tail $\Phi'/(\pi\gamma_{6000})$ is $0.999995$, $0.999997$, $0.999999$ at $a=0.4,0.45,0.5$, a check of the FEM engine on the zeros independent of everything else. The law is a property of the transform sampled by the density of zeros: at $a=0.5$ the density integral $2\int_T^\infty|F|^2\rho$, $\rho(t)=\log(t/2\pi)/2\pi$, is $1.068,\,1.060,\,1.032,\,1.020,\,1.012,\,1.006$ times $-\lambda'/(\pi T)$ at $T/\Tst=5,7,10,20,50,100$, the $O(1/\log T)$ correction of Proposition~\ref{prop:tail} below, while the zero sum is $1.004,\,1.020,\,0.981,\,1.000,\,1.002,\,1.000$ times it. $0.6$ & $48$ & $1.063$ & $0.594$ & $0.481$ & $0.370$ & $0.215$ & $0.1065$ & $1.045$ & $1.011$ & $1.002$ & $2.39$\\
$0.8$ & $64$ & $1.058$ & $0.596$ & $0.480$ & $0.415$ & $0.226$ & $0.1009$ & $1.176$ & $1.067$ & $0.953$ & $2.32$\\
$1.0$ & $80$ & $1.164$ & $0.810$ & $0.526$ & $0.407$ & $0.240$ & $0.1170$ & $1.048$ & $1.032$ & $1.005$ & $2.65$\\
$1.25$ & $140$ & $1.201$ & $0.781$ & $0.528$ & $0.433$ & $0.249$ & $0.1210$ & $1.082$ & $1.038$ & $1.007$ & $2.67$\\
$1.5$ & $230$ & $1.239$ & $0.858$ & $0.554$ & $0.434$ & $0.251$ & $0.1230$ & $1.052$ & $1.015$ & $0.993$ & $2.74$\\
