\end{heuristic}

\section{The boundary law}\label{sec:hadamard}

The derivative of the floor should be a boundary quantity. Since $Q$ does not depend on $a$, $\lambda(a)$ is the infimum of a fixed form over the growing family $L^2(-a,a)$, and its derivative is a domain variation. For the Dirichlet fractional Laplacian, Hadamard's formula expresses such a derivative through the boundary trace $u/\mathrm{dist}^s$ of the eigenfunction \cite{djitte-fall-weth}; for the logarithmic Laplacian the boundary trace is the amplitude $C$ of Computation~\ref{comp:edge}. A Pohozaev identity for $L_\Delta$ with the local boundary term $\int_{\partial\Omega}u^2\ln(\delta^{-2})\,(x\cdot\nu)$ was claimed in \cite{agv}, which its authors withdrew on 26 April 2025 ``due to a crucial error in Proof of Pohozaev identity''; to our knowledge no correct version exists. We therefore state the law as a conjecture and give the evidence.

\begin{conjecture}[boundary law]\label{conj:hadamard}
Assume (H$_\infty$). For $a$ not an entry, with $C_\pm(a)=\lim_{\delta\to0}\sqrt{\log(1/\delta)}\,|f(\pm(a-\delta))|$ the edge amplitudes of the minimizer (assumed to exist),
\[
\lambda'(a)=-\big(C_+(a)^2+C_-(a)^2\big)=-2\,C(a)^2 .
\]
Equivalently, with Proposition~\ref{prop:dini}, the Weil form on a window satisfies the Pohozaev-type identity
$\mathcal D(f)=2a\,C(a)^2$ on its minimizer. Proposition~\ref{prop:flux} below proves it under the edge law with two derivatives, and Propositions~\ref{prop:profile} and~\ref{prop:whfull} supply that law from (H$_\infty$) for the archimedean form and for $a_2<a<a_3$; Computation~\ref{comp:hadamard} is the evidence at the other supports.
\end{conjecture}

The law is a theorem under the edge law with derivatives, the hypothesis of Proposition~\ref{prop:tail}: the boundary term of the Pohozaev identity is the overlap, at the scale $1/T$, of the leak of the dilation generator through the low-pass with the singular force outside the edge, and the logarithms cancel there as they did in Lemma~\ref{lem:overlap}. We first sharpen Lemma~\ref{lem:cont}.

\begin{lemma}[the edge force to second order]\label{lem:force}
Under the hypothesis of Proposition~\ref{prop:tail}, the remainder $\rho(\delta)=g_{\mathrm{out}}(a+\delta)+CL(\delta)^{1/2}-g_{\mathrm{reg}}(a)$ of Lemma~\ref{lem:cont} satisfies $\rho(\delta)=o(L(\delta)^{-1/2})$ and $\rho'(\delta)=o\big(\delta^{-1}L(\delta)^{-3/2}\big)$ as $\delta\to0^+$.
\end{lemma}
\begin{proof}
By the formula in the proof of Lemma~\ref{lem:cont}, $g_{\mathrm{out}}(a+\delta)=-\int_0^{2a}f(a-u)k(u+\delta)\,du+(K_Pf)(a+\delta)$, and the prime part is smooth near the edge (it is made of copies of the interior of $f$). Write $k(x)=\frac1{2x}+k_0(x)$ with $k_0$ smooth and bounded; the part with $k_0$ is $C^1$ in $\delta$. In the singular part put $f(a-u)=(\varphi+\psi)(u)$ for $u<\delta_0$ and substitute $u=\delta w$:
\[
-\frac12\int_0^{\delta_0}\frac{\varphi(u)}{u+\delta}\,du=-\frac C2\int_0^{\delta_0/\delta}\frac{(L(\delta)-\log w)^{-1/2}}{1+w}\,dw ,
\]
and $\int_0^{W}\frac{(L-\log w)^{-1/2}}{1+w}dw=\int_1^{W}\frac{(L-\log w)^{-1/2}}w\,dw+\int_0^\infty\Big[\frac1{1+w}-\frac{\mathbf 1_{w>1}}w\Big](L-\log w)^{-1/2}dw+O(L^{-1/2}/W)$. The first integral is $2L^{1/2}-2L(\delta_0)^{1/2}$; in the second the bracket integrates to zero, so the expansion $(L-\log w)^{-1/2}=L^{-1/2}\big(1+\frac{\log w}{2L}+O(L^{-2}\log^2w)\big)$ leaves $O(L^{-3/2})$. Hence the $\varphi$-part is $-CL(\delta)^{1/2}+\mathrm{const}+O(L^{-3/2})$, and the $\psi$-part, $-\frac12\int\psi(u)\,du/(u+\delta)$, is $o\big(\int_\delta^{\delta_0}L(u)^{-3/2}du/u\big)+o(L^{-3/2})=o(L(\delta)^{-1/2})$. Differentiating under the integral, $\frac d{d\delta}\Big[-\frac12\int\frac{(\varphi+\psi)(u)}{u+\delta}du\Big]=\frac12\int\frac{(\varphi+\psi)(u)}{(u+\delta)^2}du$, and the same substitution gives $\frac C2\delta^{-1}L^{-1/2}\int_0^\infty\frac{(1-\log w/L)^{-1/2}}{(1+w)^2}dw+o(\delta^{-1}L^{-3/2})$, where $\int_0^\infty\frac{\log w}{(1+w)^2}dw=0$ by $w\mapsto1/w$; so the derivative is $\frac C2\delta^{-1}L^{-1/2}\big(1+O(L^{-2})\big)+o(\delta^{-1}L^{-3/2})$, which is $\frac d{d\delta}\big[-CL^{1/2}\big]+o(\delta^{-1}L^{-3/2})$.
\end{proof}

\begin{proposition}[edge law implies boundary law]\label{prop:flux}
Let $f$ be the minimizer at a support $a$ that is not an entry, and suppose it satisfies the hypothesis of Proposition~\ref{prop:tail}, the edge law with two derivatives. Then, with $B_T$ as in Proposition~\ref{prop:pairing},
\[
\lim_{T\to\infty}B_T=aC^2,\qquad\text{hence}\qquad\mathcal D(f)=2aC^2\qquad\text{and}\qquad\lambda'(a)=-2C^2
\]
at every point of differentiability of $\lambda$ (Proposition~\ref{prop:dini}): Conjecture~\ref{conj:hadamard} holds. More precisely, in the model $\psi\equiv0$,
\begin{gather*}
B_T=aC^2J_1(L_T)-aC\,g_{\mathrm{reg}}(a)\,L_T^{-1/2}\big(1+O(1/L_T)\big)+O(1/T),\\
J_1(L)=-\frac2\pi\int_0^\infty\!\!\int_0^\infty\Big[\frac{L+\log(1/v)}{L+\log(1/s)}\Big]^{1/2}k'(s+v)\,ds\,dv=1+O(L^{-2}),
\end{gather*}
with $k(w)=\sin w/w$, $L_T=L(1/T)$ and $g_{\mathrm{reg}}(a)=2P\sinh(a/2)$.
\end{proposition}
\begin{proof}
\emph{One edge.} $g_{\mathrm{out}}$ and $(xf)'$ are odd, so $B_T=2B_T^+$ with $B_T^+=\int_{\delta>0}((xf)')_T(a+\delta)\,g_{\mathrm{out}}(a+\delta)\,d\delta$.

\emph{The leak.} Near the right edge write $f(a-\delta)=u(\delta)=(\varphi+\psi)(\delta)\chi(\delta)$ as in the proof of Proposition~\ref{prop:tail}, so that $(xf)'(a-\delta)=-au'(\delta)+b(\delta)$ with $b=u+\delta u'=O(L^{-1/2})$ bounded; the rest of $(xf)'$ is $C^2$ with compact support in $(-a,a)$ except at the left edge, where it has the mirror singularity. For $\delta>0$, $((xf)')_T(a+\delta)=\int(xf)'(y)D_T(a+\delta-y)\,dy$ with $D_T(s)=\sin(Ts)/(\pi s)$. The part of $(xf)'$ supported at distance at least $c>0$ from the right edge has transform $O(t^{-1}\log^{-1/2}t)$ (Step 1 of that proof), and its leak at $a+\delta$ is the tail $\int_{|t|>T}$ of the inversion integral against $e^{-it(a+\delta-y)}$, $|a+\delta-y|\ge c$, which is $O(1/(cT\log^{1/2}T))$ uniformly in $\delta$ after one integration by parts; paired with $g_{\mathrm{out}}\in L^1$ it tends to zero. The part $b$ leaks like $f$ itself, $O\big(L_T^{-1/2}\min(1,1/(T\delta))\big)$, and its pairing with $-CL^{1/2}$ is $O(1/T)$ by Lemma~\ref{lem:overlap}, with $g_{\mathrm{reg}}+\rho$ it is $O(L_T^{-1/2}\log T/T)$. The main part is $-au'$, whose leak is, by one integration by parts ($u(0)=0$, $u$ cut off smoothly at $\delta_0$),
\[
-a\int_0^{\delta_0}u'(\delta')D_T(\delta+\delta')\,d\delta'=a\,\ell_T'(\delta),\qquad \ell_T(\delta)=\int_0^{\delta_0}u(\delta')D_T(\delta+\delta')\,d\delta' ,
\]
the derivative of the leak of $f$ of Lemma~\ref{lem:overlap}.

\emph{The pairing.} Write $g_{\mathrm{out}}(a+\delta)=-CL(\delta)^{1/2}+g_{\mathrm{reg}}(a)+\rho(\delta)$ by Lemmas~\ref{lem:cont} and~\ref{lem:force}. (i) With the singular force, $a\int\ell_T'(\delta)\big(-CL(\delta)^{1/2}\big)d\delta=-aC\iint u(\delta')L(\delta)^{1/2}\,\partial_\delta D_T(\delta+\delta')\,d\delta'\,d\delta$. For $u=\varphi\chi$ the substitution $s=T\delta'$, $v=T\delta$ gives $L(\delta')=L_T+\log(1/s)$, $L(\delta)=L_T+\log(1/v)$, $\partial_\delta D_T(\delta+\delta')=T^2k'(s+v)/\pi$, $d\delta'\,d\delta=ds\,dv/T^2$, and the double integral becomes $\frac{aC^2}2J_1(L_T)$, taken in the Abel sense and cut where the model ends, as in Lemma~\ref{lem:overlap}. Here $J_1(\infty)=-\frac2\pi\int_0^\infty\!\int_0^\infty k'(s+v)\,ds\,dv=\frac2\pi\int_0^\infty k=1$, and the term of order $1/L$ vanishes for the reason given there: $k'(s+v)$ is symmetric in $(s,v)$ and the logarithm of the ratio antisymmetric. Numerically $J_1(L)=1.029,\,1.016,\,1.012,\,1.008,\,1.006,\,1.004$ for $L=3,\dots,8$, about $1+0.27/L^2$ (\texttt{rh\_sum\_rule.py j1}). The $\psi$-part of $u$ contributes $o(1)$: with $|\psi'(\delta')|\le\varepsilon(\delta')\delta'^{-1}L^{-3/2}$, $\varepsilon\to0$, the same substitution bounds it by $o(1)$ times $\int_0^\infty(L+\log(1/s))^{-3/2}ds/s=2L^{-1/2}$, which balances the $L^{1/2}$ of the force. (ii) With the regular value, $a\,g_{\mathrm{reg}}\int_0^\infty\ell_T'(\delta)\,d\delta=-a\,g_{\mathrm{reg}}\,\ell_T(0^+)=-\frac{aC}2g_{\mathrm{reg}}L_T^{-1/2}\big(1+O(1/L_T)\big)$, since $\ell_T(0^+)=\frac C\pi\int_0^\infty(L_T+\log(1/s))^{-1/2}\frac{\sin s}s\,ds+o(L_T^{-1/2})$. (iii) With the remainder, $a\int\ell_T'\rho=-a\int\ell_T\rho'$ (as $\rho(0^+)=0$ and $\ell_T(\infty)=0$), and $|\ell_T(\delta)|\le CL_T^{-1/2}\min(1,1/(T\delta))$ with $\rho'=o(\delta^{-1}L^{-3/2})$ gives $o(L_T^{-1})$.

Collecting, $B_T^+=\frac{aC^2}2J_1(L_T)-\frac{aC}2g_{\mathrm{reg}}L_T^{-1/2}\big(1+O(1/L_T)\big)+o(1)$, so $B_T\to aC^2$; the rest is Proposition~\ref{prop:pairing}, which gives $\mathcal D=2B_\infty$, and Proposition~\ref{prop:dini}, which gives $-a\lambda'=\mathcal D$. The regular value is $g_{\mathrm{reg}}(a)=2P\sinh(a/2)$ by Lemma~\ref{lem:cont}.
\end{proof}

The rate is visible in the data of Computation~\ref{comp:pairing}: on the prime-free critical point at $a=0.5$ ($C=1.0615$, $\beta=-1.469$ fitted on the edge nodes, against $C=1.0626$ and $\beta=-1.400$ of Proposition~\ref{prop:profile} below, the fitted $\beta$ absorbing the $O(L^{-2})$ correction over the fit's window; $g_{\mathrm{reg}}=0.0679$), $aC^2-B_T=0.040,\,0.014,\,0.0135,\,0.0125$ at $3$, $5$, $10$, $20$ horizons, where the leading term $aC\,g_{\mathrm{reg}}L_T^{-1/2}$ is $0.027$ to $0.019$; at these heights $L_T$ is only $1.8$ to $3.7$ and the expansion is not yet quantitative, but the sign and the order are those of the law. In Fourier terms the same term is the polar cross term of the edge, $-\frac1\pi\int_T^\infty 4aP\sinh(a/2)|A_0|\sin\theta(t)\,dt/(t\tilde\sigma^{1/2})$ with $\sin\theta\approx-\pi/(4\log t)$, and $C=|A_0|$.

For the prime-free form the edge law with its two derivatives, and with its constants, follows from the representation.

\begin{proposition}[the edge law from the representation]\label{prop:profile}
Assume (H$_\infty$), and let $f$ be the minimizer of $W_\infty-2P^2$ at a support $a$ with $\lambda=\lambda_\infty(a)>\Psi_\infty(0)$, $A_0$ its edge amplitude (Corollary~\ref{cor:edgeform}). Then $f$ satisfies the hypothesis of Proposition~\ref{prop:tail} with
\[
C=|A_0|,\qquad \beta=-\gamma-\log(2\pi a)-\lambda ,
\]
$\gamma$ Euler's constant, and with $\psi^{(k)}(\delta)=O\big(\delta^{-k}L(\delta)^{-5/2}\big)$ for $k\le2$. Equivalently, with $\sigma_\infty=\Psi_\infty-\lambda$,
\[
f(a-\delta)=|A_0|\,\big(\sigma_\infty(1/\delta)-\gamma\big)^{-1/2}\big(1+O(L^{-2})\big):
\]
the profile of the edge is the inverse square root of the symbol at frequency $1/\delta$, shifted by Euler's constant.
\end{proposition}
\begin{proof}
\emph{The model.} For $s>0$ and $\chi$ smooth, $1$ on $[0,\delta_0/2]$ and $0$ beyond $\delta_0<1$, put $\Phi_s(t)=\int_0^{\delta_0}(\log(1/\delta))^{-s}\chi(\delta)e^{-it\delta}\,d\delta$. From the Mellin representation $(\log(1/\delta))^{-s}=\Gamma(s)^{-1}\int_0^\infty x^{s-1}\delta^x\,dx$ and $\int_0^\infty\delta^xe^{-it\delta}\,d\delta=\Gamma(1+x)(it)^{-1-x}=\frac{-i}t\Gamma(1+x)e^{-x\Lambda}$ with $\Lambda=\log t+i\pi/2$ ($t>0$), the Mellin-transform method for Fourier integrals with a logarithmic endpoint singularity \cite[Ch.~4]{bleistein-handelsman} gives, as $t\to+\infty$,
\[
\Phi_s(t)=\frac{-i}{t}\,\Lambda^{-s}\sum_{k<N}\frac{\Gamma(s+k)}{\Gamma(s)\,k!}\,\Gamma^{(k)}(1)\,\Lambda^{-k}+O\big(t^{-1}|\Lambda|^{-s-N}\big),
\]
an expansion that may be differentiated in $t$ term by term. For $s=\frac12$, with $\Gamma'(1)=-\gamma$ and $\Gamma''(1)=\gamma^2+\pi^2/6$,
\[
\Phi_{1/2}(t)=\frac{-i}{t}\Lambda^{-1/2}\Big[1-\frac{\gamma}{2\Lambda}+\frac{3(\gamma^2+\pi^2/6)}{8\Lambda^2}+O(\Lambda^{-3})\Big]=\frac{-i}{t}(\Lambda+\gamma)^{-1/2}\Big[1+\frac{\pi^2}{16\Lambda^2}+O(\Lambda^{-3})\Big],
\]
and with $M=\log t+\gamma$ one has $(\Lambda+\gamma)^{-1/2}=(M+i\pi/2)^{-1/2}=M^{-1/2}e^{-i\pi/(4M)}\big(1-\frac{\pi^2}{16M^2}+O(M^{-3})\big)$, so that
\[
\Phi_{1/2}(t)=\frac{-i}t\,M^{-1/2}e^{-i\pi/(4M)}\big(1+O(M^{-3})\big):
\]
the real corrections of order $M^{-2}$ cancel. Scaling $\delta$ by $e^{\beta'}$, the transform of $C(\log(e^{\beta'}/\delta))^{-1/2}\chi(\delta)$ is $\frac{-iC}{t}M_{\beta'}^{-1/2}e^{-i\pi/(4M_{\beta'})}\big(1+O(M^{-3})\big)$ with $M_{\beta'}=\log t+\beta'+\gamma$.

\emph{The factor.} By Stirling's formula and the rational factor, $\tilde\sigma(t)=\ell(t)\big(1+O(t^{-2})\big)$ with $\ell(t)=\log(t/2\pi)-\lambda$; and substituting $\tau=tx$ in \eqref{eq:theta}, $\theta(t)=\frac1\pi\int_0^\infty\frac{u(tx)-u(t)}{1-x^2}\,dx$ with $u=\log\tilde\sigma$. For $tx\ge\tau_0$, $u(tx)-u(t)=\log\big(1+\log x/\ell(t)\big)+O(t^{-2})$; expanding in powers of $\log x/\ell$, with $\int_0^\infty\frac{(\log x)^k}{1-x^2}\,dx=0$ for even $k$ (by $x\mapsto1/x$) and $=-\pi^2/4$, $-\pi^4/8$ for $k=1,3$, while the range $x<\tau_0/t$ and the tails $|\log x|>\ell$ contribute $O(\log\log t/t)$,
\[
\theta(t)=-\frac\pi{4\ell}-\frac{\pi^3}{24\ell^3}+O(\ell^{-5})+O\Big(\frac{\log\log t}t\Big),
\]
and the same representation differentiated under the integral gives $\theta^{(j)}$, $j\le3$, with the expansion differentiated term by term. Hence, differentiably,
\[
\frac{A_0}{t\,\sigma_-(t)}=\frac{-i|A_0|}{t}\,\ell^{-1/2}e^{-i\pi/(4\ell)}\Big(1+O(\ell^{-3})+O\Big(\frac{\log\log t}t\Big)\Big),
\]
where $A_0=-i|A_0|$ for a minimizer positive near the right edge.

\emph{Matching.} Choose $\beta'$ with $\beta'+\gamma=-\log2\pi-\lambda$, so that $M_{\beta'}=\ell$: the transform $\hat\varphi$ of the model $\varphi(\delta)=|A_0|\big(\log(a/\delta)+\beta\big)^{-1/2}\chi(\delta)$, $\beta=\beta'-\log a=-\gamma-\log(2\pi a)-\lambda$, agrees with $A_0/(t\sigma_-)$ to relative order $\ell^{-3}$. By Corollary~\ref{cor:edgeform},
\[
D(t):=\tilde F(t)-\hat\varphi(t)=\frac{A(t)-A_0}{t\,\sigma_-(t)}+\Big[\frac{A_0}{t\,\sigma_-(t)}-\hat\varphi(t)\Big]+\frac{e^{-2ita}\Phi_-(t)}{\tilde\sigma(t)} .
\]
(i) $A(t)-A_0=O(\log t/t)$ with $(A-A_0)^{(j)}=O(\log t/t^{1+j})$, $j\le3$: its pieces in the proof of Corollary~\ref{cor:edgeform} are a rational function, $t$ times the Riesz projection of a smooth function with derivatives $O(t^{-2-j})$, and $t$ times the transform of a $C^\infty$ function on $u>0$ with all derivatives integrable, and in each case the derivative of $t\,P_\mp[\,\cdot\,]$ minus its limit loses one power of $t$ (for the projection, by the Hilbert-transform estimate of that proof applied to the derivatives); so the first piece is $O(t^{-2}\log t)$ with derivatives $O(t^{-2-j}\log t)$. (ii) The bracket is $O(t^{-1}\ell^{-7/2})$ with derivatives $O(t^{-1-j}\ell^{-7/2})$, $j\le3$. (iii) The third piece is the transform of $\mathrm{far}*\check{(1/\tilde\sigma)}$ with $\mathrm{far}$ supported in $u\le-2a$, and the kernel of $1/\tilde\sigma$ is $C^\infty$ and exponentially decaying away from the origin ($\tilde\sigma$ is zero-free in a strip around the line, as in Lemma~\ref{lem:kernel}), so its inverse transform is $C^\infty$ on $(-2a+\varepsilon,0]$.

\emph{The remainder.} For $D\in C^3$ with $D^{(j)}(t)=O(t^{-1-j}\Lambda(t))$, $j\le3$, $\Lambda$ slowly varying, the inverse transform $\psi$ satisfies $\psi^{(k)}(\delta)=O(\delta^{-k}\Lambda(1/\delta))$ for $k=1,2$: split $\int(it)^kD\,e^{it\delta}\,dt$ at $|t|=1/\delta$, bound the inner part by $\int^{1/\delta}t^{k-1}\Lambda$, and integrate the outer part by parts three times, the boundary terms being $(t^kD)^{(m)}(1/\delta)\,\delta^{-m-1}$, $m\le2$, and the integral $\delta^{-3}\int_{1/\delta}^\infty|(t^kD)'''|$. Applied to (ii) with $\Lambda=\ell^{-7/2}$, to (i) with $\Lambda=t^{-1}\log t$ (where the same splitting gives $O(\delta^{1-k}\log^2(1/\delta))$), and with (iii) smooth, the inverse transform $\psi=f(a-\delta)-\varphi(\delta)$ of $D$ satisfies $\psi^{(k)}=O(\delta^{-k}L^{-7/2})+O(\delta^{1-k}\log^2(1/\delta))$ for $k=1,2$. Since $f(a^-)=0$ by Theorem~\ref{thm:edge} and $\varphi(0^+)=0$, $\psi(\delta)=\int_0^\delta\psi'=O(L(\delta)^{-5/2})$. Finally $f$ is $C^\infty$ inside the window, as $(K_\infty-\lambda)f=2Ps$ there and the symbol is elliptic at infinity. This is the hypothesis of Proposition~\ref{prop:tail} with the stated $C$, $\beta$ and remainder; the second form of the statement is $\log(a/\delta)+\beta=\sigma_\infty(1/\delta)-\gamma+O(\delta^2)$.
\end{proof}

\begin{corollary}[the boundary law and the tail law for the prime-free form]\label{cor:primefree}
Assume (H$_\infty$). For the minimizer of $W_\infty-2P^2$ at a support $a$ with $\lambda_\infty(a)>\Psi_\infty(0)$, at every point of differentiability of $\lambda_\infty$,
\[
\lambda_\infty'(a)=-2|A_0|^2,\qquad \lim_{T\to\infty}\,T\cdot\frac1{2\pi}\int_{|t|>T}|F(t)|^2\big(\Psi_\infty(t)-\lambda\big)\,dt=\frac{2|A_0|^2}\pi=-\frac{\lambda_\infty'(a)}\pi :
\]
the analytic tail law of Computation~\ref{comp:analytic} is a theorem, and Conjecture~\ref{conj:wh}(i) holds with $|A|^2=-\lambda'/2$.
\end{corollary}
\begin{proof}
Propositions~\ref{prop:dini}, \ref{prop:pairing} and~\ref{prop:flux} hold for the prime-free form with the same proofs ($K_\infty$, $\Psi_\infty$ and $W_\infty-2P^2$ in place of $K$, $\Psi$ and $Q$; the entries play no role). Proposition~\ref{prop:profile} supplies the hypothesis of Proposition~\ref{prop:flux} with $C=|A_0|$, which gives the first identity, and the tail energy is $2|A_0|^2/(\pi T)\,(1+o(1))$ by Corollary~\ref{cor:edgeform}, as computed after it.
\end{proof}

\begin{computation}[the constants of the edge]\label{comp:edgeconst}
\texttt{rh\_edge\_constants.py}, log in \texttt{data/}. On the prime-free edge-FEM critical points at $a=0.3,0.4,0.5,0.6$ ($\lambda=0.2226$, $-0.0780$, $-0.3222$, $-0.5359$; $\lambda'=-3.429$, $-2.666$, $-2.258$, $-2.039$ from the dilation engine) the ratio of the profile $f(a-\delta)$ to the prediction $(-\lambda'/2)^{1/2}\big(\log(a/\delta)+\beta\big)^{-1/2}$ of Proposition~\ref{prop:profile} and Corollary~\ref{cor:primefree}, with $\beta=-\gamma-\log(2\pi a)-\lambda=-1.434$, $-1.421$, $-1.400$, $-1.368$ and nothing fitted, is $1.001$--$1.003$ at every node between $\delta=10^{-10}$ and $10^{-5}$ at all four supports; it rises to $1.01$ at $\delta=10^{-3}$, where $L$ is only $6$, and to $1.004$ at the last nodes near $10^{-12}$. On the mesh refined below $10^{-6}$ (Computation~\ref{comp:whphase}) the ratio at $a=0.5$ is $1.0003$, $1.0005$, $1.0009$ at $\delta=10^{-10},10^{-8},10^{-6}$, so the residual is the discretization. The amplitude $C$ of Computation~\ref{comp:edge}, fitted there, is $(-\lambda'/2)^{1/2}$, and $\beta$ is the symbol's: $\log(a/\delta)+\beta=\sigma_\infty(1/\delta)-\gamma$.
\end{computation}

The entries inside the window are a finite perturbation of this structure, and for one entry everything goes through with the same constants.

\begin{proposition}[the minimizer with an entry inside the window]\label{prop:whfull}
Assume (H$_\infty$). Let $f$ be the minimizer at a support $a$ with exactly one entry $n$ inside the window and $a<d<2a$, $d=\log n$ (that is, $a_2<a<a_3$, $n=2$), $c=\Lambda(n)n^{-1/2}$, $\lambda>\Psi_\infty(0)$. Let $\tilde\sigma_\infty$ be $\Psi_\infty-\lambda$ with its real zero divided out and $k$ the kernel of $1/\tilde\sigma_\infty$, and put $L=(-a,a-d)$, $M=(a-d,d-a)$, $R=(d-a,a)$, $f_X=f\mathbf 1_X$. Then
\begin{equation}\label{eq:echoexact}
f(a+u)=c\,\big(k*f_R(a+\cdot+d)\big)(u)+r(u),\qquad r\in C^1\ \text{near }u=-d,\quad r''(u)=O\big(|u+d|^{-1}(\log1/|u+d|)^{-5/2}\big),
\end{equation}
the echo of the right edge at $a-d$; and, with $\sigma_{\mathrm{eff}}=\tilde\sigma_\infty-c^2/\tilde\sigma_\infty$, $\tilde\sigma_{\mathrm{eff}}$ its positive part after dividing out the finitely many real zeros and $\sigma_{\mathrm{eff},\pm}$ its factors (Lemma~\ref{lem:factor}),
\begin{equation}\label{eq:whfull}
\tilde F=\frac{P_-\big[\hat H'_-/\sigma_{\mathrm{eff},+}\big]}{\sigma_{\mathrm{eff},-}}\qquad\text{on }\R ,
\end{equation}
where $\hat H'_-$ is in $H^1(\R)$, analytic in the lower half-plane, and is the transform of a source made of the polar step and a smooth part near the edge, and of sources supported at distance at least $\delta_0=\min(d,2a-d)$ to the left of it.
\end{proposition}
\begin{proof}
On the window $Kf=\lambda f+2Ps$ and $K=K_\infty+K_P$ with $K_Pf=-\sum_mc_m\big(f(\cdot-\log m)+f(\cdot+\log m)\big)$; the shifts by $\log m>2a$ move the window off itself, and for $m=n$, $f(y-d)=f_L(y-d)$ on $R$, $f(y+d)=f_R(y+d)$ on $L$, both vanish on $M$. Hence on the whole line
\[
K_\infty f=\lambda f+2Ps\,\mathbf 1_{[-a,a]}+c\,f_L(\cdot-d)+c\,f_R(\cdot+d)+g_\infty\mathbf 1_{|y|>a},\qquad g_\infty=K_\infty f\ \text{outside},
\]
and after transforming and multiplying by $e^{-ita}$, $\sigma_\infty\tilde F=\tilde H+\tilde G_+$ with $\tilde G_+$ the archimedean force to the right of $a$ and $\tilde H$ the transform of the ``$-$'' sources: the polar term, the archimedean force to the left of $-a$, and the two copies, supported in $u\in(d-2a,0)$ and $u\in(-2a,-d)$ respectively ($u=y-a$). All sources are in $L^1\cap L^2$ with $uh\in L^2$, so Proposition~\ref{prop:whexact} applies verbatim: $\tilde F=P_-[\hat H_-/\sigma_{\infty,+}]/\sigma_{\infty,-}$ with $\hat H_-$ the sources corrected at the real zero.

\emph{The echo.} The copy on $L$ has transform $c\,e^{-itd}\tilde F_R$, $\tilde F_R=e^{-ita}\widehat{f_R}$, and its contribution to $\tilde F$ is $P_-[c\,e^{-itd}\tilde F_R/\sigma_{\infty,+}]/\sigma_{\infty,-}=c\,e^{-itd}\tilde F_R/\tilde\sigma_\infty-P_+[c\,e^{-itd}\tilde F_R/\sigma_{\infty,+}]/\sigma_{\infty,-}$. The first term is the transform of $c\,k*f_R(a+\cdot+d)$; the second is the transform of a function smooth on $u<0$: by Lemma~\ref{lem:kernel} the kernel of $1/\sigma_{\infty,+}$ is smooth on $[d,\infty)$, so $P_+[\cdot]$ is the transform of a smooth function on $u>0$, and $1/\sigma_{\infty,-}$ then spreads it to $u<0$ through its kernel evaluated away from the origin. The remaining terms of $\tilde F$ are the polar step, the far force (smooth near $-d$), the copy on $R$ (supported in $u\in(d-2a,0)$, whose contribution is smooth away from its support's endpoints), and the rational corrections of Proposition~\ref{prop:whexact}, which are weighted primitives of the sources, $-ie^{\pm it_\lambda u}\int_{-\infty}^uh(u')e^{\mp it_\lambda u'}du'$: one derivative smoother than the sources, with the same singular support and a step at the edge. All are smooth near $u=-d$ except the correction of the copy on $L$, which is $C^1$ there with the second derivative of the primitive of the edge profile, $O(|u+d|^{-1}L^{-3/2})$, and $O(|u+d|^{-1}L^{-5/2})$ after the convolution with $k$, which gains a logarithm; this is \eqref{eq:echoexact}, with $k$ smooth away from the origin and $f_R(a+\cdot+d)$ supported in $u\le-d$.

\emph{The return.} The copy on $R$ near the edge is $c\,f_L(a+u-d)=c\,f(a+(u-d))$ for $u\in(d-2a,0)$, and by \eqref{eq:echoexact} at $u-d$, $f(a+(u-d))=c\,(k*f_R(a+\cdot))(u)+r(u-d)$; so the copy on $R$ equals $c^2(k*f_R(a+\cdot))(u)\mathbf 1_{u<0}$ plus $c\,r(u-d)\mathbf 1_{u<0}$, a function $C^1$ up to the edge with a step there (value $c\,r(-d)$) and a transform $O(t^{-2}(\log t)^{-3/2})$ beyond the step, plus, at its left end $u=d-2a$, the copy of the left edge. Its transform is therefore $c^2P_-[\tilde F_R/\tilde\sigma_\infty]$ plus a step plus a far-type source. Since $\tilde F_R=\tilde F-\tilde F_L-\tilde F_M$ and $f_L,f_M$ are supported at distance at least $2a-d$ from the edge, $c^2P_-[\tilde F_R/\tilde\sigma_\infty]=c^2P_-[\tilde F/\tilde\sigma_\infty]-c^2P_-[(\tilde F_L+\tilde F_M)/\tilde\sigma_\infty]$, the second term the transform of a source smooth near the edge. Write $c^2P_-[\tilde F/\tilde\sigma_\infty]=c^2\tilde F/\tilde\sigma_\infty-c^2P_+[\tilde F/\tilde\sigma_\infty]$ and move the first term to the left of $\tilde\sigma_\infty\tilde F=\hat H_-+\hat G_+$: this is $\sigma_{\mathrm{eff}}\tilde F=\hat H'_-+\hat G'_+$ with $\hat H'_-=\hat H_--c^2P_-[\tilde F/\tilde\sigma_\infty]$ and $\hat G'_+=\hat G_++c^2P_+[\tilde F/\tilde\sigma_\infty]$, a ``$-$'' and a ``$+$'' function with the properties required in the proof of Proposition~\ref{prop:whexact} ($1/\tilde\sigma_\infty$ has an integrable kernel, so $f*k\in L^1$). The symbol $\sigma_{\mathrm{eff}}$ is real, even, real-analytic, $\sim\log t$, with finitely many real zeros; dividing them out and applying the Liouville argument of that proof gives \eqref{eq:whfull}, and $\hat H'_-$ has the stated structure.
\end{proof}

\begin{corollary}[the edge, the echo and the tail law with an entry inside the window]\label{cor:full}
Under the hypotheses of Proposition~\ref{prop:whfull}: (i) the edge of the transform has the form of Corollary~\ref{cor:edgeform} with $\sigma_{\mathrm{eff}}$ in place of $\sigma$, $A(t)\to A_0$ purely imaginary; (ii) $f$ satisfies the hypothesis of Proposition~\ref{prop:tail} at the edges, with the same constants as in Proposition~\ref{prop:profile}, $C=|A_0|$ and $\beta=-\gamma-\log(2\pi a)-\lambda$, and is smooth on the window except at the edges and at the two echo points $\pm(a-d)$, where it is continuous with the singularity of $k*(\text{edge profile})$; (iii) the echo form \eqref{eq:echo} holds, with $\rho=c/\tilde\sigma_\infty$; (iv) $\lambda'(a)=-2|A_0|^2$ at every point of differentiability (Conjecture~\ref{conj:hadamard} at these supports); (v) under RH, $\lim_{T\to\infty}T\sum_{|\gamma|>T}|F(\gamma)|^2=-\lambda'(a)/\pi$: Conjecture~\ref{conj:tail} holds for $a_2<a<a_3$.
\end{corollary}
\begin{proof}
(i) is the proof of Corollary~\ref{cor:edgeform} applied to \eqref{eq:whfull}: the near sources of $\hat H'_-$ are the steps $-2iP\sinh(a/2)-ic\,f(a-d)$ and a smooth part, the far sources sit at distance at least $\delta_0$, and Lemma~\ref{lem:kernel} holds for $\sigma_{\mathrm{eff}}$ (a smooth symbol, $\sigma_{\mathrm{eff}}=\ell-c^2/\ell+O(t^{-2})$, zero-free in a strip once its real zeros are divided out), so the far feedback is again $-ir(0^+)+O(1/t)$ and the symmetry argument gives $A_0$ purely imaginary. (ii) The proof of Proposition~\ref{prop:profile} goes through with $\sigma_{\mathrm{eff}}$: $\theta_{\mathrm{eff}}=\theta_\infty+\tfrac12\mathcal H[\log(1-c^2/\ell^2)]=-\pi/(4\ell)+O(\ell^{-3})$, since the added term is even and $O(\ell^{-2})$ with the expansion of that proof giving $\pi c^2/(2\ell^3)$ at the first order, and $\tilde\sigma_{\mathrm{eff}}^{-1/2}=\ell^{-1/2}\big(1+c^2/(2\ell^2)+O(\ell^{-3})\big)$; so the edge term matches the model $|A_0|(\log(a/\delta)+\beta)^{-1/2}$ with the same $\beta$ up to a relative $c^2/(2\ell^2)$, whose inverse transform is $\tfrac12c^2|A_0|(L+\beta)^{-5/2}(1+O(1/L))$ by the Mellin expansion with $s=\tfrac52$, inside the remainder class; the far-type sources contribute smooth functions near the edge. The regularity off the edges is interior regularity on $L$, $M$, $R$ (the forcing $c\,f_L(\cdot-d)$ on $R$ is smooth inside $R$), and the structure at $\pm(a-d)$ is \eqref{eq:echoexact} and its mirror. (iii) The copy on $L$ contributes $c\,e^{-itd}\tilde F_R/\tilde\sigma_\infty$ to $\tilde F$ up to smooth feedback, by the proof above, and $\tilde F_R=A(t)/(t\sigma_{\mathrm{eff},-})+\dots$ by (i); with $F=2i\,\mathrm{Im}(F_R+F_e)$ this is \eqref{eq:echo}. (iv) Lemma~\ref{lem:force} holds with the copies, which are continuous across the edge with the cusp of the echo, $O(L^{-3/2})$ with derivative $O(\delta^{-1}L^{-5/2})$, inside the remainder class; and in the proof of Proposition~\ref{prop:flux} the singularities of $(xf)'$ at the echo points, at distance at least $\delta_0$ from the edge, leak like the left edge does. (v) Proposition~\ref{prop:tail} applies with one change in its Step~1: $f_0$ now carries the two echo cusps, whose transforms are $O(t^{-1}L^{-3/2})$ with the phases $e^{\pm it(a-d)}$; their squares are $O(t^{-2}L^{-3})$, and their cross terms with the edges oscillate at the frequencies $d$ and $2a-d$, both nonzero, which Step~2 handles exactly as it handles $\cos(2ta)$. Hence $\lim T\sum_{|\gamma|>T}|F(\gamma)|^2=2|A_0|^2/\pi=-\lambda'/\pi$ by (iv).
\end{proof}

\begin{computation}[the constants of the edge with the prime inside the window]\label{comp:edgefull}
\texttt{rh\_edge\_constants.py}, log in \texttt{data/}. On the minimizers with primes at $a=0.4,0.45,0.5$ ($\lambda=0.01471$, $0.002405$, $0.000194$; $\lambda'=-0.4659$, $-0.1013$, $-0.01124$ from the dilation engine; the entry $2$ inside the window, $0.347<a<0.549$) the ratio of $f(a-\delta)$ to $(-\lambda'/2)^{1/2}\big(\log(a/\delta)+\beta\big)^{-1/2}$ with $\beta=-\gamma-\log(2\pi a)-\lambda$ ($-1.514$, $-1.619$, $-1.722$), nothing fitted, is $1.000$--$1.002$ at every node between $\delta=10^{-12}$ and $10^{-6}$ at the three supports, $1.004$--$1.008$ at $10^{-4}$, and $1.03$--$1.05$ at $10^{-3}$, where $L$ is $6$ and the relative correction $c^2/(2L^2)$ of the symbol, $0.3\%$, is joined by the corrections of the asymptotic regime. The alternative $\beta-2c$, which a first-order return of the echo would produce, is off by $2$--$4\%$ over the whole range. So the prime inside the window changes neither constant: the edge amplitude is $(-\lambda'/2)^{1/2}$ and the shift is the symbol's.
\end{computation}

\begin{computation}[the boundary law]\label{comp:hadamard}
$C$ from the edge FEM (two-stage mesh to $10^{-12}a$, fit of $1/f^2$ against $\log(a/\delta)$ on $14\le t\le20$, residual $\le5\times10^{-5}$, mesh bias $10^{-3}$ to $10^{-2}$ in $C$) and $\lambda_{48}'$ from \texttt{rh\_dilation.py} (Hellmann--Feynman, ball arithmetic, $48$ modes; the truncation error of $\lambda_{48}'$ is below $10^{-4}$ at these supports by the $K$-stability of Computation~\ref{comp:relay}); data in \texttt{boundary\_law.csv} and \texttt{data/}, reproduced by \texttt{rh\_hadamard.py}:
\begin{center}
\begin{tabular}{@{}rcrccrc@{}}
\toprule
$a$ & primes & $\lambda$ (FEM) & $C$ & $\beta$ & $\lambda_{48}'$ & $r=-\lambda'/2C^2$\\
\midrule
$0.3$ & no & $+0.2225566$ & $1.30891$ & $-1.49$ & $-3.42881$ & $1.0007$\\
$0.4$ & no & $-0.0780502$ & $1.15425$ & $-1.48$ & $-2.66640$ & $1.0007$\\
$0.5$ & no & $-0.3222425$ & $1.06222$ & $-1.45$ & $-2.25815$ & $1.0007$\\
$0.6$ & no & $-0.5358860$ & $1.00932$ & $-1.42$ & $-2.03880$ & $1.0007$\\
$0.4$ & yes & $1.470510\times10^{-2}$ & $0.48266$ & $-1.57$ & $-0.465947$ & $1.0001$\\
$0.45$ & yes & $2.404578\times10^{-3}$ & $0.22494$ & $-1.67$ & $-0.101283$ & $1.0009$\\
$0.5$ & yes & $1.936801\times10^{-4}$ & $0.07493$ & $-1.78$ & $-1.123556\times10^{-2}$ & $1.0005$\\
$0.549$ & yes & $1.491447\times10^{-5}$ & $0.02412$ & $-1.87$ & $-1.143529\times10^{-3}$ & $0.983$\\
\bottomrule
\end{tabular}
\end{center}
Seven cases give $r=1$ to better than $10^{-3}$, across supports where the prime-free floor is positive and negative and with the primes present; the prime-free offset $7\times10^{-4}$ is the mesh bias of $C$ on that family, and the full-form offsets ($1,\,9,\,5\times10^{-4}$) are within the stated bias. The last case measures $C$ at $a_3=0.54931$ and $\lambda'$ at $0.549$, inside the sawtooth where $\Phi'$ changes by about $9\%$ per $0.001$ (Computation~\ref{comp:relay}); its $1.7\%$ is within the combined error of the amplitude ($10^{-2}$ in $C$, hence $2\%$ in $C^2$) and the offset in $a$. The evidence is confined to $a\le0.6$ (heights below $22$), far from the regime of Conjecture~\ref{conj:A}; extending it needs a multiprecision edge engine.
\end{computation}

An exactly solvable case: for one half of the Dirichlet form of $L_\Delta$ on $(-a,a)$, the scaling identity $L_\Delta(u(\cdot/a))=(L_\Delta u)(\cdot/a)-2\log a\,u(\cdot/a)$ gives $\lambda(a)=\lambda(1)-\log a$, so the law predicts $C(a)^2=1/(2a)$. The prime-free form differs from this by the bounded corrections (the polar term and $\Ree\psi-\log|t|$); its amplitudes give $2aC^2=1.028,\,1.066,\,1.129,\,1.223$ at $a=0.3,\dots,0.6$ and its derivatives $-a\lambda'=1.029,\,1.067,\,1.129,\,1.223$, both near $1$ and equal to each other, which is the content of the table restated, not an independent test.

\begin{remark}[consequence of Conjecture~\ref{conj:hadamard}]
$\Phi'(a)=2C(a)^2/\lambda(a)$, and Conjecture~\ref{conj:A} becomes
\[
C(a)^2\le\tfrac{c}{2}\,\Tst(a)\,\lambda(a)\qquad(a\ge a_0):
\]
the edge amplitude of the minimizer is bounded by its energy times the horizon. This is the form in which the conjecture is a statement about one function at one place. The relay of Computation~\ref{comp:relay} reads: as $a$ approaches an entry from below, $\lambda$ falls toward the deleted form's failure point while $C$ stays of the same order, and $\Phi'=2C^2/\lambda$ rises until the entry rescues the floor. Under the Riemann Hypothesis, $C(a)$ is the boundary trace of the band-limited function that best hides from the zeros above the horizon.
\end{remark}


\subsection{The echo tree}\label{sec:tree}

Proposition~\ref{prop:whfull} treats one entry and one echo. At a general support the entries copy the window onto itself in several ways, the echoes have echoes, and a copy of an echo can return to the edge along several chains. The structure is combinatorial, and it is finite as long as the walks that the entries generate inside the window are confined.

Let $D_a=\{\log m:\Lambda(m)\ne0,\ \log m<2a\}$ be the entries inside the window and $c_d=\Lambda(m)m^{-1/2}$ for $d=\log m$. A \emph{walk} is a finite sequence of points of $[-a,a]$ whose consecutive points differ by $\pm d$ with $d\in D_a$. The \emph{echo set} $S$ is the set of points visited by walks starting at $a$ or $-a$, and $S^\circ=S\cap(-a,a)$; $S=-S$. The walks are \emph{confined} at $a$, (F$_a$), if the set of points visited by walks starting anywhere in $[-a,a]$ is finite. Write $S=\{s_0=-a<s_1<\dots<s_N=a\}$, $I_j=(s_{j-1},s_j)$, $f_j=f\mathbf 1_{I_j}$. On $S^\circ$ let $C_{pq}=c_{|p-q|}$ if $|p-q|\in D_a$ and $0$ otherwise (the weighted adjacency of the echo points), $b_p=c_{a-p}$ if $a-p\in D_a$ and $0$ otherwise, $\bar b_p=b_{-p}$ (the couplings to the two edges), and let $C_a$ be the matrix on $S^\circ\cup\{a\}$ obtained from $C$ by adjoining the row and column $b$; $C$ and $C_a$ are real symmetric.

\begin{lemma}[the entries move the pieces onto pieces]\label{lem:pieces}
For $d\in D_a$ and each $j$, each of $I_j+d$ and $I_j-d$ is either disjoint from $(-a,a)$ or equal to some $I_{j'}$.
\end{lemma}
\begin{proof}
If $s_{j-1}+d\ge a$, the interval $I_j+d$ lies to the right of the window. Otherwise $s_{j-1}+d\in[-a,a)$ is one step from a point of $S$, so it belongs to $S$; if also $s_j+d\le a$, then $s_j+d\in S$ for the same reason, and no point $q$ of $S$ lies strictly between them, since $q-d$ would be a point of $S$ in $I_j$; so $I_j+d=I_{j'}$. The remaining case $s_{j-1}+d<a<s_j+d$ is impossible: then $a-d\in I_j$, while $a-d\in S$ because $d<2a$. The same holds for $-d$.
\end{proof}

\begin{proposition}[the echo tree]\label{prop:tree}
Assume (H$_\infty$). Let $f$ be the minimizer at a support $a$ that is not an entry, with $\lambda>\Psi_\infty(0)$ and the walks confined, (F$_a$). Let $\tilde\sigma_\infty$ and $k$ be as in Proposition~\ref{prop:whfull}, and for $p\in S$ let $\Phi_p(t)=\int\chi(v)f(p+v)e^{itv}\,dv$, with one fixed $\chi\in C_c^\infty$ equal to $1$ near $0$ and supported in $(-\delta_1,\delta_1)$, $\delta_1$ half the least gap of $S$; for $p=\pm a$ this is the transform of $f$ near the edge, and $\Phi_{-a}=-\overline{\Phi_a}$. Then:
\begin{enumerate}
\item[(i)] $f\in C^\infty\big((-a,a)\setminus S^\circ\big)$.
\item[(ii)] For $p\in S^\circ$, near $v=0$,
\begin{equation}\label{eq:localecho}
f(p+v)=\sum_{q\in S,\ |p-q|\in D_a}c_{|p-q|}\,\big(k*f(q+\cdot)\big)(v)+r_p(v),
\end{equation}
where $f(q+\cdot)$ is the window function shifted by $q$ and $r_p$ is $C^1$ near $0$ with $r_p''(v)=O\big(|v|^{-1}(\log1/|v|)^{-5/2}\big)$. In Fourier form,
\begin{equation}\label{eq:treesystem}
\big(\tilde\sigma_\infty(t)I-C\big)\Phi_{S^\circ}(t)=b\,\Phi_a(t)+\bar b\,\Phi_{-a}(t)+\Psi(t),\qquad \Psi(t)=O\big(t^{-2}(\log t)^{-3/2}\big).
\end{equation}
\item[(iii)] Let $R=b^{T}(\tilde\sigma_\infty I-C)^{-1}b$ and $\bar R=\bar b^{T}(\tilde\sigma_\infty I-C)^{-1}b$, the returns of the right edge to itself and to the left edge through the tree, and
\begin{equation}\label{eq:sigmatree}
\sigma_{\mathrm{eff}}=\tilde\sigma_\infty-R=\frac{\det(\tilde\sigma_\infty I-C_a)}{\det(\tilde\sigma_\infty I-C)},
\end{equation}
a rational function of $\tilde\sigma_\infty(t)$ whose real zeros and poles are the heights at which $\tilde\sigma_\infty$ equals an eigenvalue of $C_a$, respectively of $C$: finitely many, none above the height where $\tilde\sigma_\infty>\|C_a\|$, and $\sigma_{\mathrm{eff}}=\tilde\sigma_\infty-|b|^2/\tilde\sigma_\infty+O(\tilde\sigma_\infty^{-2})$. With $\tilde\sigma_{\mathrm{eff}}>0$ the symbol with its real zeros and poles divided out by rational factors and $\sigma_{\mathrm{eff},\pm}$ its factors (Lemma~\ref{lem:factor}),
\begin{equation}\label{eq:whtree}
\tilde F=\frac{P_-\big[\hat H''_-/\sigma_{\mathrm{eff},+}\big]}{\sigma_{\mathrm{eff},-}}\qquad\text{on }\R ,
\end{equation}
where $\hat H''_-\in H^1(\R)$ is analytic in the lower half-plane and is the transform of a source made of steps at the edge, a part smooth near the edge, a part whose transform is $O(t^{-2}(\log t)^{-3/2})$, the mirror chains $P_-[\bar R\,\Phi_{-a}]$, and sources supported at distance at least $\delta_0=a-\max S^\circ$ to the left of the edge.
\end{enumerate}
\end{proposition}
\begin{proof}
\emph{The equation on the line.} As in the proof of Proposition~\ref{prop:whfull}, with $g_\infty=K_\infty f$ outside the window,
\[
(K_\infty-\lambda)f=2Ps\,\mathbf 1_{[-a,a]}+\sum_{d\in D_a}c_d\sum_j\big(f_j(\cdot-d)+f_j(\cdot+d)\big)\mathbf 1_{(-a,a)}+g_\infty\mathbf 1_{|y|>a},
\]
and by Lemma~\ref{lem:pieces} every copy $f_j(\cdot\mp d)\mathbf 1_{(-a,a)}$ is either zero or the whole shifted piece, landing on some $I_{j'}$. All sources are in $L^1\cap L^2$ with $yh\in L^2$, so Proposition~\ref{prop:whexact} applies at the right edge with $\hat H_-$ the sources to the left of $a$ corrected at $\pm t_\lambda$: $\tilde F=P_-[\hat H_-/\sigma_{\infty,+}]/\sigma_{\infty,-}=\hat H_-/\tilde\sigma_\infty-P_+[\hat H_-/\sigma_{\infty,+}]/\sigma_{\infty,-}$, and on the window the second term is the transform of a smooth function (Lemma~\ref{lem:kernel}: $P_+[\cdot]$ is the transform of a function on $u>0$, and the kernel of $1/\sigma_{\infty,-}$ is smooth away from the origin). So on $(-a,a)$, $f=k*\hat h_-+(\text{smooth})$, where $\hat h_-$ is the source with its corrections, which are weighted primitives of the sources, $-ie^{\pm it_\lambda u}\int_{-\infty}^u h(u')e^{\mp it_\lambda u'}\,du'$ on $u<0$: one derivative smoother than $h$, with the same singular support, and a step at the edge. Since $k$ is smooth away from the origin, $k*g$ is smooth near a point where $g$ is smooth, for $g\in L^1$.

\emph{Regularity, (i).} Let $p\notin S$ and let $O(p)$ be the set of points visited by walks from $p$: finite by (F$_a$) and disjoint from $S$, since a walk from $p$ to $\pm a$ reversed is a walk from $\pm a$ to $p$. The sources near $q\in O(p)$ are the polar term, smooth inside the window, the copies landing on intervals adjacent to $q$, which are $c_d f(q\mp d+\cdot)$ with $q\mp d\in O(p)$ (a copy landing on an interval not adjacent to $q$ is supported away from $q$), the far force, and the corrections; so the vector $(f(q+\cdot))_{q\in O(p)}$ solves near $0$ the system $(K_\infty-\lambda)f(q+\cdot)=\sum_{q'\in O(p),\,|q-q'|\in D_a}c_{|q-q'|}(1+\mathcal M)f(q'+\cdot)+(\text{smooth})$, with $\mathcal M$ the primitive of the corrections, an operator of order $-1$. The commutator of $K_\infty$ with a cutoff has a bounded kernel ($J_\Gamma(s)(\chi(y)-\chi(y-s))$ is bounded near $s=0$), so the usual bootstrap, one derivative per step on a shrinking neighbourhood and for all $q\in O(p)$ at once, gives $f\in C^\infty$ near $p$.

\emph{The local relations, (ii).} For $p\in S^\circ$ the same list of sources near $p$ holds with $q\in S$: the copies landing on the two intervals adjacent to $p$ are $c_{|p-q|}f(q+\cdot)$ for $q=p\mp d\in S$ (one-sided when $q=\pm a$), the other copies, the polar term, the far force and the far corrections are smooth near $p$, and the corrections of the adjacent copies are $C^1$ at $p$ with the second derivative of the primitive of an echo, $O(|v|^{-1}L^{-3/2})$ before and $O(|v|^{-1}L^{-5/2})$ after the convolution with $k$, which gains a logarithm; this is \eqref{eq:localecho}. Transforming \eqref{eq:localecho} against $\chi$: $\chi\cdot(k*f(q+\cdot))$ has the transform $\Phi_q/\tilde\sigma_\infty$ up to the transform of a smooth compactly supported function (the error of the localization, $\chi(k*(\chi f(q+\cdot)))-k*(\chi f(q+\cdot))$, is smooth since $k$ is smooth away from the origin), and $\chi r_p$ has a transform $O(t^{-2}(\log t)^{-3/2})$; multiplying by $\tilde\sigma_\infty$ gives \eqref{eq:treesystem}, where $\Psi$ collects $\tilde\sigma_\infty\widehat{\chi r_p}$ and the rapidly decaying errors.

\emph{The edge, (iii).} The sources landing on $I_N=(s_{N-1},a)$ are $c_d f_{j(d)}(\cdot-d)$ with $I_{j(d)}=I_N-d$, that is, $c_d\,f(a-d+u)$ for $u\in(s_{N-1}-a,0)$, for each $d\in D_a$; their transforms are $c_d\,P_-[\Phi_{a-d}]$ up to far sources (the cut at $s_{N-1}-a$, at distance $\delta_0$) and smooth ones. By \eqref{eq:treesystem}, wherever $\tilde\sigma_\infty(t)I-C$ is invertible, $\sum_dc_d\Phi_{a-d}=b^{T}\Phi_{S^\circ}=R\,\Phi_a+\bar R\,\Phi_{-a}+b^T(\tilde\sigma_\infty I-C)^{-1}\Psi$. Here $\Phi_a=\tilde F+(\text{far source})$ by the choice of $\chi$, and $P_-[R\tilde F]=R\tilde F-P_+[R\tilde F]$; moving $R\tilde F$ to the left of $\tilde\sigma_\infty\tilde F=\hat H_-+\hat G_+$ gives $\sigma_{\mathrm{eff}}\tilde F=\hat H'_-+\hat G'_+$ with $\hat H'_-=\hat H_--R\tilde F+P_-[R\,\Phi_a+\bar R\,\Phi_{-a}+b^T(\tilde\sigma_\infty I-C)^{-1}\Psi]-(\text{the copies on }I_N)$ and $\hat G'_+=\hat G_++P_+[R\tilde F]$. The identity \eqref{eq:sigmatree} is the Schur complement of the entry $(a,a)$ of $\tilde\sigma_\infty I-C_a$, and since $\tilde\sigma_\infty$ is real-analytic with $\tilde\sigma_\infty\to\infty$, its level sets at the eigenvalues of $C$ and $C_a$ are finite. At a real zero $\pm t_j$ of $\sigma_{\mathrm{eff}}$ the right-hand side vanishes, and the rational correction of Proposition~\ref{prop:whexact} applies; at a real pole $\pm s_k$ (an eigenvalue of $C$ reached by $\tilde\sigma_\infty$: a standing wave of the interior tree) both $\sigma_{\mathrm{eff}}\tilde F$ and $\hat H'_-$ have a simple pole, with equal residues, and dividing by the rational factor $(t^2+s_k^2)/(t^2-s_k^2)$ of $\tilde\sigma_{\mathrm{eff}}/\sigma_{\mathrm{eff}}$ removes them; the Liouville function of the proof of Proposition~\ref{prop:whexact} is then entire off $\pm s_k$ with at most simple poles there, hence a rational function of the form $\sum_kc_k^\pm/(z\mp s_k)$ by its growth, and the $c_k^\pm$ are fixed by the regularity of $\tilde F$ at $\pm s_k$; these terms are transforms of one-sided exponentials, smooth near the edge up to a step. Collecting, $\hat H''_-$ is $\hat H'_-$ with these rational corrections, and its sources are as stated: the polar step and the steps $c_d\,r_{a-d}(0^-)$ of the smooth parts of the copies, smooth parts, the corrections of order $t^{-2}(\log t)^{-3/2}$, the mirror chains, and the far sources. Lemma~\ref{lem:factor} applies to $\tilde\sigma_{\mathrm{eff}}$, which is smooth, positive, and $\tilde\sigma_\infty(1+O(\tilde\sigma_\infty^{-2}))$ at infinity with bounded log-derivative, and Lemma~\ref{lem:kernel} to its factors, since $\sigma_{\mathrm{eff}}$ is analytic in the strip of $\tilde\sigma_\infty$ with $R=O(1/\log|t|)$ there; the Liouville argument of Proposition~\ref{prop:whexact} then gives \eqref{eq:whtree}.
\end{proof}

\begin{corollary}[the edge, the echoes and the tail law at a support with a finite tree]\label{cor:tree}
Under the hypotheses of Proposition~\ref{prop:tree}, with $\rho=(\tilde\sigma_\infty I-C)^{-1}b$ (the echo amplitudes) and $E(t)=1+\sum_{p\in S^\circ}\rho_p(t)e^{-it(a-p)}$:
\begin{enumerate}
\item[(i)] the edge of the transform has the form of Corollary~\ref{cor:edgeform} with $\sigma_{\mathrm{eff}}$ in place of $\sigma$, $A(t)\to A_0$ purely imaginary;
\item[(ii)] $f$ satisfies the edge law with derivatives with the constants of Proposition~\ref{prop:profile}, $C=|A_0|$ and $\beta=-\gamma-\log(2\pi a)-\lambda$, and is smooth on the window except at the edges and at the points of $S^\circ$, where it has the cusps of \eqref{eq:localecho};
\item[(iii)] the echo form:
\[
S(t)=-\frac{|A_0|\,|E(t)|\cos\big(ta+\theta_{\mathrm{eff}}(t)+\arg E(t)-\kappa(t)/t\big)}{t\,\tilde\sigma_{\mathrm{eff}}(t)^{1/2}}+O\big(t^{-2}(\log t)^{-3/2}\big),
\]
with $\theta_{\mathrm{eff}}$ the Hilbert phase of $\tilde\sigma_{\mathrm{eff}}$ and $\kappa(t)=O(\log t)$ real; the chains of the tree are the terms of the Neumann series $\rho=\sum_{\ell\ge0}C^\ell b/\tilde\sigma_\infty^{\ell+1}$, convergent where $\tilde\sigma_\infty>\|C\|$, and $R$ is a finite continued fraction along a chain;
\item[(iv)] the sharp form is an identity of the tree: with $\sigma_a=\tilde\sigma_\infty-\sum_{d\in D_a}2c_d\cos(\tau d)$ and $t$ frozen in $\tilde\sigma_\infty$ and $\rho$,
\[
\big\langle\sigma_a(\tau)\,|E(\tau)|^2\big\rangle_\tau=\sigma_{\mathrm{eff}}(t),\qquad\big\langle\sigma_a(\tau)\,e^{2i\tau a}E(\tau)^2\big\rangle_\tau=-\bar R(t)
\]
(means over $\tau\in\R$); hence the local envelope of $t^2(\Psi-\lambda)|F(t)|^2$ over the oscillations is $2|A_0|^2\big(1-\bar R/\tilde\sigma_{\mathrm{eff}}+O(\theta_{\mathrm{eff}}^2)\big)(1+o(1))$, and $\bar R\equiv0$ when the trees of the two edges are disjoint;
\item[(v)] $\lambda'(a)=-2|A_0|^2$ at every point of differentiability, and under RH, $\lim_{T\to\infty}T\sum_{|\gamma|>T}|F(\gamma)|^2=-\lambda'(a)/\pi$: Conjecture~\ref{conj:tail} holds at $a$;
\item[(vi)] under RH, when the trees of the two edges are disjoint, the sampling constant of Corollary~\ref{cor:M} is
\[
M_\chi(T)-1=\frac{R+\tilde\sigma_\infty\,(|\rho|^2-1)}{\sigma_{\mathrm{eff}}}\Big|_{t=T}\big(1+o(1)\big)=\frac{2|b|^2}{\tilde\sigma_\infty(T)^2}\big(1+O(1/\tilde\sigma_\infty)\big),\qquad |\rho|^2:=1+\sum_p\rho_p^2 ,
\]
and $|b|^2=\sum_{\log m<2a}\Lambda(m)^2/m$.
\end{enumerate}
\end{corollary}
\begin{proof}
(i), (ii), (v) are the proofs of Corollary~\ref{cor:full}(i), (ii), (iv), (v) with $\sigma_{\mathrm{eff}}$ of \eqref{eq:sigmatree}: the near sources are steps and smooth parts, the far feedback is $-ir(0^+)+O(1/t)$, the corrections of order $t^{-2}(\log t)^{-3/2}$ contribute $O(t^{-1}(\log t)^{-3/2})$ to $A(t)$, inside $A_0+O(\log t/t)$; $\tilde\sigma_{\mathrm{eff}}^{-1/2}=\ell^{-1/2}(1+|b|^2/(2\ell^2)+O(\ell^{-3}))$ and $\theta_{\mathrm{eff}}=-\pi/(4\ell)+O(\ell^{-3})$, so the profile matches the model with the same $\beta$ up to a relative $|b|^2/(2\ell^2)$, inside the remainder class; the cusps at the finitely many points of $S^\circ$, at distance at least $\delta_0$ from the edges, have transforms $O(t^{-1}L^{-3/2})$ with the phases $e^{itp}$, their cross terms with the edges oscillate at the nonzero frequencies $a\mp p$, and Step~2 of Proposition~\ref{prop:tail} handles them as it handles $\cos(2ta)$. (iii) By \eqref{eq:treesystem}, $\Phi_{S^\circ}=\rho\,\Phi_a+\bar\rho\,\Phi_{-a}+O(t^{-2}(\log t)^{-3/2})$ with $\bar\rho=(\tilde\sigma_\infty I-C)^{-1}\bar b$, $\bar\rho_p=\rho_{-p}$ by the symmetry $S=-S$, and $F=\sum_{p\in S}e^{itp}\Phi_p$ up to the transform of a smooth function; so $F=e^{ita}\Phi_aE(t)+e^{-ita}\Phi_{-a}\overline{E(t)}+\dots=2i\,\mathrm{Im}\big(e^{ita}\Phi_aE\big)+\dots$, and $\Phi_a=\tilde F+(\text{far})=A(t)/(t\sigma_{\mathrm{eff},-})+\dots$ by (i). The Neumann series and the continued fraction are the expansion of the resolvent and the Schur complement of a path. (iv) Write $E=\sum_{p\in S^\circ\cup\{a\}}e_pe^{-i\tau(a-p)}$ with $e_a=1$, $e_p=\rho_p$. The mean of $|E|^2$ is $|\rho|^2$ and the mean of $2\cos(\tau d)|E|^2$ is $\sum_{|p-q|=d}e_pe_q$, so the mean of $\sigma_a|E|^2$ is $\tilde\sigma_\infty|\rho|^2-e^TC_ae=\tilde\sigma_\infty|\rho|^2-2b^T\rho-\rho^TC\rho$, and $\rho^TC\rho=\tilde\sigma_\infty(|\rho|^2-1)-b^T\rho$ by $(\tilde\sigma_\infty-C)\rho=b$; the sum is $\tilde\sigma_\infty-b^T\rho=\sigma_{\mathrm{eff}}$. For the mirror term, $e^{2i\tau a}E^2=\sum_{p,q}e_pe_qe^{i\tau(p+q)}$, whose mean against $\sigma_a$ is $\tilde\sigma_\infty\sum_{p\in S^\circ}\rho_p\rho_{-p}-\sum_pe_p(C_Se)_{-p}$, with $C_S$ the adjacency on $S$ and $e_{-a}=0$; by $(\tilde\sigma_\infty-C)\rho=b$, $(C_Se)_{-p}=\tilde\sigma_\infty\rho_{-p}$ for $p\in S^\circ$ and $(C_Se)_{-a}=\bar b^T\rho=\bar R$, which leaves $-\bar R$. With $|F|^2=2|\Phi_a|^2|E|^2-2\,\mathrm{Re}(e^{2ita}\Phi_a^2E^2)$ and $\Phi_a^2=-|A_0|^2e^{2i\theta_{\mathrm{eff}}-2i\kappa/t}/(t^2\tilde\sigma_{\mathrm{eff}})(1+o(1))$, the envelope follows; $\bar R\equiv0$ if no chain of $C$ joins the two trees. (vi) In \eqref{eq:defect}, $\frac1{2\pi}\int|F|^2\chi_T\Psi_\infty=(\tilde\sigma_\infty+\lambda)|\rho|^2I_T(1+o(1))$ and $2\sum_n\Lambda(n)n^{-1/2}\check h_T(\log n)=e^TC_ae\,I_T(1+o(1))$ with $I_T=\frac1{2\pi}\int2|\Phi_a|^2\chi_T$, the oscillating terms averaging out and the mirror cross terms having no mean when the trees are disjoint; so $M_\chi-1=e^TC_ae/(\tilde\sigma_\infty|\rho|^2-e^TC_ae)(1+o(1))$, and $e^TC_ae=R+\tilde\sigma_\infty(|\rho|^2-1)$ by the computation in (iv).
\end{proof}

\begin{computation}[the echo set]\label{comp:tree}
\texttt{rh\_echo\_tree.py}, log in \texttt{data/}. The echo set $S$ and the orbits of walks from $200$ interior points, with a cap of $5000$ points, for $0.35\le a\le0.84$:
\begin{center}\small
\begin{tabular}{@{}lllccccl@{}}
\toprule
$a$ & entries & $|S^\circ|$ & chain & orbit & $\|C\|$ & $\|C_a\|$ & trees of $\pm a$\\
\midrule
$0.35$--$0.53$ & $2$ & $2$ & $1$ & $2$ & $0$ & $0.490$ & disjoint\\
$0.55$--$0.68$ & $2,3$ & $6$ & $2$ & $4$ & $0.490$ & $0.901$ & disjoint\\
$0.70$--$0.75$ & $2,3,4$ & $12$ & $3$ & $7$ & $0.901$ & $1.172$ & disjoint\\
$0.775$--$0.80$ & $2,3,4$ & $22$ & $6$ & $12$ & $1.172$ & $1.219$ & disjoint\\

$0.8422$ & $2,3,4,5$ & $3434$ & $482$ & & & & \\

\noindent (chain: the longest shortest chain from the edge; orbit: the largest orbit of a walk from an interior point.) The walks are confined up to $a_\infty=0.8430$ (between $0.84297$ and $0.84336$) and not beyond: with the entries $2,3,4,5$ in a window of length $2a>1.686$ the net moves $\log3-\log2$, $\log4-\log3$, $\log5-\log4$ can be composed indefinitely without leaving the window, and the echo set becomes dense. Below $a_\infty$ the tree is finite; its size grows from $2$ points for one entry, through $6$ (one echo of an echo, $a-\log3+\log2$) for $a_3<a<a_4$, to thousands near $a_\infty$. At every support of the table the trees of the two edges are disjoint, so $\bar R\equiv0$ and the sharp form of Corollary~\ref{cor:tree}(iv) holds without the mirror correction. The resonances: $\min\tilde\sigma_\infty=1.003$ (at $t=6.6$, just above $t_\lambda=6.29$, for $\lambda\approx0$), so $\sigma_{\mathrm{eff}}$ has no real zeros or poles for $a<a_4$ ($\|C_a\|=0.90$), real zeros but no poles for $0.70<a<0.77$, and both beyond; all of them lie below the horizon ($\tilde\sigma_\infty(\Tst)=2a-\lambda$). Where the walks deconfine, the symbol of the window is negative on sets reaching far above the horizon (the last sign change at $33\,\Tst$ for $a=1.0$ and beyond $150\,\Tst$ for $a=1.25$ in Computation~\ref{comp:whphase}): the infinite tree has a band, and the finite tree does not.
\end{computation}

\begin{computation}[the tree at $a=0.6$: the second echo]\label{comp:tree06}
Edge FEM at $a=0.6$ with the entries $2$ and $3$ ($486$ nodes, the mesh of Computation~\ref{comp:hadamard}, $\lambda=5.9459\times10^{-7}$; $\lambda_{64}=5.9583\times10^{-7}$ and $\lambda_{64}'=-4.1545\times10^{-5}$ from the dilation engine; $\Tst=20.86$). The tree: $S^\circ=\{\pm0.0931,\ \pm0.1945,\ \pm0.4986\}$, the right edge's chain $a\to a-\log3=-0.4986\to a-\log3+\log2=0.1945$ and its branch $a\to a-\log2=-0.0931$, $b=(c_3,c_2,0)$ on $(-0.4986,-0.0931,0.1945)$, $C$ with the single link $c_2$ between $-0.4986$ and $0.1945$; so $R=c_2^2/\tilde\sigma_\infty+c_3^2/(\tilde\sigma_\infty-c_2^2/\tilde\sigma_\infty)$ and the second echo has the amplitude $\rho_{0.1945}=c_2c_3/(\tilde\sigma_\infty^2-c_2^2)$: $0.040$, $0.026$, $0.018$ at $5,10,20\,\Tst$. (i) \emph{The constants.} With nothing fitted, $f/[(-\lambda'/2)^{1/2}(L+\beta)^{-1/2}]$, $\beta=-1.904$, is $1.0003$--$1.003$ on $10^{-12}<\delta<10^{-6}$ and $1.013$ at $10^{-4}$: the two entries change neither constant. (ii) \emph{The nulls and the pointwise form}, $463$ nulls on $3.5$--$120\,\Tst$: with the tree's phase $\theta_{\mathrm{eff}}+\arg E$ the residual $r+\vartheta-\kappa/t$ has rms $0.0114$ on $5$--$80\,\Tst$ ($300$ nulls), against $0.0162$ for the first generation alone ($\rho_p=b_p/\tilde\sigma_\infty$, $\sigma_{\mathrm{eff}}=\tilde\sigma_\infty-|b|^2/\tilde\sigma_\infty$), $0.036$ for the window's symbol and $0.41$ for no shift; at $5,7,10,15,20,30\,\Tst$ the tree leaves $-0.002,-0.006,-0.006,-0.004,-0.003,-0.0002$ where the first generation leaves $-0.039,+0.019,+0.010,+0.017,-0.011,+0.008$. The relative $L^2$ distance of $S$ from the tree form over one period, $|A|=(-\lambda'/2)^{1/2}$, is $0.038$, $0.0088$, $0.0034$ at $5,10,20\,\Tst$, against $0.053$, $0.028$, $0.020$ for the first generation and $0.20$, $0.097$, $0.055$ for the window's symbol: the first generation's error is the missing second echo, $0.04$, $0.026$, $0.018$. Beyond $35\,\Tst$ the interior mesh ($1/\text{spacing}\approx34\,\Tst$) limits $S$ and all forms degrade alike ($0.011$ at $50\,\Tst$, $0.11$ at $100$). (iii) \emph{The sampling defect.} The identity \eqref{eq:defect} holds at every height to $10^{-5}$ of the zero sum. With $\lambda=6\times10^{-7}$ the quartic cutoff is too soft, its weight $(t/T)^4$ below the horizon letting through the density integral there (which is $10^{-3}$ at $\kappa=1$ against a zero sum of $6\times10^{-7}$), so the cutoff $1-e^{-(t/T)^8}$ is used (Proposition~\ref{prop:defect} with $m=4$). Its $M_\chi$ against the tree's prediction of Corollary~\ref{cor:tree}(vi) averaged over the tail, $1+\langle e^TC_ae/\tilde\sigma_\infty\rangle_T/\langle\sigma_{\mathrm{eff}}/\tilde\sigma_\infty\rangle_T$, is $1.110/1.106$, $1.076/1.079$, $1.050/1.057$ at $\kappa=6,10,20$ (first order $2|b|^2\langle\tilde\sigma_\infty^{-2}\rangle_T$: $1.098$, $1.075$, $1.054$; the value at $T$ instead of the average, $1.160$, $1.113$, $1.077$, is off by a factor $0.7$ in $M_\chi-1$); at $\kappa=e$ the cutoff still lets through the density integral below the horizon, and $M_\chi=1.68$ against $1.19$. The sharp constant $M(\kappa)$ of Computation~\ref{comp:analytic} is $1.14$, $1.12$, $1.064$, $1.049$ at $\kappa=e,6,10,20$.
\end{computation}


\subsection{Beyond the finite tree: the window's symbol and the band}\label{sec:band}

Above $a_\infty$ the echo set is dense and the tree has no finite matrix. The representation that survives is the one with the symbol of the window, which has only two sources, the polar term and the force outside the far edge; the copies are inside the symbol. It is exact at every support, and what it does not give is the asymptotics of its edge amplitude below the band of the echo lattice, where the symbol changes sign.

\begin{proposition}[the window's symbol at every support]\label{prop:window}
Assume (H$_\infty$). Let $f$ be the minimizer at a support $a$ that is not an entry, $\sigma_a=\Psi_a-\lambda$ with $\Psi_a=\Psi_\infty-\sum_{d\in D_a}2c_d\cos(td)$ the symbol of the window, $\pm t_1,\dots,\pm t_N$ its real zeros, finitely many, all below the last sign change $t_s$, $\tilde\sigma_a=\sigma_a\prod_j(t^2+t_j^2)/(t^2-t_j^2)>0$, and $g_{\mathrm{out}}=K_af$ outside the window, $K_a$ the operator with symbol $\Psi_a$. Then:
\begin{enumerate}
\item[(i)] Lemma~\ref{lem:factor} holds for $\tilde\sigma_a$ with the bounds on $\theta$ and $\theta'$ replaced by $O(\log\log(e+|t|))$: $\sigma_{a,\pm}=\tilde\sigma_a^{1/2}e^{\pm i\theta_a}$ are the boundary values of the outer functions, $c\le|\sigma_{a,\pm}(z)|\le C(\log(e+|z|))^{1/2}$, and $1/\sigma_{a,\pm}$ are bounded on the line with derivative $O(\log\log(e+|t|))$.
\item[(ii)] With $\tilde H$ the transform of $e^{-ita}\big(2Ps\mathbf 1_{[-a,a]}+g_{\mathrm{out}}\mathbf 1_{y<-a}\big)$ and $\hat H_-$ its correction at the $\pm t_j$ as in Proposition~\ref{prop:whexact},
\begin{equation}\label{eq:whwindow}
\tilde F=\frac{P_-\big[\hat H_-/\sigma_{a,+}\big]}{\sigma_{a,-}}\qquad\text{on }\R ,
\end{equation}
and, with $\Phi_-$ as in Corollary~\ref{cor:edgeform} built from $g_{\mathrm{out}}$, $\tilde F=A_a(t)/(t\sigma_{a,-})+e^{-2ita}\Phi_-/\tilde\sigma_a$ with $A_a$ given by the formula of \eqref{eq:edgeform} with $\sigma_{a,\pm}$, $A_a(-t)=-\overline{A_a(t)}$.
\item[(iii)] At a support with a finite tree, \eqref{eq:whwindow} and \eqref{eq:whtree} represent the same $\tilde F$: the echoes of the right edge inside the window are the first terms of the expansion of $1/\sigma_{a,-}$ in the lattice frequencies, and the terms of that expansion which would place an echo beyond the far edge are cancelled by the far source.
\end{enumerate}
\end{proposition}
\begin{proof}
$\sigma_a$ is real-analytic, even, and tends to $+\infty$, so its real zeros are finitely many and lie below $t_s$; $\tilde\sigma_a$ is even, positive, $C^2$, bounded below, and $O(\log t)$. (i) In the proof of Lemma~\ref{lem:factor} the hypothesis $(\log\tilde\sigma)'\in L^2$ serves only to bound $\theta'=\tfrac12\mathcal H[(\log\tilde\sigma)']$. Write $\log\tilde\sigma_a=\log\tilde\sigma_\infty+u$ with $\tilde\sigma_\infty$ the archimedean symbol with its real zero divided out; $u$ is $C^2$ with $u',u''$ bounded ($\tilde\sigma_a\ge c_1$ and $\sigma_a'$, $\sigma_a''$ are bounded), and $u=O(\log\log(e+|t|))$ since $c_1\le\tilde\sigma_a\le C\tilde\sigma_\infty$. The Hilbert transform of $u'$ is bounded on $|\tau-t|<1$ by $\sup|u''|$, and on $|\tau-t|>1$ an integration by parts gives $u(t\pm1)+\int_{|\tau-t|>1}u(\tau)(t-\tau)^{-2}d\tau=O(\log\log(e+|t|))$; the same integration by parts in \eqref{eq:theta} bounds $\mathcal H[u]$. The modulus bounds of the lemma use only $\log\tilde\sigma_a$, and hold. (ii) On the window $K_af=\lambda f+2Ps$, since the copies $f(\cdot\mp\log m)$ with $\log m>2a$ vanish there; so on the line $K_af=\lambda f\mathbf 1_{[-a,a]}+2Ps\mathbf 1_{[-a,a]}+g_{\mathrm{out}}\mathbf 1_{|y|>a}$, where $g_{\mathrm{out}}=K_\infty f+\sum_{d\in D_a}c_d(\text{copies landing outside})$ is in $L^1\cap L^2$ with $yg_{\mathrm{out}}\in L^2$, and $\sigma_a\tilde F=\tilde H+\tilde G_+$. The proof of Proposition~\ref{prop:whexact} applies with $\sigma_{a,\pm}$: the rational correction at the $N$ pairs of zeros, the $H^1$ property of $\hat H_-/\sigma_{a,+}$ (from (i)), and the Liouville argument with the bounds of (i). The edge form is \eqref{eq:edgeform} read with $\sigma_{a,\pm}$, and the symmetry is as before. (iii) Both representations are exact for the same function, and $\tilde F$ is supported in $(-2a,0)$; for one entry, with $\tilde\sigma_\infty$ frozen at $s$ the factorization $s-2c\cos\tau=\sigma_{\mathrm{fp}}(1-\rho_{\mathrm{fp}}e^{i\tau})(1-\rho_{\mathrm{fp}}e^{-i\tau})$, $\sigma_{\mathrm{fp}}+c^2/\sigma_{\mathrm{fp}}=s$, $\rho_{\mathrm{fp}}=c/\sigma_{\mathrm{fp}}$, is exact for $s\ge2c$, and $1/\sigma_{a,-}=\sigma_{\mathrm{fp}}^{-1/2}\sum_k\rho_{\mathrm{fp}}^ke^{-ik\tau}$ is the infinite chain of echoes at $a-kd$, of which the window holds $k=0,1$.
\end{proof}

What (ii) does not give is $A_a(t)\to A_0$. In the proof of Corollary~\ref{cor:edgeform} the far feedback $-tP_+[e^{-2ita}\Phi_-/\sigma_+]$ was $-ir(0^+)+O(1/t)$ because the kernel of $1/\sigma_+$ is smooth beyond the origin (Lemma~\ref{lem:kernel}); the kernel of $1/\sigma_{a,+}$ is singular at the points $\sum_dn_dd$ of the lattice, because $\log\sigma_{a,+}$ contains all the harmonics of the entries, and the feedback of the far edge through it is the family of its echoes landing beyond the right edge, an almost-periodic function of $t$ whose amplitudes decay only like inverse powers of $\log t$. In the finite tree this feedback is $\bar R\,\Phi_{-a}$, and it is absent when the trees of the two edges are disjoint; in the window's symbol it is always present, together with the rational corrections of the $N$ zeros, which are $O(\sum_jt_j^2/t^2)$ and, with $N$ zeros up to $t_s$, are not small below $t\approx N^{1/2}t_s$. So \eqref{eq:whwindow} is exact everywhere and asymptotic only far above the last sign change. Section~\ref{sec:everysupport} proves that $A_a(t)\to A_0$ at every support that is not an entry, the mirror chains being the leading correction, and derives the edge law and the boundary law from it; the finite tree is what makes the asymptotics start at the horizon.

\begin{lemma}[the periodic limit of the window's symbol]\label{lem:periodic}
Let $\nu$ be the law of
\[
X=\sum_{p\le e^{2a}}\ \sum_{k\ge1,\ p^k<e^{2a}}2c_{p^k}\cos(k\tau_p)
\]
with independent uniform phases $\tau_p$, one per prime, so that $\operatorname{supp}\nu=[-X_{\max},X_{\max}]$ with $X_{\max}=\sum_{d\in D_a}2c_d$. For every continuous $\varphi$ and every $s$,
\[
\lim_{T\to\infty}\frac1T\int_0^T\varphi\Big(s-\sum_{d\in D_a}2c_d\cos(td)\Big)dt=\int\varphi(s-x)\,d\nu(x);
\]
in particular the mean of $\log|s-\sum_d2c_d\cos(td)|$ over $t$ is the logarithmic potential $U_\nu(s)=\int\log|s-x|\,d\nu(x)$, a continuous function of $s$. Put $G(s)=e^{U_\nu(s)}$, the geometric mean of the window's symbol at the frozen height $s$. Then
\[
G(s)=s-\sum_{d\in D_a}\frac{c_d^2}{s}+O(s^{-3})\quad(s\to\infty),
\]
equal to the effective symbol of the finite tree to this order; and for one entry
\[
G(s)=\tfrac12\big(s+\sqrt{s^2-4c^2}\big)\ \ (s\ge2c),\qquad G(s)=c\ \ (|s|\le2c),
\]
the former being the fixed point of the infinite continued fraction $\sigma\mapsto s-c^2/\sigma$.
\end{lemma}
\begin{proof}
The numbers $\log p$ for distinct primes are linearly independent over $\mathbb Q$, so $(t\log p\bmod2\pi)_p$ is equidistributed on the torus (Weyl), the prime powers riding on the phases of their primes; this gives the limit. The law of $\sum_k2c_{p^k}\cos(k\tau_p)$ for one prime is the push-forward of $d\tau/2\pi$ by a trigonometric polynomial, absolutely continuous with inverse-square-root singularities, and $\nu$ is the convolution of these laws over the primes; so $\log|\cdot|$ is locally integrable against $\nu$ and $U_\nu$ is continuous. For $s>X_{\max}$, $U_\nu(s)=\log s+\mathbb E\log(1-X/s)=\log s-\mathbb E[X^2]/(2s^2)+O(s^{-3})$ with $\mathbb E X=0$ and $\mathbb E X^2=\sum_d2c_d^2$. For one entry $\nu$ is the arcsine law on $[-2c,2c]$, whose logarithmic potential is $\log\tfrac12(|s|+\sqrt{s^2-4c^2})$ outside and $\log c$ inside the support.
\end{proof}

The band of the echo lattice is $\{t:\ \tilde\sigma_\infty(t)<X_{\max}\}$, the heights at which the periodic limit of the window's symbol changes sign: up to $47\,\Tst$ at $a=1$, $412\,\Tst$ at $a=1.25$ and $2\times10^4\,\Tst$ at $a=1.5$, while the actual last sign changes are at $33$, $259$ and $397\,\Tst$ (Computation~\ref{comp:band}). Below $a_\infty$ the band is empty or lies below the horizon ($X_{\max}=2.25$ at $a=0.6$, $\tilde\sigma_\infty(\Tst)=2a$), which is the other face of the confinement: a finite tree has resonances, not a band. In the band the geometric mean of the symbol is $G(\tilde\sigma_\infty)$, finite and smooth, so the outer factor of the window's symbol has modulus $G^{1/2}$ on average, and $|F|^2$ cannot follow $1/|\sigma_a|$ where $\sigma_a<0$ without the envelope of $t^2\sigma_a|F|^2$ losing twice the measure of the negative set. Whether the minimizer loses it, or hides from the negative set as it hides from the zeros, cannot be read from the $K$-mode minimizers of Computation~\ref{comp:tail}, the only ones computed in the band, which above a few horizons carry the edge alone (Computation~\ref{comp:mirror}); what Computation~\ref{comp:band} measures on them is the envelope, which they carry correctly, and Proposition~\ref{prop:lattice} answers the pointwise question above the band top.

\begin{computation}[the sharp form in the band]\label{comp:band}
\texttt{rh\_band.py}, logs \texttt{band\_a*.log} in \texttt{data/}. On the $K$-mode minimizers of Computation~\ref{comp:tail} at $a=1.0$, $1.25$, $1.5$ ($K=80$, $140$, $230$; $\lambda=1.5\times10^{-26}$, $3.4\times10^{-51}$, $9.2\times10^{-93}$; $\lambda'$ from the dilation engine; $F$ in closed form through spherical Bessel functions at $80$ digits, reproducing the zero sums of Computation~\ref{comp:tail} to all digits), the local envelope of $t^2(\Psi-\lambda)|F|^2$ over the window of width $4\Tst$ centred at $\kappa\Tst$, divided by $-\lambda'$; the fraction of the window on which the window's symbol $\sigma_a$ is negative; and the part of the envelope carried by the negative set:
\begin{center}\footnotesize
\begin{tabular}{@{}lccc|ccc|ccc@{}}
\toprule
& \multicolumn{3}{c|}{$a=1.0$} & \multicolumn{3}{c|}{$a=1.25$} & \multicolumn{3}{c}{$a=1.5$}\\
$\kappa$ & envelope & $\sigma_a<0$ & on it & envelope & $\sigma_a<0$ & on it & envelope & $\sigma_a<0$ & on it\\
\midrule
$3$ & $1.708$ & $0.049$ & $-0.172$ & $2.151$ & $0.060$ & $-0.217$ & $3.156$ & $0.084$ & $-1.40$\\
$5$ & $1.071$ & $0.026$ & $-0.018$ & $1.216$ & $0.045$ & $-0.016$ & $1.084$ & $0.061$ & $-0.025$\\
$7$ & $1.046$ & $0.019$ & $-0.004$ & $1.049$ & $0.031$ & $-0.008$ & $1.027$ & $0.048$ & $-0.015$\\
$10$ & $1.001$ & $0.011$ & $-0.000$ & $1.012$ & $0.019$ & $-0.004$ & $1.010$ & $0.034$ & $-0.009$\\
$15$ & $0.987$ & $0.008$ & $-0.001$ & $1.013$ & $0.012$ & $-0.001$ & $0.998$ & $0.026$ & $-0.005$\\
$20$ & $0.968$ & $0.005$ & $-0.000$ & $0.996$ & $0.010$ & $-0.001$ & $0.997$ & $0.018$ & $-0.003$\\
$30$ & $0.989$ & $0$ & $0$ & $0.997$ & $0.007$ & $-0.000$ & $0.976$ & $0.012$ & $-0.002$\\
$50$ & $1.041$ & $0$ & $0$ & $0.990$ & $0.003$ & $-0.000$ & $0.994$ & $0.008$ & $-0.001$\\
\bottomrule
\end{tabular}
\end{center}
From five horizons on the envelope is $-\lambda'$ within $2$--$8\%$, and within $1$--$3\%$ from seven, at all three supports, inside the region where the symbol of the window changes sign (its last sign change is at $33$, $259$ and $397\,\Tst$). The negative set occupies $2.6$--$6\%$ of the heights at $5\,\Tst$ and carries $-0.02$ of the envelope, and the correlation coefficient of $t^2|F|^2$ with $1/|\sigma_a|$ over the window is below $0.04$ in modulus from $5\,\Tst$ on; but these are properties of the polynomial, not of the minimizer. Above $(2K-1)/a$, which is $3.4$, $2.9$, $2.4\,\Tst$ here, the $K$-mode transform is the edge's alone (Computation~\ref{comp:mirror}), and it cannot show the echo structure that the minimizer has (Corollary~\ref{cor:tree}(iii), and the FEM of Computation~\ref{comp:tree06}). What the polynomial carries correctly is the envelope, a mean, to the few per cent of $\sigma_{\mathrm{eff}}/\tilde\sigma_\infty-1$. The pointwise statement that holds for the minimizer is Proposition~\ref{prop:lattice}: above the band top $|F|^2\propto1/\sigma_a$ to first order in $c_d/s$, the first-generation echoes being the first terms of the expansion of $1/\sigma_{a,-}$, while inside the band the law is carried by the mean (Corollary~\ref{cor:tree}(iv)). The envelope against the archimedean symbol alone, $\langle t^2(\Psi_\infty-\lambda)|F|^2\rangle/(-\lambda')$, is $1.24$, $1.35$, $1.18$ at $5\,\Tst$ and $1.05$, $1.02$, $1.01$ at $10\,\Tst$: the prime sum at the entries, the sampling defect of Proposition~\ref{prop:defect}, is $15$--$30\%$ of the tail at five horizons and a few per cent at ten. Below five horizons the windows reach the mass of the floor (medians $2.4$--$2.7\,\Tst$ in Computation~\ref{comp:tail}) and the envelope is not a tail quantity. The $K$-mode minimizers resolve the edge to $\delta\approx e^{-2}a/K^2$, heights of order $10^3\,\Tst$, and their tails were validated on the zeros in Computation~\ref{comp:tail}.
\end{computation}

The band that matters is not the periodic limit's. The walks are confined to the window even when the echo set is dense: from a point $p$ only the steps $\pm d$ with $p\pm d\in[-a,a]$ are allowed, and from most points these are a small fraction of the $2|D_a|$ steps of the lattice. The echo graph is the quasi-periodic structure cut out of $\mathbb Z^{\pi(e^{2a})}$ by the window, and its adjacency is a far smaller operator than the periodic limit's.

\begin{proposition}[the confined lattice]\label{prop:confined}
Let $a$ be any support that is not an entry, $S$ its echo set, finite or not, and $C$ the weighted adjacency on $S^\circ$, $C_{pq}=c_{|p-q|}$ for $|p-q|\in D_a$. Let $D_{\max}=\max_{p\in S^\circ}\sum_{q\sim p}C_{pq}$ be the largest weighted degree. Then $C$ is a bounded self-adjoint operator on $\ell^2(S^\circ)$ with
\[
\rho(C)=\|C\|\le D_{\max}\le\sum_{d\in D_a,\ d\le a}2c_d+\sum_{d\in D_a,\ a<d<2a}c_d<X_{\max}=\sum_{d\in D_a}2c_d ,
\]
and $\rho(C)$ is the supremum of the top eigenvalues of the adjacencies of the finite subgraphs. In the other direction, let $\Sigma$ be the full echo lattice of the window, the set of all points $\pm a+\sum_pm_p\log p\in[-a,a]$ ($m_p\in\mathbb Z$, $p$ prime, $p<e^{2a}$), of which $S$ is the component of $\pm a$ under the allowed steps, with the same adjacency; then
\[
\rho(C_\Sigma)\ \ge\ \bar\rho(a):=\sum_{d\in D_a}2c_d\Big(1-\frac d{2a}\Big),\qquad \bar\rho(a)=\frac{4e^a-4a-4}{a}\,(1+o(1))\quad(a\to\infty),
\]
the mean weighted degree of the lattice. The formal echo expansion of Corollary~\ref{cor:tree}(iii), $\rho=\sum_{\ell\ge0}C^\ell b/\tilde\sigma_\infty^{\ell+1}$, converges in $\ell^2(S^\circ)$ at every height where $\tilde\sigma_\infty(t)>\rho(C)$, and the return $R=b^T(\tilde\sigma_\infty-C)^{-1}b$ and the effective symbol $\sigma_{\mathrm{eff}}=\tilde\sigma_\infty-R$ are real-analytic functions of $t$ there, with $\sigma_{\mathrm{eff}}=\tilde\sigma_\infty-|b|^2/\tilde\sigma_\infty+O(\tilde\sigma_\infty^{-2})$. The \emph{band of the confined lattice} is $\{t:\tilde\sigma_\infty(t)\le\rho(C)\}$; it ends at $t_{\mathrm{band}}=2\pi e^{\rho(C)+\lambda}(1+o(1))$.
\end{proposition}
\begin{proof}
A symmetric matrix with non-negative entries is bounded by its largest row sum (Schur's test), which is $D_{\max}$. A step $\pm d$ from $p$ counts in the degree of $p$ only if $p\pm d\in[-a,a]$; both directions are possible only if $d\le a-|p|\le a$, so an entry $d>a$ contributes at most $c_d$ and an entry $d\le a$ at most $2c_d$, which is the middle bound, and it is strictly below $X_{\max}$ because some entry lies in $(a,2a)$ at every support $a\ge a_2$. For a bounded self-adjoint operator with non-negative entries the norm is the supremum of $\langle v,Cv\rangle$ over finitely supported unit vectors, which is the supremum of the top eigenvalues of the finite truncations, increasing with the truncation. For the lower bound take the points of $\Sigma$ with exponent vectors in the cube $|m_p|\le L$; by Weyl's equidistribution their projections $\pm a+\sum_pm_p\log p$ are uniformly distributed modulo any interval, so a fixed fraction of the $(2L+1)^{\pi}$ vectors ($\pi$ the number of primes) lands in the window with projections uniformly distributed in $[-a,a]$, and all but $O(L^{\pi-1})$ of their allowed steps stay in the cube; the Rayleigh quotient of the normalized indicator of this set is the mean over it of the weighted degree, and the weighted degree of a point at $p$ is $\sum_dc_d(\mathbf 1_{p+d\le a}+\mathbf 1_{p-d\ge-a})$, whose mean over $p$ uniform in $[-a,a]$ is $\sum_d2c_d(1-d/2a)$. The asymptotic form is the prime number theorem in the form $\sum_{\log m<X}\Lambda(m)m^{-1/2}(X-\log m)=4e^{X/2}-2X-4+o(e^{X/2})$. The Neumann series converges in operator norm for $\tilde\sigma_\infty>\|C\|$, and the resolvent is analytic in $\tilde\sigma_\infty$ there, hence in $t$, with the expansion of its first terms.
\end{proof}

What the proposition does not do is extend Proposition~\ref{prop:tree} to a dense echo set: the local relations \eqref{eq:localecho} were proved with the other singular points at a positive distance, and the representation with the effective symbol needs them. Beyond $a_\infty$ the exact statement is Proposition~\ref{prop:window}, the echo expansion is formal, and the band of the confined lattice is where it ceases to converge. It is nevertheless the quantity to compare with the onset: the tail law of the zeros was measured from about five horizons at $a=1.0$, $1.25$, $1.5$ (Computation~\ref{comp:tail}), where the periodic band reaches $47$, $412$ and $2\times10^4$ horizons, and Computation~\ref{comp:confined} finds the confined band ending at one to three.

\begin{computation}[the band of the confined lattice]\label{comp:confined}
\texttt{rh\_lattice\_band.py}, logs \texttt{lattice\_band*.log} in \texttt{data/}. The echo set by breadth-first search from $\pm a$ in the exponent-vector representation, truncated to the first $N$ points by chain length, and the top eigenvalue $\rho_N$ of the truncated adjacency by power iteration; $D_{\max}$ over the points found; the mean degree $\bar\rho(a)$ of Proposition~\ref{prop:confined}; and the band tops in units of the horizon, $t_{\mathrm{band}}/\Tst=e^{\rho-2a}$:

The chains reached by $40000$ points are $13$ links long at $a=1.25$ and $7$ at $a=1.5$, where $\rho_N$ is still rising; the mean degree $\bar\rho$ is a lower bound for the lattice, which the truncations approach from below at $a=1.5$ and exceed by $10\%$ at $a\le1.25$, so $\rho(C)$ is within a few per cent of $\bar\rho$ at these supports: the confined lattice is nearly regular. Its spectral radius is a third of the periodic band top and two thirds of the largest degree. The band of the confined lattice ends at $0.9$, $1.5$ and about $3.0$ horizons at $a=1.0$, $1.25$, $1.5$ (the last from $\bar\rho$), against the periodic band top at $47$, $412$ and $2\times10^4$ horizons and the last sign change of the window's symbol at $33$, $259$ and $397$. The onset of the tail law of the zeros is five horizons at the three supports (Computation~\ref{comp:tail}: $T\vartheta/T_{\mathrm{eff}}=1.03$, $1.04$, $1.015$ at $5\,\Tst$) and of the sharp form five to seven (Computation~\ref{comp:band}): two to five times the confined band top, as it is a few horizons above the horizon for the finite trees, whose band lies below it.

\emph{The lattice's band against the edge's.} The lower bound $\bar\rho(a)\sim4e^a/a$ grows exponentially while $\tilde\sigma_\infty(\kappa\Tst)=2a+\log\kappa$ grows linearly, so the band of the confined lattice outruns the horizon: $e^{\bar\rho(a)-2a}$ is $3.0$, $34$ and $2041$ at $a=1.5$, $2$ and $2.5$. If the onset of the sharp form followed the spectral radius, the effective height of the zeros would outrun the horizon from $a\approx2$ on and the route of Proposition~\ref{prop:sharp} to Conjecture~\ref{conj:A} would need $\kappa$ growing like $e^{\bar\rho(a)-2a}$. It does not follow it: the edge is coupled to the lattice through $b$ alone, and the spectral measure of $C$ at $b$ sits at the bottom of the spectrum (Proposition~\ref{prop:spectral}, Computation~\ref{comp:spectral}), with its upper tail ending within a few horizons at every support up to $a=2.5$. The spectral radius is the band of the worst coupling, not of the edge's. The decisive measurement remains a minimizer at $a=2$ ($\Tst=343$, about $500$ modes at $1500$ bits), where the edge's band predicts the onset of the law at seven to ten horizons rather than five.
\end{computation}


The edge does not see the whole lattice. It is coupled to it through $b$ alone, the first-generation echoes, and what enters the effective symbol is the return $R=b^T(\tilde\sigma_\infty-C)^{-1}b$: a function of the spectral measure of $C$ at $b$, which may have little weight near the top of the spectrum.

\begin{proposition}[the band the edge sees]\label{prop:spectral}
Let $C$ be the adjacency of the confined lattice on $\ell^2(S^\circ)$ and $\mu_b$ its spectral measure at $b$, $\int\varphi\,d\mu_b=\langle b,\varphi(C)b\rangle$, a positive measure of mass $|b|^2$ supported in $[-\rho(C),\rho(C)]$. Then for real $s$ outside $\operatorname{supp}\mu_b$,
\[
R(s)=\int\frac{d\mu_b(\nu)}{s-\nu},\qquad \sigma_{\mathrm{eff}}(s)=s-R(s),
\]
$R$ is real-analytic and decreasing on $(\sup\operatorname{supp}\mu_b,\infty)$ with $R(s)=|b|^2/s+\langle b,Cb\rangle/s^2+O(s^{-3})$, and $\sigma_{\mathrm{eff}}$ is increasing there. For $s$ inside the support, $R(s\pm i0)=\mathrm{p.v.}\!\int d\mu_b(\nu)/(s-\nu)\mp i\pi\mu_b'(s)$ wherever $\mu_b$ has a density: the effective symbol with limiting absorption is complex, the imaginary part being the rate at which the edge radiates into the lattice. The moments $\int\nu^kd\mu_b=\langle b,C^kb\rangle$ count the weighted closed walks of length $k+2$ through the edge, so the truncation of the lattice to the points within $g$ steps of the edge reproduces the moments of $\mu_b$ of order up to $2g-2$ exactly. The band the edge sees is $\{t:\tilde\sigma_\infty(t)\in\operatorname{supp}\mu_b\}$, contained in the band of the confined lattice; it reaches the top of its own component's band (Proposition~\ref{prop:edgeband}), and what ends within a few horizons is its mass, bounded by the moments.
\end{proposition}
\begin{proof}
The spectral theorem for the bounded self-adjoint $C$, the resolvent identity $\langle b,(s-C)^{-1}b\rangle=\int(s-\nu)^{-1}d\mu_b$ for $s\notin\sigma(C)$, and the Sokhotski--Plemelj formula for the boundary values of a Stieltjes transform. A walk of length $k$ from a first-generation point to a first-generation point, weighted by its steps and by $b$ at both ends, is a closed walk of length $k+2$ through the edge, and all such walks of length $k\le2g-2$ stay within $g$ steps of the edge.
\end{proof}

\begin{computation}[the spectral measure at the edge]\label{comp:spectral}
\texttt{rh\_spectral\_measure.py}, logs in \texttt{data/}. The spectral measure $\mu_b$ of the confined lattice at the edge's coupling, by $80$ Lanczos steps from $b$ on the lattice truncated to $150000$ points (the moments of $\mu_b$ are exact to order $2g-2$, $g$ the depth reached: $58$, $18$, $9$, $6$, $5$, $4$, $4$ links at $a=1.0$, $1.25$, $1.5$, $1.75$, $2.0$, $2.25$, $2.5$), checked against the dense eigenproblem on the first $6000$ points. The weight of $\mu_b$ above $s=\tilde\sigma_\infty(\kappa\Tst)$ as a fraction of $|b|^2$, the heights at which it drops below $5\%$ and $1\%$, and $\sigma_{\mathrm{eff}}/s$ at five and ten horizons against the first order $1-|b|^2/s^2$:
