The measure the edge sees sits at the bottom of the spectrum: its mean $\langle b,Cb\rangle/|b|^2$ is $0.09$--$0.25$ (two first-generation points are adjacent only when their entries differ by an entry), its width $(\langle b,C^2b\rangle/|b|^2)^{1/2}$ is $1.0$--$2.3$, and its weight above the horizon's height $\tilde\sigma_\infty(\Tst)=2a$ is at most $6\%$ at every support. Its $1\%$ quantile, the band the edge sees, ends at $0.8$, $1.3$, $1.7$, $2.1$ horizons at $a=1.0$--$1.75$, where the depth is adequate, and at $4$--$5$ horizons at $a=2$--$2.5$, where the truncation reaches four or five links and the extreme tail is underestimated (the top node moves up with the depth: $4.34\to5.52$ at $a=2$ between $6000$ and $150000$ points, while the $5\%$ quantile moves from $1.4$ to $1.0\,\Tst$ and is stable); the top of the lattice's own spectrum is $\rho(C)\ge\bar\rho(a)$, at $34$ and $2000$ horizons for $a=2$ and $2.5$, and the edge's measure reaches the top of its own component's band (Proposition~\ref{prop:edgeband}), which the truncations put at $5.52$, $5.83$, $6.52$ for $a=2$, $2.25$, $2.5$, within $2\%$ of five horizons and still rising, with a weight they cannot resolve. What the exact moments bound is the mass: by Chebyshev with the highest exact even moment (orders $14$, $14$, $14$, $10$, $8$, $6$, $6$), the weight above five horizons is at most $2\times10^{-6}$, $10^{-4}$, $10^{-3}$, $0.8\%$, $2.2\%$, $4.7\%$, $5.6\%$ at $a=1.0$, $1.25$, $1.5$, $1.75$, $2.0$, $2.25$, $2.5$; the Lanczos measure's vanishing weight above $s$ at $a\ge1.5$ is the truncation's, its top node lying below $s$. At five horizons $\sigma_{\mathrm{eff}}/s$ is $0.84$, $0.82$, $0.76$, $0.70$ at $a=1.0$--$1.75$ (a few per cent below the first order, the longer chains) and $0.5$ at $a\ge2$, where $s$ is just above the resolved top of the support and the return is large: the onset there is predicted a little above five horizons, at seven to ten, where $\sigma_{\mathrm{eff}}/s$ is $0.72$--$0.77$. So the band that the sharp form has to clear is the one the edge sees, whose mass stays within a few horizons as far as $a=2.5$, and the prediction of Computation~\ref{comp:confined}, drawn from the spectral radius, is withdrawn: the spectral radius bounds the band of every coupling, and the edge's coupling sits far below it. What remains predicted for $a=2$ is a mild recession of the onset, from five horizons to about seven to ten, and the sampling defect $\sigma_{\mathrm{eff}}/s$ growing from $0.84$ to $0.7$ at ten horizons.
\end{computation}


\section{The reduction to one inequality}\label{sec:unconditional}

This section states the main theorem and names the gap. The theorems of Sections~\ref{sec:identity}, \ref{sec:hadamard} and~\ref{sec:everysupport} reduce the Hypothesis to one inequality about one function, every other link being a theorem under the boundedness hypothesis (H$_\infty$) at every support that is not an entry; the inequality is as hard as the Hypothesis, because it fails wherever the floor is negative, and the section gives the form in which it would have to be attacked, which is a statement about multiplicative relations among the integers below $e^{2a}$. It is where the paper rests.

\begin{theorem}[the reduction]\label{thm:reduction}
Assume (H$_\infty$). Let $a_Z=0.8$ be the support to which the floor is certified positive (Theorem~\ref{thm:zhu}), and suppose that for some $c>0$ and every support $a\ge a_Z$ that is not an entry,
\begin{equation}\label{eq:theinequality}
2|A_0(a)|^2\;\le\;c\,\Tst(a)\,\lambda(a),
\end{equation}
where $A_0(a)$ is the edge amplitude of the minimizer, the constant term of the amplitude $A(t)$ in the representation of its transform with the symbol of the window (Proposition~\ref{prop:window}). Then Conjecture~\ref{conj:A} holds with this $c$ and the Riemann Hypothesis follows (Proposition~\ref{prop:AimpliesRH}). The link is the boundary law $\lambda'(a)=-2|A_0(a)|^2$: a theorem at every support that is not an entry (Corollary~\ref{cor:tree}(v) below $a_\infty=0.843$, Theorem~\ref{thm:everysupport} in general); it is verified at every support computed (Computation~\ref{comp:hadamard}).
\end{theorem}
\begin{proof}
Corollary~\ref{cor:tree}(v) gives $\lambda'=-2|A_0|^2$ at every point of differentiability below $a_\infty$, with $A_0$ the amplitude of the representation with the effective symbol, which is the amplitude of the representation with the window's symbol by Proposition~\ref{prop:window}(iii); Theorem~\ref{thm:everysupport}(iii) gives it at every support that is not an entry. So \eqref{eq:theinequality} reads $\lambda'\ge-c\Tst\lambda$ almost everywhere on $[a_Z,\infty)$, which is Conjecture~\ref{conj:A} with $a_0=a_Z$, given the absolute continuity of $\lambda$ (Lemma~\ref{lem:exist}, Proposition~\ref{prop:lip}).
\end{proof}

Three remarks on the inequality.

\emph{It is one inequality, and everything else is proved.} The chain is: boundary law (a theorem at every support, Theorem~\ref{thm:everysupport}) $\Rightarrow$ \eqref{eq:theinequality} is the conjecture; the analytic tail law (Corollary~\ref{cor:tree}(v) and Theorem~\ref{thm:everysupport}(iii), a theorem at every support, no zeros involved) makes $2|A_0|^2$ the limit of $\pi T\cdot\frac1{2\pi}\int_{|t|>T}|F|^2(\Psi-\lambda)$, so \eqref{eq:theinequality} says that the energy of the minimizer against the shifted symbol above $\kappa$ horizons is at most $c\lambda/(\pi\kappa)$ once the law has set in; and the zeros enter nowhere. Under RH this energy is the zero sum above $\kappa\Tst$ (Proposition~\ref{prop:defect}), which is at most $\lambda$ because every term is a square: that is the content of Propositions~\ref{prop:sampling} and~\ref{prop:sharp}, and it is where RH is used. Without RH the zero sum is $\sum_\rho F(\gamma_\rho)F(-\gamma_\rho)$ with the pairs off the line contributing $2\,\mathrm{Re}\,F(\gamma)\overline{F(\bar\gamma)}$, of either sign, and the bound by $\lambda$ is lost.

\emph{It contains the Hypothesis.} If RH fails, the floor is negative from some support on (the odd-sector Weil criterion on windows, Section~\ref{sec:form}), while $2|A_0|^2\ge0$; so \eqref{eq:theinequality} fails there. A proof of \eqref{eq:theinequality} must therefore use the minimality of $f$ in a way that excludes a negative floor, and no argument that treats $\lambda$ as a given number can succeed. The quantity on the left is a boundary trace and the one on the right an energy; for the Dirichlet Laplacian the corresponding inequality, $u'(a)^2\le C\lambda$ for the ground state, is the Rellich--Pohozaev identity, and it is unconditional because the identity's remainder is a sum of squares. The dilation identity of Section~\ref{sec:identity} is the Pohozaev identity of the Weil form, $a\lambda'=D_\infty(f)+D_P(f)+2P^2+2PM$; what is needed is a sign for its remainder against $\Tst\lambda$, and the obstruction is the prime part $D_P$, whose sign is not fixed.

\emph{A bootstrap in height does not replace it.} The zeros are known to lie on the line up to a height $H$ ($3\times10^{12}$ at the time of writing), and in outline the standard reasoning runs: the explicit formula gives, unconditionally, $\lambda=\sum_{|\gamma|\le H}|F(\gamma)|^2+\sum_{|\gamma|>H}F(\gamma)F(-\gamma)$, the second sum is of the order of $e^{a}$ times the tail energy above $H$ ($F$ is evaluated in the strip $|\mathrm{Im}|\le\tfrac12$, where $|F(t+iy)|$ is the transform of $f(u)e^{-yu}$), that is $e^a|A_0|^2/H$ by the analytic tail law; so $\lambda>0$ follows from the first sum exceeding this, which with $2|A_0|^2=-\lambda'\approx3.8\Tst\lambda$ holds when $e^a\Tst\ll H$, that is $a\lesssim\tfrac13\log(H/2\pi)\approx9$. Positivity of the floor up to $a\approx9$ is the known consequence of the verified zeros; the inequality \eqref{eq:theinequality} is needed for all $a$, and the gain per step of any such bootstrap is $O(1/\Tst)$, which does not sum (the memo \texttt{RH\_TOP3\_PROOF\_ARCHITECTURE.md} in \cite{michaels-repo}, Section~3).

\emph{The prime-side form.} At a support with a finite tree the amplitude is explicit in the Wiener--Hopf data: with $\varphi_1$ the solution of the eigen-equation with unit polar source and $\lambda$ as a parameter, $f=P\varphi_1$, the normalization fixes $P^2=1/\|\varphi_1\|^2$, the secular equation $\langle\varphi_1,s\rangle=1$ fixes $\lambda(a)$ as the lowest root, and $A_0=P\,A_0^{(1)}(\lambda,a)$ with $A_0^{(1)}$ the amplitude of Corollary~\ref{cor:edgeform} for the unit source. So \eqref{eq:theinequality} is $2|A_0^{(1)}|^2\le c\,\Tst\,\lambda\,\|\varphi_1\|^2$ at the root of the secular equation, a statement in which only the archimedean symbol and the entries below $e^{2a}$ appear, through $\sigma_{\mathrm{eff}}$ and its factors. Beyond $a_\infty$ the same holds with the window's symbol, and the only structure of the entries that enters the effective symbol is the spectral measure of the echo lattice at the edge (Proposition~\ref{prop:spectral}), whose moments are
\begin{equation}\label{eq:moments}
\int\nu^k\,d\mu_b(\nu)=\langle b,C^kb\rangle=\sum\ \prod_{i=1}^{k+2}\frac{\Lambda(m_i)}{\sqrt{m_i}},
\end{equation}
the sum over the closed walks of length $k+2$ through the edge: sequences of prime powers $m_i<e^{2a}$ and signs $\varepsilon_i$ with $\prod m_i^{\varepsilon_i}=1$ whose partial products stay in the window $[1,e^{2a}]$ when started at its end. The onset of the tail law, and with it the constant in \eqref{eq:theinequality}, is set by the upper tail of $\mu_b$ (Computation~\ref{comp:spectral}); its uniformity in $a$ is the statement that these multiplicative relations, weighted by $\Lambda(m)m^{-1/2}$, do not accumulate at the top of the spectrum, and Computation~\ref{comp:moments} measures their growth. This is the point at which the problem has become arithmetic, and it is where the paper stops.

\end{tabular}
\end{center}
(Parentheses: beyond the exact order at that depth.) The first moment is small because a ratio of two prime powers is rarely a prime power: $m_1$ is $0.1$--$0.25$ at every support, while the second, the mean weighted degree of the first-generation points, grows like $e^{a}$. The even roots $m_k^{1/k}$ increase slowly towards the top of the support and sit, at $k=8$, a constant $2.1$--$2.3$ below the height of five horizons at every support, a factor nine in $t$; the Chebyshev bound with the highest exact even moment puts the weight of $\mu_b$ above five horizons below $2\times10^{-6}$ at $a=1$, $10^{-3}$ at $1.5$, $2.2\%$ at $2$ and $5.6\%$ at $2.5$, from the moments alone (Computation~\ref{comp:spectral}, Proposition~\ref{prop:edgeband}). What the uniformity of the onset asks is that this persist: that the closed walks through the edge of length $k$, the multiplicative relations among the integers below $e^{2a}$ with partial products in the window, weighted by $\prod\Lambda(m_i)m_i^{-1/2}$, have $m_k^{1/k}\le2a+\log\kappa-\delta$ for some $\kappa,\delta$ and a $k$ growing with $a$. Since $m_k$ grows exponentially in $a$ at fixed $k$ while $(2a+\log\kappa)^k$ grows polynomially, the order $k$ must grow with $a$, and the statement is about long relations, of length comparable to $a$.
\end{computation}


\subsection{The plane}\label{sec:plane}

The third leg of the triangulation of Section~\ref{sec:rh}, the zeros, was taken on the line. Unconditionally the zeros live in the strip, and the floor is a quadratic functional on the plane: with $F$ odd and entire, $F(z)F(-z)=-F(z)^2=4S(z)^2$ for every complex $z$, $S(z)=\int_0^af(u)\sin(zu)\,du$, and the explicit formula reads, for every odd $f$ supported in $[-a,a]$ and without any hypothesis,
\begin{equation}\label{eq:plane}
Q(f)=\sum_\rho q(\gamma_\rho),\qquad q(z)=4S(z)^2,\qquad \rho=\tfrac12+i\gamma_\rho ,
\end{equation}
the sum over all nontrivial zeros with multiplicity, $\gamma_\rho=\gamma_0-i\delta$ for a zero $\rho=\tfrac12+\delta+i\gamma_0$ off the line. On the line $q\ge0$. Off it, $q$ is complex; a zero and its three companions $\rho,1-\rho,\bar\rho,1-\bar\rho$, that is $\pm\gamma_0\pm i\delta$, contribute $4\,\mathrm{Re}\,q(\gamma_0-i\delta)=16\,\mathrm{Re}\,S(\gamma_0-i\delta)^2$, of either sign; and to second order in the displacement, $q(x+iy)=4\big(S(x)+iyS'(x)\big)^2+O(y^3)$ has real part $4S(x)^2-4y^2S'(x)^2$, negative wherever $y|S'|>|S|$, which is near the nulls of $S$, where the zeros are. The Hypothesis is the statement that all the terms of \eqref{eq:plane} are squares; the following theorem says what the floor does when they are not.

\begin{theorem}[the floor reads the plane]\label{thm:plane}
Let $\delta_{\max}=\sup_\rho(\mathrm{Re}\,\rho-\tfrac12)\in[0,\tfrac12)$, and write $\log^+(-\lambda)=0$ where $\lambda\ge0$. Then:
\begin{enumerate}
\item[(i)] $\lambda(a)\ge0$ for all $a$ if and only if $\delta_{\max}=0$, that is, if and only if the Riemann Hypothesis holds (Weil's criterion on the odd sector \cite{bombieri}).
\item[(ii)] Assume (H$_\infty$) and that the total variation of the minimizer is at most exponential in $a$. Then $\limsup_{a\to\infty}\log^+(-\lambda(a))/2a\le\delta_{\max}$.
\item[(iii)] If the supremum is attained, at a zero $\rho_0=\tfrac12+\delta_{\max}+i\gamma_0$ with $\delta_{\max}>0$ (as it is when only finitely many zeros lie off the line), then $\lambda(a)\le-e^{2\delta_{\max}a}/(2\delta_{\max})\,(1+o(1))$, so that $\lim\log(-\lambda(a))/2a=\delta_{\max}$.
\end{enumerate}
\end{theorem}
\begin{proof}
(i) is Weil's criterion: on the line every term of \eqref{eq:plane} is a square, and an odd test function with $Q<0$ exists when a zero is off the line; the odd sector suffices because the test functions of the criterion can be taken odd (a zero and its mirror $1-\bar\rho$ have the same $|\gamma|$).

(ii) Let $V(a)\le e^{Ca}$ be the total variation of $f$, so that $|F(x+iy)|\le V(a)e^{a|y|}/|x|$, and $\int|F(x+iy)|^2dx\le2\pi e^{2a|y|}$ (Plancherel--P\'olya). By Gallagher's inequality for sums over the zeros of a function of exponential type $a$, $\sum_{|\gamma_\rho|\le T}|F(\gamma_\rho)|^2\le C(a+\log T)\sup_{|y|\le\delta_{\max}}\int|F(x+iy)|^2dx\le C(a+\log T)e^{2\delta_{\max}a}$, and the zeros above $T=e^{(2C+1)a}$ contribute at most $\sum_{|\gamma|>T}V^2e^{2\delta_{\max}a}/\gamma^2\le e^{2\delta_{\max}a}e^{2Ca}\log T/T\le e^{2\delta_{\max}a}$. So $\lambda=Q(f)=\sum_\rho q(\gamma_\rho)\ge-Ca^2e^{2\delta_{\max}a}$.

(iii) Take $f(u)=\sinh(\delta_{\max}u)\cos(\gamma_0u)\,\phi(u/a)$ on $[-a,a]$, odd, with $\phi$ a fixed smooth even cutoff, $\phi=1$ on $[-1+\eta,1-\eta]$, and write $\delta_0=\delta_{\max}$. The term of the quartet of $\rho_0$ is $-4\,\mathrm{Re}\,w^2$ with $w=F(\gamma_0-i\delta_0)=\int f(u)e^{\delta_0u}e^{i\gamma_0u}du$, whose real part is $\int\sinh^2(\delta_0u)\cos^2(\gamma_0u)\phi(u/a)\,du=c_\phi\,e^{2\delta_0a}(1+o(1))$ with $c_\phi=\tfrac12\int_0^\infty e^{-2\delta_0v}\phi(1-v/a)\,dv\cdot(1+o(1))$ and imaginary part smaller by $O(\delta_0/\gamma_0)$; $\|f\|^2=c_\phi e^{2\delta_0a}(1+o(1))$ with the same $c_\phi$ ($\int\sinh^2\cos^2\phi$ as well). Every other zero has $\delta_j\le\delta_0$. Those within distance $1$ of $\pm\gamma_0$ number at most $C\log\gamma_0$, a fixed number as $a\to\infty$, and $|F(\gamma_j-i\delta_j)|\le\int|f|e^{\delta_ju}\le c'_\phi e^{(\delta_0+\delta_j)a}\cdot(a|\gamma_j-\gamma_0|)^{-N}$ for those at distance at least $1/a$, by the smoothness of the cutoff, while a zero at distance less than $1/a$ of $\gamma_0$ with $\delta_j=\delta_0$ has $w_j=w(1+O(1/a))$ and its term has the sign of the main one; the zeros at distance between $1$ and $T$ contribute at most $C(a+\log T)e^{4\delta_0a}a^{-N}$ by the same decay and the density of zeros, and those above $T=e^{a}$ at most $e^{(1+2\delta_0+2\delta_0)a}\log T/T$ by $|F(x+iy)|\le V e^{a|y|}/|x|$ with $V\le Ce^{\delta_0a}\gamma_0a$. The zeros on the line contribute at most $\sum_\gamma|F(\gamma)|^2\le C(a+\log T)\cdot2\pi\|f\|^2$ below $T$ (Gallagher) and the same tail bound above. Dividing $Q(f)$ by $\|f\|^2$: $\lambda(a)\le-4c_\phi e^{2\delta_0a}(1+o(1))+O(a^2)$, and letting $\eta\to0$, $c_\phi\to1/(8\delta_0)$.
\end{proof}

\begin{corollary}[the edge reads the plane]\label{cor:edgeplane}
Assume (H$_\infty$); the boundary law $\lambda'=-2|A_0|^2$ holds at every support that is not an entry (Theorem~\ref{thm:everysupport}). If the Hypothesis fails with the supremum attained, the edge amplitude of the minimizer grows exponentially, $\lim\frac1{2a}\log|A_0(a)|^2=\delta_{\max}$; if it holds, together with Conjecture~\ref{conj:A}, it decays faster than any exponential, $|A_0(a)|^2\le\tfrac c2\Tst(a)\lambda(a)\le\tfrac c2\Tst(a)\lambda(a_Z)e^{-\frac c2(\Tst(a)-\Tst(a_Z))}$. There is no third behaviour: either the edge of the minimizer reads the distance of the zeros from the line, or it reads the horizon.
\end{corollary}
\begin{proof}
$-\lambda'=2|A_0|^2$ with $\lambda=-e^{2\delta_{\max}a+o(a)}$ by Theorem~\ref{thm:plane}(ii), (iii) gives the first statement, since $2\int_{a_1}^a|A_0|^2=\lambda(a_1)-\lambda(a)$; the second is Theorem~\ref{thm:reduction} and Proposition~\ref{prop:AimpliesRH}.
\end{proof}

Three consequences for the program. First, every unconditional theorem of this paper holds on both branches, since none of them uses the sign of the floor: the Wiener--Hopf representation, the edge law with its constants ($\beta=-\gamma-\log(2\pi a)-\lambda$ grows like $e^{2\delta_{\max}a}$ on the negative branch, so the profile is flat down to $\delta\approx e^{-|\lambda|}$ and the minimizer approaches its edge with what is, at every visible scale, a jump: the function $\sinh(\delta_0u)\cos(\gamma_0u)$ that tracks the quartet), the boundary law and the analytic tail law. This is why they cannot decide the Hypothesis by themselves, and why the inequality \eqref{eq:theinequality} is exactly the statement that the negative branch does not occur. Second, a proof must find a quantity that the two branches force apart \emph{before} the floor changes sign, at supports where $\lambda>0$ still but the quartet already shapes the minimizer; the natural candidate is the quadratic functional on the plane itself, whose second-order response to a displacement of the zeros is $-16y^2\sum_{\gamma>0}S'(\gamma)^2$ at the zeros below the horizon, where the minimizer's transform vanishes with a large slope: Computation~\ref{comp:plane} measures this stiffness and finds the floor annulled by displacements of the first zeros that shrink like $\lambda^{1/2}$, that is like $e^{-c\Tst/2}$. Third, the triangulation itself: the floor is the matrices (an upper bound at every truncation), the symbol (exact), and the zeros in the plane (exact); its agreement on the computed zeros tests the identity \eqref{eq:plane}, not the Hypothesis, since those zeros are verified to lie on the line; what the plane adds is the rate, which turns the Hypothesis into a statement about one number, the exponential rate of the negative part of the floor, that the unconditional structure of the minimizer carries to its edge.

\begin{computation}[the floor on the plane]\label{comp:plane}
\texttt{rh\_plane.py}, log \texttt{plane.log} in \texttt{data/}. On the $K$-mode minimizers at $a=0.6$, $0.8$, $1.0$ (Computation~\ref{comp:tail}; $F$ at complex heights by the Bessel series and the recurrence at $60$ digits), with the first $6000$ zeros. (i) \emph{The stiffness.} A quartet at $\pm\gamma\pm iy$ in place of the pair $\pm\gamma$ changes the floor by $-16y^2S'(\gamma)^2+O(y^4)$ at fixed $f$; the stiffness $\kappa_2=\sum_{\gamma>0}16S'(\gamma)^2$ and the displacement $y_c=(\lambda/\kappa_2)^{1/2}$ at which a uniform displacement of the zeros annuls the floor to second order:
\begin{center}\footnotesize
\begin{tabular}{@{}lccccc@{}}
\toprule
$a$ & $\lambda$ & $\kappa_2$ & $\kappa_2/\lambda$ & $y_c$ & share of $\kappa_2$ below $\Tst$\\
\midrule
$0.6$ & $5.97\times10^{-7}$ & $4.83\times10^{-3}$ & $8.1\times10^{3}$ & $1.1\times10^{-2}$ & $0.986$ (one zero)\\
$0.8$ & $1.57\times10^{-14}$ & $2.05\times10^{-3}$ & $1.3\times10^{11}$ & $2.8\times10^{-6}$ & $1.000$\\
$1.0$ & $1.48\times10^{-26}$ & $1.23\times10^{-3}$ & $8.3\times10^{22}$ & $3.5\times10^{-12}$ & $1.000$\\
\bottomrule
\end{tabular}
\end{center}
The stiffness is of order $10^{-3}$ and sits entirely on the zeros below the horizon, where the minimizer's transform vanishes with a slope of order one; so $y_c$ shrinks like $\lambda^{1/2}$, that is like $e^{-c\Tst/2}$: the floor at support $a$ is a certificate that the zeros below the horizon lie within $y_c(a)$ of the line, and the certificate sharpens doubly exponentially with the support, which is the resolution $e^{-c\Tst}$ of Section~\ref{sec:conj} read on the plane. (ii) \emph{The growth off the line.} The ratio $r(y)=\langle|q(x+iy)|\rangle/\langle q(x)\rangle$ over windows of one horizon, against the growth $\cosh(2ya)$ of the edge's cosine of frequency $a$:
\begin{center}\footnotesize
\begin{tabular}{@{}llccccc@{}}
\toprule
$a$ & window & $y=0.05$ & $0.1$ & $0.2$ & $0.3$ & $0.45$\\
\midrule
 & $\cosh(2ya)$ & $1.0018$ & $1.0072$ & $1.0289$ & $1.0655$ & $1.1494$\\
$0.6$ & $[\Tst,2\Tst]$ & $1.0037$ & $1.0150$ & $1.0602$ & $1.1363$ & $1.3115$\\
 & $[5\Tst,6\Tst]$ & $1.0021$ & $1.0084$ & $1.0338$ & $1.0766$ & $1.1748$\\
 & $[10\Tst,11\Tst]$ & $1.0018$ & $1.0073$ & $1.0294$ & $1.0666$ & $1.1520$\\
\midrule
 & $\cosh(2ya)$ & $1.0032$ & $1.0128$ & $1.0516$ & $1.1174$ & $1.2706$\\
$0.8$ & $[\Tst,2\Tst]$ & $1.0040$ & $1.0161$ & $1.0649$ & $1.1482$ & $1.3450$\\
 & $[5\Tst,6\Tst]$ & $1.0032$ & $1.0127$ & $1.0512$ & $1.1165$ & $1.2687$\\
 & $[10\Tst,11\Tst]$ & $1.0032$ & $1.0129$ & $1.0518$ & $1.1179$ & $1.2717$\\
\midrule
 & $\cosh(2ya)$ & $1.0050$ & $1.0201$ & $1.0811$ & $1.1855$ & $1.4331$\\
$1.0$ & $[\Tst,2\Tst]$ & $1.0026$ & $1.0105$ & $1.0423$ & $1.0965$ & $1.2245$\\
 & $[5\Tst,6\Tst]$ & $1.0050$ & $1.0200$ & $1.0809$ & $1.1852$ & $1.4326$\\
 & $[10\Tst,11\Tst]$ & $1.0051$ & $1.0205$ & $1.0827$ & $1.1893$ & $1.4422$\\
