\end{center}
Above the horizon the growth of the transform into the strip is the edge's to three or four digits at every support and every displacement up to $0.45$: the plane reads the edge. In the mass region it departs from it, upward at $a=0.6$, $0.8$ (the transform hides from the zeros there, with nulls denser than the edge's cosine, so $|S'|$ is large where $|S|$ is small) and downward at $a=1.0$ (the frequency content of $[\Tst,2\Tst]$ then comes from the interior of the window). (iii) \emph{The sign.} The fraction of heights at which $\mathrm{Re}\,q(x+iy)<0$ is $0.02$--$0.05$ at $y=0.1$, $0.07$--$0.15$ at $0.2$, $0.12$--$0.19$ at $0.3$ and $0.19$--$0.27$ at $0.45$, nearly independent of the height and of the support: a zero displaced by $y$ lowers the floor at that fraction of the positions, around the nulls of $S$, and raises it elsewhere; the minimizer on the negative branch of Theorem~\ref{thm:plane} is the function that moves its nulls to make the displaced zeros' terms negative.
\end{computation}


\subsection{The boundary law at every support}\label{sec:everysupport}

Theorem~\ref{thm:reduction} carries one link that is a theorem only below $a_\infty$: the boundary law $\lambda'=-2|A_0|^2$, which Corollary~\ref{cor:tree}(v) proves from the edge law, and the edge law from the representation of the transform with a symbol whose factors have smooth kernels away from the origin (Lemma~\ref{lem:kernel}). Beyond $a_\infty$ the exact representation is the one with the window's symbol (Proposition~\ref{prop:window}), whose factors have kernels singular on the lattice generated by the entries, dense in the window. This section proves the edge law, and with it the boundary law and the analytic tail law, at every support that is not an entry. Two facts carry the proof. Above its periodic band the window's symbol has an absolutely convergent expansion in the frequencies of the lattice, with coefficients indexed by the walks of the echo tree and summable with their lengths (Proposition~\ref{prop:lattice}); and a walk of $k$ steps cannot return to within $\tfrac12e^{-2ak}$ of its start, because $\log(M/M')\ne0$ for integers $M\ne M'$ below $e^{2ak}$ is at least $1/(2\min(M,M'))$ (Lemma~\ref{lem:dioph}). So the echoes that accumulate at an edge are long chains, and their total weight within $\varepsilon$ of the edge is a power of $\varepsilon$. The mirror chains, the walks from the far edge across the window, are controlled in the same way at every support, the chain that lands exactly on the near edge at a resonant support included; they are the leading correction to the edge amplitude $A_a(t)$ of the window's representation, and Computation~\ref{comp:mirror} finds them in the data at their predicted frequencies.

\begin{lemma}[the lattice of the entries]\label{lem:dioph}
Let $\Lambda_a$ be the additive group generated by $D_a$, that is $\Lambda_a=\{\log(M/M'):M,M'\in\N\text{ with all prime factors below }e^{2a}\}$, dense in $\R$ for $a>\tfrac12\log3$. A \emph{word} of length $k$ is a sequence $w=(\varepsilon_1d_1,\dots,\varepsilon_kd_k)$ with $\varepsilon_i=\pm1$, $d_i\in D_a$; its point is $u_w=\sum_i\varepsilon_id_i\in\Lambda_a$ and its weight $c_w=\prod_ic_{d_i}$, so that $\sum_{|w|=k}c_w=X^k$ with $X=\sum_{d\in D_a}2c_d$ the top of the periodic band (Lemma~\ref{lem:periodic}).
\begin{enumerate}
\item[(i)] If $u_w\ne0$ then $|u_w|>\tfrac12e^{-2a|w|}$.
\item[(ii)] Call $a$ \emph{resonant} if $2a\in\Lambda_a$; the resonant supports are countable. At a non-resonant support, $k_a(\varepsilon)=\min\{|w|:|u_w-2a|<\varepsilon\}$ is finite for every $\varepsilon>0$ and tends to infinity as $\varepsilon\to0$, and $d_a=\min_{|w|\le2}|u_w-2a|>0$. At a resonant support $2a=u_{w_0}$ for a word with $|w_0|\ge2$, and $|u_w-2a|>\tfrac12e^{-2a(|w|+|w_0|)}$ for every word with $u_w\ne2a$.
\end{enumerate}
\end{lemma}
\begin{proof}
(i) $u_w=\log(M/M')$ with $M=\prod_{\varepsilon_i=1}m_i$ and $M'=\prod_{\varepsilon_i=-1}m_i$ ($d_i=\log m_i$), positive integers below $e^{2a|w|}$; if $u_w\ne0$ then $M\ne M'$ and $|u_w|\ge\log\big(1+1/\min(M,M')\big)\ge1/(2\min(M,M'))>\tfrac12e^{-2a|w|}$. (ii) $\Lambda_a$ is countable. There are finitely many words of each length, so at a non-resonant support $\min_{|w|\le k}|u_w-2a|>0$ for every $k$, which is $k_a(\varepsilon)\to\infty$ and $d_a>0$, while $k_a(\varepsilon)<\infty$ because $\Lambda_a$ is dense. A single step is shorter than $2a$, the entries being below $e^{2a}$, so a word with $u_{w_0}=2a$ has length at least $2$; and for $u_w\ne2a=u_{w_0}$ the concatenation of $w$ with the reverse of $w_0$ is a word of length $|w|+|w_0|$ with the point $u_w-2a\ne0$, to which (i) applies.
\end{proof}

\begin{proposition}[the window's factors above the band]\label{prop:lattice}
Let $s>X$ and $\sigma_s(\tau)=s-\sum_{d\in D_a}2c_d\cos(\tau d)$, the window's symbol at the frozen height $s$. Then:
\begin{enumerate}
\item[(i)] $\log\sigma_s(\tau)=\log s+\sum_{u\in\Lambda_a}\beta_u(s)e^{i\tau u}$, absolutely convergent, with
\[
\beta_u(s)=-\sum_{k\ge1}\frac1k\sum_{|w|=k,\ u_w=u}\frac{c_w}{s^k}\le0,\qquad \beta_{-u}=\beta_u,\qquad \sum_u|\beta_u(s)|=-\log(1-X/s),
\]
and $\beta_0(s)=\log G(s)-\log s$, $G$ the geometric mean of Lemma~\ref{lem:periodic}.
\item[(ii)] $\sigma_{s,\pm}(\tau)=G(s)^{1/2}\exp\big(\sum_{\pm u>0}\beta_u(s)e^{i\tau u}\big)$ are the Wiener--Hopf factors of $\sigma_s$: boundary values of bounded, zero-free analytic functions in the upper and lower half-planes with bounded inverses, $\sigma_{s,+}\sigma_{s,-}=\sigma_s$ on the line, $\sigma_{s,-}=\overline{\sigma_{s,+}}$, and $\arg\sigma_{s,+}(\tau)=\sum_{u>0}\beta_u(s)\sin(\tau u)$, an odd function vanishing at $0$.
\item[(iii)] $1/\sigma_{s,+}=G(s)^{-1/2}\sum_{u\ge0}\rho_u(s)e^{i\tau u}$ with $\rho_0=1$, $\rho_u(s)\ge0$, and $\sum_{u>0}\rho_u(s)\le(1-X/s)^{-1}-1$, with $\rho_u(s)=O(s^{-k(u)})$, $k(u)$ the least length of a word with $u_w=u$; the terms of the expansion that arise from words of total length $k$ have total weight at most $(X/s)^k$, hence
\[
\sum_{0<u<\varepsilon}\rho_u(s)\le\frac{(2\varepsilon)^{\alpha(s)}}{1-X/s},\qquad\alpha(s)=\frac{\log(s/X)}{2a},
\]
and near $2a$: at a non-resonant support $\sum_{|u-2a|<\varepsilon}\rho_u(s)\le(X/s)^{k_a(\varepsilon)}/(1-X/s)$, which is $O(s^{-3})$ for $\varepsilon<d_a$ and $O(s^{-N})$ for every $N$ as $\varepsilon\to0$, while at a resonant support, $2a=u_{w_0}$, the words landing exactly on $2a$ have total weight $\rho_{2a}(s)\le(X/s)^{|w_0|}/(1-X/s)=O(s^{-2})$ and the others within $\varepsilon$ at most $(s/X)^{|w_0|}(2\varepsilon)^{\alpha(s)}/(1-X/s)$ (Lemma~\ref{lem:dioph}(ii)). The same holds for $1/\sigma_{s,-}$ with $u\le0$.
\item[(iv)] $(s/G(s))^{1/2}=1+|b|^2/(2s^2)+O(s^{-4})$ with $|b|^2=\sum_{d\in D_a}c_d^2$, and $\rho_{\pm d}(s)=c_d/s+O(s^{-2})$ for $d\in D_a$: the first terms of the expansion are the first-generation echoes of Corollary~\ref{cor:tree}, and the correction to the modulus of the factor is that of the effective symbol, $\tilde\sigma_{\mathrm{eff}}^{-1/2}=\tilde\sigma_\infty^{-1/2}(1+|b|^2/(2\tilde\sigma_\infty^2)+\dots)$.
\end{enumerate}
\end{proposition}
\begin{proof}
(i) With $x_s(\tau)=\sum_d(c_d/s)(e^{i\tau d}+e^{-i\tau d})$, $|x_s|\le X/s<1$, so $\log\sigma_s=\log s+\log(1-x_s)=\log s-\sum_{k\ge1}x_s^k/k$, and $x_s^k=\sum_{|w|=k}c_ws^{-k}e^{i\tau u_w}$; the double series converges absolutely, with sum of moduli $\sum_k(X/s)^k/k=-\log(1-X/s)$, and regrouping by $u$ gives the expansion, the symmetry from $w\mapsto-w$, and the sign. Its constant term is the mean of $\log\sigma_s$ over $\tau$, which is $U_\nu(s)=\log G(s)$ by Lemma~\ref{lem:periodic}. (ii) For $\Imm z\ge0$ and $u>0$, $|e^{izu}|\le1$, so $B_+(z)=\sum_{u>0}\beta_u(s)e^{izu}$ converges absolutely and uniformly on the closed upper half-plane, is analytic inside and continuous up to the line, with $|B_+|\le-\log(1-X/s)$; hence $\sigma_{s,+}=G^{1/2}e^{B_+}$ is analytic, bounded, zero-free, with $|\sigma_{s,+}|\ge G^{1/2}(1-X/s)$; likewise $\sigma_{s,-}=G^{1/2}e^{B_-}$ below. On the line $B_++B_-=\sum_{u\ne0}\beta_ue^{i\tau u}=\log\sigma_s-\log G$, so $\sigma_{s,+}\sigma_{s,-}=\sigma_s$, and $B_-=\overline{B_+}$ since $\beta_{-u}=\beta_u$ is real; the argument is $\Imm B_+$. (iii) $1/\sigma_{s,+}=G^{-1/2}e^{-B_+}=G^{-1/2}\sum_{n\ge0}(-B_+)^n/n!$, where $-B_+=\sum_{u>0}|\beta_u|e^{i\tau u}$ has nonnegative coefficients; so the regrouped coefficients $\rho_u$ are nonnegative, $\rho_0=1$ (no product of frequencies $u>0$ vanishes), and $\sum_{u>0}\rho_u=e^{\sum_{u>0}|\beta_u|}-1\le(1-X/s)^{-1}-1$. Mark the length of the words by a variable $z$: replacing $c_w$ by $c_wz^{|w|}$ throughout, every coefficient of the resulting power series in $z$ is bounded by the corresponding coefficient of $\exp\big(\sum_k(Xz/s)^k/k\big)=(1-Xz/s)^{-1}$, which is $(X/s)^k$ at $z^k$. A term contributing to $\rho_u$ is a product $\beta_{u_1}\cdots\beta_{u_n}$ with $u_1+\dots+u_n=u$, each $\beta_{u_i}$ a sum over words $w_i$ with $u_{w_i}=u_i$, and the concatenation $w_1\cdots w_n$ is a word of length $k=\sum|w_i|$ with $u_w=u$; if $0<u<\varepsilon$ then $k>\log(1/2\varepsilon)/(2a)$ by Lemma~\ref{lem:dioph}(i), and $\sum_{k>k_\varepsilon}(X/s)^k\le(X/s)^{k_\varepsilon}/(1-X/s)=(2\varepsilon)^{\alpha(s)}/(1-X/s)$. Near $2a$: at a non-resonant support a word within $\varepsilon$ of $2a$ has length at least $k_a(\varepsilon)$, which is at least $3$ for $\varepsilon<d_a$; at a resonant one, Lemma~\ref{lem:dioph}(ii) gives $|w|>\log(1/2\varepsilon)/2a-|w_0|$ for the words with $0<|u_w-2a|<\varepsilon$; the same geometric sums give the bounds. (iv) is Lemma~\ref{lem:periodic}, $G=s-|b|^2/s+O(s^{-3})$, and the first terms of the series: $\beta_{\pm d}=-c_d/s+O(s^{-2})$ (the one-letter words; the two-letter words with $u_w=d$ are $O(s^{-2})$), so $\rho_{\pm d}=|\beta_{\pm d}|+O(s^{-2})$.
\end{proof}

In the frozen picture the kernel of $1/\sigma_{s,+}$ is the discrete measure $G^{-1/2}\sum_{u\ge0}\rho_u\delta_u$ on the positive part of the lattice: the chain expansion of Proposition~\ref{prop:window}(iii), now at every support and with the weights of all the chains, summable above the band. Its mass near the origin is a power of the distance, and its mass near $2a$ is $O(s^{-3})$ at every support, the resonant ones putting an $O(s^{-2})$ atom at $2a$ itself. In the actual symbol $s=\tilde\sigma_\infty(t)$ is slowly varying, the band top $t_{\mathrm{band}}=2\pi e^{X+\lambda}$ is a fixed height, and the edge law is a statement about $t\to\infty$, so every support is eventually above its band; the following theorem turns this into the edge law by the method of Corollary~\ref{cor:edgeform} and Proposition~\ref{prop:profile}.

The proof of the theorem below is written so that every estimate is a lemma with its constants. Three lemmas carry the analytic part: the kernel of a symbol with finitely many derivatives, the Hilbert transform of a modulated symbol, and the regularity of the frozen coefficients $\beta_u$ along the archimedean symbol. Throughout, $\check g(\eta)=\frac1{2\pi}\int g(t)e^{-it\eta}\,dt$ is the inverse transform, taken in the sense of tempered distributions and, on $\eta\ne0$, as the oscillatory integral obtained by integrating by parts.

\begin{lemma}[the kernel of a symbol with finitely many derivatives]\label{lem:symbolkernel}
Let $p\in\{0,2\}$, $j\ge0$, and let $g\in C^{j+2}(\R)$ satisfy $|g^{(i)}(t)|\le M(1+|t|)^{-p-i}$ for $0\le i\le j+2$. Then $\check g$ is a $C^j$ function on $\R\setminus\{0\}$, and for $0\le m\le j$ and $\eta\ne0$:
\begin{enumerate}
\item[(i)] if $p=0$: $|\check g^{(m)}(\eta)|\le c_mM\,|\eta|^{-1-m}\min(1,|\eta|^{-1})$, with $c_m=\frac1\pi\big(\frac1{m+1}+\sum_{k=0}^{m+2}d_{k,m}\big)$ and $d_{k,m}=\sum_{i\le\min(k,m)}\binom ki\frac{m!}{(m-i)!}$;
\item[(ii)] if $p=2$: for $0<|\eta|\le1$, $|\check g(\eta)|\le c_0'M$, $|\check g'(\eta)|\le c_1'M(1+\log(1/|\eta|))$, and $|\check g^{(m)}(\eta)|\le c_m'M|\eta|^{1-m}$ for $2\le m\le j$, with $c_m'=\frac1\pi\big(2+\sum_{k=0}^{m+2}d_{k,m}\big)$; for $|\eta|\ge1$ the bounds of (i) hold, an order $-2$ symbol being of order $0$ with the same $M$.
\end{enumerate}
\end{lemma}
\begin{proof}
For $\eta\ne0$ put $T=1/|\eta|$ and write $2\pi\check g^{(m)}(\eta)=\int(-it)^mg(t)e^{-it\eta}dt$ as the sum over $|t|<T$ and $|t|>T$, the second part integrated by parts $m+2$ times on each half-line; the boundary terms at infinity vanish because $|\partial^k(t^mg)(t)|\le M\,d_{k,m}(1+|t|)^{m-p-k}\to0$ for $k\ge m+1$, and $|\partial^k(t^mg)(t)|\le Md_{k,m}(1+|t|)^{m-p-k}$ follows from Leibniz's rule. For $p=0$ and $|\eta|\le1$ ($T\ge1$): the inner part is at most $2MT^{m+1}/(m+1)$; the boundary terms at $\pm T$ are at most $2\sum_{k\le m+1}Md_{k,m}T^{m-k}|\eta|^{-k-1}=2M|\eta|^{-m-1}\sum_{k\le m+1}d_{k,m}$; the remaining integral is at most $2|\eta|^{-m-2}\int_T^\infty Md_{m+2,m}t^{-2}dt=2M|\eta|^{-m-1}d_{m+2,m}$. Dividing by $2\pi$ gives $c_mM|\eta|^{-1-m}$. For $|\eta|\ge1$ integrate by parts $m+2$ times over the whole line: $2\pi|\check g^{(m)}(\eta)|\le|\eta|^{-m-2}\int|\partial^{m+2}(t^mg)|\le|\eta|^{-m-2}Md_{m+2,m}\int(1+|t|)^{-2}dt=2Md_{m+2,m}|\eta|^{-m-2}$, which is at most $2\pi c_mM|\eta|^{-m-2}$. For $p=2$ the same splitting with the extra factor $(1+|t|)^{-2}$ gives, for $m\ge2$, an inner part at most $2MT^{m-1}/(m-1)$ and the same boundary and remainder bounds with $T^{m-p-k}$ in place of $T^{m-k}$, hence $|\eta|^{1-m}$; for $m=0$ the inner part is at most $2M$ and the rest is bounded by $2M\sum_kd_{k,0}|\eta|^{\dots}\le2M\sum_kd_{k,0}$ for $|\eta|\le1$; for $m=1$ the inner part is at most $2M\int_0^T(1+t)^{-1}dt\le2M(1+\log T)$. The bounds for $|\eta|\ge1$ are as in (i). That $\check g$ is $C^j$ on $\eta\ne0$ and that the oscillatory integral is the distribution $\check g$ there is the standard fact that $t^mg$ is a tempered function whose transform on $\eta\ne0$ is given by the integrated-by-parts formula.
\end{proof}

\begin{lemma}[the Hilbert transform of a modulated symbol]\label{lem:hilbmod}
Let $u\ne0$, $j\ge0$, and let $g\in C^{j+3}(\R)$ satisfy $|g^{(i)}(t)|\le M(1+|t|)^{-i}$ for $i\le j+3$. Then $h=g\,e^{i\cdot u}$ has a Hilbert transform $\mathcal H[h]=\mathcal F[-i\,\mathrm{sign}(\xi)\check h]$, and
\[
\mathcal H[h](t)=-i\,\mathrm{sign}(u)\,g(t)e^{itu}+E(t),\qquad E(t)=-2i\,\mathrm{sign}(u)\int_{\mathrm{sign}\,\xi=-\mathrm{sign}\,u}\check g(\xi-u)\,e^{it\xi}\,d\xi ,
\]
where $E\in C^j(\R)$ and, for $0\le k\le j$ and $t\ne0$,
\[
|E^{(k)}(t)|\le\frac{e_k\,M}{|u|\,|t|^{k+1}},\qquad e_k=2\Big(k!\,c_0+\sum_{m=1}^{k+1}\binom{k+1}m\frac{k!}{(m-1)!}\,(c_m+c_m)\Big),
\]
with the $c_m$ of Lemma~\ref{lem:symbolkernel}(i). In particular $\sum_u|E_u^{(k)}(t)|\le e_k|t|^{-k-1}\sum_uM_u/|u|$ for a family $g_u$ with constants $M_u$.
\end{lemma}
\begin{proof}
$\check h(\xi)=\check g(\xi-u)$, and $-i\,\mathrm{sign}(\xi)\check h=-i\,\mathrm{sign}(u)\check h-i\big(\mathrm{sign}(\xi)-\mathrm{sign}(u)\big)\check h$; the first term transforms to $-i\,\mathrm{sign}(u)h$, and the second is supported where $\mathrm{sign}\,\xi=-\mathrm{sign}\,u$, where $\mathrm{sign}(\xi)-\mathrm{sign}(u)=-2\,\mathrm{sign}(u)$ and $|\xi-u|\ge|u|$. On that half-line $\check g(\xi-u)$ is $C^j$ with $|\check g^{(m)}(\xi-u)|\le c_mM|\xi-u|^{-1-m}\min(1,|\xi-u|^{-1})$ by Lemma~\ref{lem:symbolkernel}, so $E$ is an absolutely convergent integral, $C^j$ by differentiation under the integral sign ($|\xi|^k|\check g(\xi-u)|\le c_0M|\xi-u|^{k-2}$ is integrable at infinity for $k\le j$ once $j+2$ derivatives of $g$ are controlled; we assumed $j+3$). Integrate $E^{(k)}(t)=-2i\,\mathrm{sign}(u)\int(i\xi)^k\check g(\xi-u)e^{it\xi}d\xi$ by parts $k+1$ times over the half-line. The boundary terms at $\xi=0$ are $\partial^m[(i\xi)^k\check g(\xi-u)]_{\xi=0}/(it)^{m+1}$, $m\le k$, which vanish for $m<k$ and equal $i^kk!\,\check g(-u)/(it)^{k+1}$ for $m=k$, of modulus at most $k!c_0M|u|^{-1}|t|^{-k-1}$; the boundary terms at infinity vanish. The remaining integral is $(it)^{-k-1}\int\partial^{k+1}[(i\xi)^k\check g(\xi-u)]e^{it\xi}d\xi$ with $\partial^{k+1}[\xi^k\check g(\xi-u)]=\sum_{m=1}^{k+1}\binom{k+1}m\frac{k!}{(m-1)!}\xi^{m-1}\check g^{(m)}(\xi-u)$, and on the half-line $|\xi|^{m-1}|\check g^{(m)}(\xi-u)|\le c_mM|\xi-u|^{m-1}|\xi-u|^{-1-m}\min(1,|\xi-u|^{-1})\le c_mM|\xi-u|^{-2}$, whose integral over $|\xi-u|\ge|u|$ is at most $2c_mM/|u|$. Collecting gives $e_k$ (the factor $2$ in front is the $2$ of $-2i\,\mathrm{sign}(u)$; the two copies of $c_m$ are a safe overcount of the half-line integral).
\end{proof}

\begin{lemma}[the frozen coefficients along the archimedean symbol]\label{lem:frozen}
Let $t_\lambda$ be the real zero of $\sigma_\infty$ and $s(t)=\tilde\sigma_\infty(t)$. There is an absolute height $t_S$ such that for $t\ge\max(t_S,4t_\lambda)$ the function $\tilde\sigma_\infty$ is analytic on the disc $|z-t|\le t/2$ with $|\tilde\sigma_\infty(z)-s(t)|\le2$ there. Consequently, for $s_*>X$ and $t_1\ge\max(t_S,4t_\lambda)$ with $s(2t_1)-2\ge s_*$, and for every $u\in\Lambda_a$: the function $\beta_u(\tilde\sigma_\infty(t))$ is $C^\infty$ on $t\ge2t_1$ with
\[
\Big|\frac{d^i}{dt^i}\beta_u\big(\tilde\sigma_\infty(t)\big)\Big|\le i!\,(2/t)^i\,|\beta_u|(s_*)\qquad(t\ge2t_1,\ i\ge0),
\]
where $|\beta_u|(s)=\sum_k\frac1k\sum_{|w|=k,u_w=u}c_ws^{-k}$ is decreasing in $s$; and with $\chi$ even, smooth, $0$ on $|t|\le2t_1$, $1$ on $|t|\ge4t_1$, $|\chi^{(i)}|\le\kappa_i t_1^{-i}$, the function $g_u=\chi\,\beta_u(\tilde\sigma_\infty(\cdot))$ satisfies $|g_u^{(i)}(t)|\le M_u(1+|t|)^{-i}$ for $i\le6$ with $M_u=\mu\,|\beta_u|(s_*)$, $\mu=\max_{i\le6}\sum_{l\le i}\binom il\kappa_l4^l\,(i-l)!\,2^{i-l}\,(1+1/(2t_1))^i$. The same holds for $\beta_0$ and for the coefficients $\rho_u(s)=\sum_kr_{u,k}s^{-k}$ of Proposition~\ref{prop:lattice}(iii), which are power series in $1/s$ with nonnegative coefficients, with $\rho_u(s_*)$ in place of $|\beta_u|(s_*)$; moreover $\rho_u(s)\le\rho_u(s_*)\,s_*/s$ for $s\ge s_*$ and $u\ne0$, since every term has $k\ge1$. And $g_0=\chi\beta_0(\tilde\sigma_\infty(\cdot))$ is even and real with $\beta_0(s)=-|b|^2s^{-2}+O(s^{-4})$; its Hilbert transform is real, even in the sense $\mathcal H[g_0](-t)=-\mathcal H[g_0](t)$, and
\[
\mathcal H[g_0](t)=-\frac{\pi}{2}\,\frac{d}{d\log t}\big[\beta_0(\tilde\sigma_\infty(t))\big]+O\big((\log t)^{-5}\big)+O\Big(\frac{t_1\log t_1}{t}\Big)=-\frac{\pi|b|^2}{\ell(t)^3}+O\big(\ell^{-5}\big)+O\Big(\frac{t_1\log t_1}t\Big),
\]
with $\ell(t)=\log(t/2\pi)-\lambda$, and the same expansion differentiated term by term for $j\le3$ derivatives.
\end{lemma}
\begin{proof}
For $z$ in the disc, $\Ree z\ge t/2>0$, so $w_\pm=\tfrac14\pm iz/2$ have $\Imm w_+=\Ree z/2\ge t/4$ and $\Imm w_-\le-t/4$: both lie in $|\arg w|\le3\pi/4$ once $t\ge2$, where Stirling's formula $\psi(w)=\log w-\frac1{2w}+r(w)$, $|r(w)|\le\frac{1}{8|w|^2\cos^2(\arg w/2)}$ \cite[5.11.ii]{dlmf} applies; hence $\Psi_\infty(z)=\log(z/2\pi)+\rho(z)$ with $|\rho(z)|\le4/t$ for $t\ge t_S$, an absolute constant, and the rational factor $(z^2+t_\lambda^2)/(z^2-t_\lambda^2)$ is analytic on the disc and within $4t_\lambda^2/t^2\cdot\log$-size of $1$ once $t\ge4t_\lambda$ (its poles $\pm t_\lambda$ lie outside the disc, and $\sigma_\infty(\pm t_\lambda)=0$ removes the real ones). With $|\log(z/t)|\le\log2+\pi/6<1.3$ on the disc, $|\tilde\sigma_\infty(z)-s(t)|\le1.3+4/t+O(t_\lambda^2\log t/t^2)\le2$ for $t\ge t_S$. Then $|\tilde\sigma_\infty(z)|\ge s(t)-2\ge s(2t_1)-2\ge s_*>X$ on the disc for $t\ge2t_1$ ($s$ is increasing), so $\beta_u\circ\tilde\sigma_\infty$ is analytic there, bounded by $|\beta_u|(s_*)$ since every term of its series has modulus at most $c_w/(k|\tilde\sigma_\infty(z)|^k)$; Cauchy's estimate on the disc of radius $t/2$ gives the derivative bound. The bound for $g_u$ is Leibniz's rule with $|\chi^{(l)}|\le\kappa_lt_1^{-l}\le\kappa_l(4/t)^l$ on $|t|\le4t_1$ and $(2/t)^i\le2^i(1+1/(2t_1))^i(1+t)^{-i}$ for $t\ge2t_1$. For the Hilbert transform of $g_0$, substitute $\tau=tx$ in $\mathcal H[g_0](t)=\frac1\pi\int_0^\infty\frac{2t\,(g_0(\tau)-g_0(t))}{t^2-\tau^2}d\tau$ (valid for even $g_0$) and expand $g_0(tx)-g_0(t)$ in powers of $\log x$ as in the proof of Proposition~\ref{prop:profile}, $\int_0^\infty\frac{(\log x)^k}{1-x^2}dx$ vanishing for even $k$ and equal to $-\pi^2/4$, $-\pi^4/8$ for $k=1,3$: the terms with $k=1,3$ give $-\frac\pi2\frac{d}{d\log t}g_0-\frac{\pi^3}{24}\frac{d^3}{d(\log t)^3}g_0$, the second of order $\ell^{-5}$ and the range $x<4t_1/t$, where $\chi\ne1$, contributes $O(t_1\log t_1/t)$ (the integrand is bounded by $\sup|g_0|\cdot2t/(t^2-\tau^2)$ there). The derivatives are the same representation differentiated under the integral.
\end{proof}

\begin{lemma}[inversion of a remainder]\label{lem:inversion}
Let $\Lambda\ge0$ be non-increasing on $[T_0,\infty)$ with $\Lambda(t)\le\Lambda_0\Lambda(2t)$ (doubling), and let $D$ be bounded on $|t|\le T_0$, $C^3$ on $|t|\ge T_0$, with $|D^{(j)}(t)|\le D_0|t|^{-1-j}\Lambda(|t|)$ for $j\le3$ and $D(-t)=\overline{D(t)}$. Then the inverse transform $\psi=\check D$ is a real $C^2$ function on $\R\setminus\{0\}$, and for $0<|x|\le1/T_0$ and $k=1,2$,
\[
|\psi^{(k)}(x)|\le\frac{D_0\,\Lambda(1/|x|)}{\pi\,|x|^k}\Big(\frac{\Lambda_0}{1-2^{-k}}+\frac{2^{k+1}\Lambda_0}{3-k}+6\cdot2^k\Big)+\frac{\|D\|_{L^\infty(|t|\le T_0)}T_0^{k+1}}{\pi(k+1)} .
\]
If moreover $\Lambda(t)\le\Lambda_1(\log t)^{-5/2}$, then $\psi^{(k)}(x)=O(|x|^{-k}L^{-5/2})$: $\psi$ is in the remainder class of Proposition~\ref{prop:profile}.
\end{lemma}
\begin{proof}
$2\pi\psi^{(k)}(x)=\int(-it)^kD(t)e^{-itx}dt$ as an oscillatory integral. Split at $|t|=1/|x|=:T$. The inner part is at most $\int_{|t|<T_0}|t|^k\|D\|_\infty+2\int_{T_0}^Tt^{k-1}D_0\Lambda(t)dt$, and with the doubling property $\int_{T_0}^Tt^{k-1}\Lambda\le\sum_{n\ge0}\int_{T2^{-n-1}}^{T2^{-n}}t^{k-1}\Lambda\le\Lambda(T)\sum_n\Lambda_0^{n+1}\dots$; more simply, $\Lambda(t)\le\Lambda_0\Lambda(2t)\le\dots$ gives $\Lambda(T2^{-n})\le\Lambda_0^n\Lambda(T)$ and $\int_{T2^{-n-1}}^{T2^{-n}}t^{k-1}dt\le(T2^{-n})^k/k$, so the sum is at most $\Lambda(T)T^k\sum_n\Lambda_0^n2^{-nk}/k$; we use the cruder bound $\int_{T_0}^Tt^{k-1}\Lambda\le\Lambda_0\Lambda(T)T^k/(1-2^{-k})$, valid when $\Lambda_0\le2^{k}$, which is the case for the two functions used below ($\Lambda=1$ and $\Lambda=(\log t)^{-5/2}$ with $\Lambda_0=1$; and $\Lambda=t^{-1/2}$ with $\Lambda_0=\sqrt2$). The outer part is integrated by parts three times on each half-line: the boundary terms are $|(t^kD)^{(m)}(T)|\,|x|^{-m-1}$, $m\le2$, each at most $2^kD_0T^{k-1-m}\Lambda(T)|x|^{-m-1}=2^kD_0\Lambda(T)|x|^{-k}$ ($(t^kD)^{(m)}$ is a sum of at most $2^m$ terms $t^{k-i}D^{(m-i)}$, each bounded by $D_0t^{k-1-m}\Lambda$), six of them in all; the remaining integral is at most $|x|^{-3}\cdot2\int_T^\infty|(t^kD)'''|\le|x|^{-3}\cdot2\cdot2^3D_0\int_T^\infty t^{k-4}\Lambda\le|x|^{-3}\cdot16D_0\Lambda(T)T^{k-3}/(3-k)$, using that $\Lambda$ is non-increasing. Collecting and dividing by $2\pi$ gives the bound (the constant in front of $2^{k+1}\Lambda_0/(3-k)$ is a safe overcount). The integrals converge absolutely after the integrations by parts, so $\psi$ is $C^2$ on $x\ne0$, and real since $D(-t)=\overline{D(t)}$.
\end{proof}

\begin{theorem}[the edge law and the boundary law at every support]\label{thm:everysupport}
Assume (H$_\infty$). Let $a$ be a support that is not an entry, $f$ the minimizer at $a$, $\sigma_a$, $\tilde\sigma_a$, $\sigma_{a,\pm}$, $A_a(t)$ as in Proposition~\ref{prop:window}, $s=s(t)=\tilde\sigma_\infty(t)$, $w=\tilde\sigma_a/\tilde\sigma_\infty$, $C_w=\sup_\R|\log w|$, $C_R=\sum_jt_j^2$ over the real zeros of $\sigma_a$, and let $s_*>Xe^{2a}$ (that is, $\alpha(s_*)=\log(s_*/X)/2a>1$) and $t_1$ be as in Lemma~\ref{lem:frozen}, with $2t_1$ above the real zeros of $\sigma_a$. Then:
\begin{enumerate}
\item[(i)] (\emph{the factors}) For $|t|\ge8t_1$, with $m_\pm=\sigma_{\infty,\pm}/\sigma_{a,\pm}$,
\begin{equation}\label{eq:mline}
m_\pm(t)=\Big(\frac s{G(s)}\Big)^{1/2}e^{\mp\frac i2\mathcal H[g_0](t)}\sum_{\pm u\ge0}\rho_u(s)\,e^{itu}\,\big(1+\varepsilon_\pm(t)\big),\qquad|\varepsilon_\pm^{(j)}(t)|\le\frac{\varepsilon_j\,t_1}{|t|^{1+j}}\quad(j\le3),
\end{equation}
where $\mathcal H[g_0]=-\pi|b|^2\ell^{-3}+O(\ell^{-5})$ is the real slowly varying phase of Lemma~\ref{lem:frozen} and $\varepsilon_j$ depends only on $a$, $s_*$, $C_w$, $C_R$ and $t_1$, explicitly in the proof.
\item[(ii)] (\emph{the amplitude}) $A_a(t)\to A_0$ as $t\to\infty$, $A_0$ purely imaginary; more precisely
\[
A_a(t)=A_0+M_a(t)+O\big((\log t)^{-2}\big),\qquad M_a(t)=\sum_{u\in\Lambda_a,\ u>2a}m_u(t)\,e^{it(u-2a)},\qquad |m_u(t)|\le C\rho_u(s),
\]
$M_a$ the contribution of the \emph{mirror chains}, the walks from the far edge across the window, so that $|M_a(t)|\le C'(X/s)^{k_a}$ with $k_a\ge2$ the least length of a word exceeding $2a$.
\item[(iii)] (\emph{the edge}) With $\varphi(\delta)=|A_0|\big(\log(a/\delta)+\beta\big)^{-1/2}$, $\beta=-\gamma-\log(2\pi a)-\lambda$, and $L=\log(a/\delta)+\beta$, on $0<\delta<\delta_0$
\[
f(a-\delta)=\varphi(\delta)+e(\delta)+\psi(\delta),\qquad \psi^{(k)}(\delta)=O\big(\delta^{-k}L^{-5/2}\big)\ (k=1,2),\qquad e(0^+)+\psi(0^+)=0 ,
\]
where the \emph{echo part} $e$ is the inverse transform, at position $-\delta$, of
\[
\hat e(t)=\frac{A_0\,(s/G)^{1/2}e^{\frac i2\mathcal H[g_0]}}{t\,\sigma_{\infty,-}(t)}\sum_{u\in\Lambda_a,\ u<0}\rho_u(s)\,e^{itu}\,\big(1+\varepsilon_-(t)\big):
\]
a sum over the lattice points $u<0$ of cusps of the type of \eqref{eq:localecho} at distance $|u|$ from the edge, with weights $\rho_u(s)$ whose total within $\varepsilon$ of the edge is at most $(2\varepsilon)^{\alpha(s)}/(1-X/s)$ (Proposition~\ref{prop:lattice}(iii)); $e$ is continuous with $e(\delta)-e(0^+)=O(\delta)$. Hence the edge law holds pointwise with the constants of Proposition~\ref{prop:profile},
\[
f(a-\delta)=\varphi(\delta)\big(1+O(L^{-2})\big),
\]
while the two-derivative form holds for $\varphi+\psi$ and fails for $e$ exactly at the echo points, where $f$ has the cusps of its echoes.
\item[(iv)] (\emph{the laws}) $\lambda'(a)=-2|A_0|^2$ at every point of differentiability of $\lambda$, and under RH
\[\lim_{T\to\infty}T\sum_{|\gamma|>T}|F(\gamma)|^2=-\lambda'(a)/\pi .\]
\end{enumerate}
Since $\lambda$ is absolutely continuous (Lemma~\ref{lem:exist}, Proposition~\ref{prop:lip}) and the entries are countable, $\lambda(a_2)-\lambda(a_1)=-2\int_{a_1}^{a_2}|A_0(a)|^2\,da$ for all $a_1<a_2$.
\end{theorem}
\begin{proof}
Throughout, $w=\tilde\sigma_a/\tilde\sigma_\infty=(1-x)R$ with $x=\sum_d2c_d\cos(td)/\tilde\sigma_\infty$ and $R=\prod_j(t^2+t_j^2)/(t^2-t_j^2)$ the rational factor of the real zeros, and $m_\pm=\sigma_{\infty,\pm}/\sigma_{a,\pm}=\exp(-P_\pm[\log w])$, bounded above and below together with $1/m_\pm$ (Proposition~\ref{prop:window}(i)), analytic in the respective half-planes; $1/\sigma_{a,\pm}=m_\pm/\sigma_{\infty,\pm}$, and the kernels of $m_+$, $m_-$ are supported on $u\ge0$, $u\le0$. The Hilbert transform is taken through the kernel, as in Lemma~\ref{lem:hilbmod}; on the functions below it agrees with the boundary values of the Cauchy integrals that define $\sigma_{a,\pm}$ in Lemma~\ref{lem:factor}.

\emph{Step 1: the factors on the line, \eqref{eq:mline}.} Let $\chi$ be the cutoff of Lemma~\ref{lem:frozen}. On $|t|\ge2t_1$, $s(t)\ge s(2t_1)>X$, so Proposition~\ref{prop:lattice}(i) gives $\log w=\sum_{u\in\Lambda_a}\beta_u(s)e^{itu}+\log R$, absolutely and uniformly convergent; hence on the whole line
\[
\log w=\sum_{u\in\Lambda_a}g_ue^{itu}+r_0,\qquad g_u=\chi\,\beta_u(\tilde\sigma_\infty(\cdot)),\qquad r_0=(1-\chi)\log w+\chi\log R ,
\]
with $\sum_u\sup|g_u|\le-\log(1-X/s(2t_1))$. By Lemma~\ref{lem:frozen}, $|g_u^{(i)}|\le M_u(1+|t|)^{-i}$, $i\le6$, $M_u=\mu|\beta_u|(s_*)$, so Lemma~\ref{lem:hilbmod} applies to each $g_ue^{i\cdot u}$, $u\ne0$, with $j=3$: $P_\pm[g_ue^{i\cdot u}]=\tfrac12g_ue^{itu}\pm\tfrac i2\mathcal H[g_ue^{i\cdot u}]=\tfrac12(1\pm\mathrm{sign}\,u)\,g_ue^{itu}\pm\tfrac i2E_u$, and $P_\pm[g_0]=\tfrac12g_0\pm\tfrac i2\mathcal H[g_0]$. The interchange of $P_\pm$ with the sum over $u$ is dominated convergence on the kernels, $\sum_uM_u<\infty$. Therefore
\[
-P_\pm[\log w]=-\tfrac12g_0\mp\tfrac i2\mathcal H[g_0]-\sum_{\pm u>0}g_ue^{itu}+e_\pm,\qquad e_\pm=\mp\tfrac i2\sum_{u\ne0}E_u-P_\pm[r_0].
\]
On $|t|\ge8t_1$ one has $g_u=\beta_u(s)$, $e^{-g_0/2}=(s/G(s))^{1/2}$ (Proposition~\ref{prop:lattice}(i), $\beta_0=\log G-\log s$) and $\exp(-\sum_{\pm u>0}\beta_u(s)e^{itu})=\sum_{\pm u\ge0}\rho_u(s)e^{itu}$ (Proposition~\ref{prop:lattice}(iii)), which is \eqref{eq:mline} with $1+\varepsilon_\pm=e^{e_\pm}$. The bounds on $e_\pm$ for $|t|\ge8t_1$, $j\le3$:
\begin{itemize}
\item[(a)] $\big|\sum_{u\ne0}E_u^{(j)}(t)\big|\le e_j|t|^{-1-j}\sum_{u\ne0}M_u/|u|\le B_j|t|^{-1-j}$, $B_j=2e_j\mu\,\big(-\log(1-Xe^{2a}/s_*)\big)$, since $|u_w|>\tfrac12e^{-2a|w|}$ (Lemma~\ref{lem:dioph}(i)) gives $\sum_{u\ne0}|\beta_u|(s_*)/|u|\le\sum_k\frac1k\sum_{|w|=k}c_ws_*^{-k}\,2e^{2ak}=-2\log(1-Xe^{2a}/s_*)$; this is where $\alpha(s_*)>1$ is used.
\item[(b)] $r_0=\chi\log R$ on $|t|\ge8t_1$, and for $|t|\ge2t_1$ (above the real zeros) $\log R=\sum_j\log\big(1+2t_j^2/(t^2-t_j^2)\big)$ satisfies $|(\log R)^{(j)}|\le r_jC_R(1+|t|)^{-2-j}$ with absolute constants $r_j$; so $|P_\pm[r_0]^{(j)}|\le\tfrac12r_jC_R|t|^{-2-j}+\tfrac12|\mathcal H[r_0]^{(j)}|$.
\item[(c)] $\mathcal H[(1-\chi)\log w]^{(j)}(t)=\frac{j!}\pi\int\frac{(1-\chi)\log w(\tau)}{(t-\tau)^{j+1}}d\tau$ with $|\tau|\le4t_1\le|t|/2$, so its modulus is at most $\frac{j!}\pi\,8t_1C_w\,(2/|t|)^{j+1}$.
\item[(d)] $\chi\log R$ is a symbol of order $-2$ with $M=rC_R$, $r=\max_{j\le6}r_j\max_l\kappa_l$ (Leibniz with $\chi$), so by Lemma~\ref{lem:symbolkernel}(ii) its kernel $\check r$ is $C^3$ on $\xi\ne0$ with the stated bounds, and $\mathcal H[\chi\log R]^{(j)}(t)=\int(-i\,\mathrm{sign}\,\xi)(i\xi)^j\check r(\xi)e^{it\xi}d\xi$; integrating by parts $j+1$ times on each half-line, the boundary terms at $0$ vanish because $\partial^m[\xi^j\check r]\to0$ as $\xi\to0$ for $m\le j$ (each term is $\xi^{j-i}\check r^{(m-i)}$ with $|\check r^{(m-i)}|\le c'M|\xi|^{1-(m-i)}(1+\log(1/|\xi|))$), and
\[
|\mathcal H[\chi\log R]^{(j)}(t)|\le|t|^{-1-j}\int\big|\partial^{j+1}[\xi^j\check r]\big|d\xi\le h_j\,rC_R\,|t|^{-1-j},\qquad h_j=\sum_{m=1}^{j+1}\binom{j+1}m\frac{j!}{(m-1)!}\big(4c_m'+c_m\big)
\]
(the two integrals, near and far, bounded as in the proof of Lemma~\ref{lem:hilbmod}).
\end{itemize}
Collecting, $|e_\pm^{(j)}(t)|\le\varepsilon_j'\,t_1|t|^{-1-j}$ on $|t|\ge8t_1$ with $\varepsilon_j'=\tfrac12\big(B_j+r_jC_R+j!\,2^{j+4}C_w/\pi+h_jrC_R\big)$ (we used $t_1\ge1$ and $|t|\ge8t_1$ to absorb the $|t|^{-2-j}$ term). Since $|e_\pm|\le\varepsilon_0'/8\le1$ once $t_1$ is also taken with $\varepsilon_0'\le8$, which only enlarges $t_1$ (the constants $B_j$ depend on $s_*$ and $a$ alone, $C_w$, $C_R$ on $a$ alone), Fa\`a di Bruno's formula for $e^{e_\pm}-1$ gives $|\varepsilon_\pm^{(j)}|\le\varepsilon_jt_1|t|^{-1-j}$ with $\varepsilon_j=e\,\mathcal B_j(\varepsilon_0',\dots,\varepsilon_j')$, $\mathcal B_j$ the complete Bell polynomial ($\varepsilon_0=e\varepsilon_0'$). This is (i).

\emph{Step 2: the amplitude, (ii).} By Proposition~\ref{prop:window}(ii), as in \eqref{eq:edgeform},
\[
A_a(t)=t\,P_-\big[(\hat H_--e^{-2ita}\Phi_-)/\sigma_{a,+}\big]-t\,P_+\big[e^{-2ita}\Phi_-/\sigma_{a,+}\big].
\]
The near part is as in Corollary~\ref{cor:edgeform}: $P_-[1/((t-ib)\sigma_{a,+})]=1/((t-ib)\sigma_{a,+}(ib))$ exactly, $\sigma_{a,+}(ib)>0$, and the smooth interior source $\psi$ gives $-\frac i{2\pi}\int\psi/\sigma_{a,+}+O(\log t\log\log t/t)$, the derivative of $1/\sigma_{a,+}$ being $O(\log\log t)$ (Proposition~\ref{prop:window}(i)). For a far source $\mathrm{src}\in L^1\cap L^2$ supported in $u\le-\delta_0$ with transform $\tilde\Phi$, $\tilde\Phi/\sigma_{a,+}$ is the transform of $r=\mathrm{src}*k_{a,+}$ with $k_{a,+}=k_{m_+}*k_{\infty,+}$ the kernel of $1/\sigma_{a,+}$, and $P_+$ restricts $r$ to $u>0$. By (i) and Lemma~\ref{lem:symbolkernel}, on $\xi>0$ the kernel $k_{m_+}$ is the sum over $u\in\Lambda_a$, $u\ge0$, of the kernels $k_u(\xi-u)$ of the symbols $(s/G)^{1/2}e^{-\frac i2\mathcal H[g_0]}\rho_u(s)$, each an $L^1$ function away from its point $u$ with $|k_u^{(m)}(\eta)|\le c_m\mu'\rho_u(s_*)|\eta|^{-1-m}\min(1,|\eta|^{-1})$, plus the kernel of the remainder $(\dots)\varepsilon_+$, which is $C^2$ with $|\partial^m|\le C\varepsilon_2t_1|\eta|^{-m}$ by the same lemma applied to a symbol of order $-1$; the sum converges in $L^1_{\mathrm{loc}}(\xi>0)$ because $\sum_u\rho_u(s_*)<\infty$. So $r(u)$, $u>0$, is $C^1$ off the points $v-\delta_0'$, $v\in\Lambda_a$, $\delta_0'\ge\delta_0$ the positions of the singularities of $\mathrm{src}$; for the far edge, $\mathrm{src}=\mathrm{far}$ is singular at $-2a$ alone, so $r$ is singular at $v-2a$ for $v\in\Lambda_a$, $v>2a$, with the singularity of $\mathrm{far}*k_{\infty,+}$ (the force $-CL^{1/2}$ convolved with the kernel of $1/\sigma_{\infty,+}$, transform $O(1/t)$) weighted by $\rho_v$. The singular points within $\varepsilon$ of $0^+$ come from the words within $\varepsilon$ of $2a$, of total weight $O(s^{-3})$ for $\varepsilon<d_a$ at a non-resonant support and $O(s^{-N})$ as $\varepsilon\to0$ (Proposition~\ref{prop:lattice}(iii)); at a resonant support the words with $u_v=2a$ put a singularity of weight $\rho_{2a}(s)=O(s^{-2})$ at $0^+$ itself, which the integration by parts below returns as a slowly varying relative correction $O(s^{-2})$ to the amplitude. Integrating by parts as in Corollary~\ref{cor:edgeform}, $-t\,P_+[\tilde\Phi/\sigma_{a,+}]=-ir(0^+)+\sum_{v>2a}m_v(t)e^{it(v-2a)}+O(t^{-1/2})$ with $|m_v(t)|\le C\rho_v(s)$, $r(0^+)$ real. (\emph{Sketch density:} the uniformity, over the infinitely many singular points $v-2a$ accumulating at $0^+$, of the integration by parts that produces the $m_v$, and the $O(t^{-1/2})$, are asserted here at the density of Corollary~\ref{cor:edgeform}'s proof; see the verification notes.) This is (ii), with $A_0=A'/\sigma_{a,+}(ib)-\frac i{2\pi}\int\psi/\sigma_{a,+}-i\sum r(0^+)$ purely imaginary and the $O((\log t)^{-2})$ collecting $(s/G)^{1/2}-1$, the slowly varying phase and the smooth remainders; $A_a(-t)=-\overline{A_a(t)}$ is Proposition~\ref{prop:window}(ii).

\emph{Step 3: the edge, (iii).} By Proposition~\ref{prop:window}(ii) and (i), on $|t|\ge8t_1$,
\[
\tilde F=\frac{A_a\,m_-}{t\sigma_{\infty,-}}+\frac{e^{-2ita}\Phi_-}{\tilde\sigma_a},\qquad m_-=m_-^{(0)}(1+\varepsilon_-),\qquad m_-^{(0)}=\Big(\frac s{G}\Big)^{1/2}e^{\frac i2\mathcal H[g_0]}\sum_{u\le0}\rho_u(s)e^{itu}.
\]
Write $\hat\varphi$ for the transform of the model $\varphi\chi_0$ of Proposition~\ref{prop:profile} ($\chi_0$ its cutoff), $\hat e=\sum_{u<0}\hat e_u$ with $\hat e_u=\rho_u(s)e^{itu}A_0(s/G)^{1/2}e^{\frac i2\mathcal H[g_0]}(1+\varepsilon_-)/(t\sigma_{\infty,-})$ (the $u<0$ terms, with their share of $\varepsilon_-$), and decompose
\begin{align*}
\tilde F-\hat\varphi-\hat e&=D_1+D_2+D_3+D_4,\\
D_1&=\frac{A_0(s/G)^{1/2}e^{\frac i2\mathcal H[g_0]}}{t\sigma_{\infty,-}}-\hat\varphi,\qquad D_2=\frac{A_0(s/G)^{1/2}e^{\frac i2\mathcal H[g_0]}\varepsilon_-}{t\sigma_{\infty,-}},\\
D_3&=\frac{(A_a-A_0)\,m_-}{t\sigma_{\infty,-}},\qquad D_4=\frac{e^{-2ita}\Phi_-}{\tilde\sigma_a},
\end{align*}
all four extended by the exact difference on $|t|<8t_1$, where they are bounded (every function involved is). Each $D_i$ is inverted by Lemma~\ref{lem:inversion}, with the inverse transform read at the position $-\delta$.

$D_1$: $(s/G)^{1/2}=1+|b|^2/(2s^2)+O(s^{-4})$ (Proposition~\ref{prop:lattice}(iv)) and $e^{\frac i2\mathcal H[g_0]}=1-\frac{i\pi|b|^2}{2\ell^3}+O(\ell^{-5})$ (Lemma~\ref{lem:frozen}), both with derivatives gaining $t^{-j}$ (Cauchy's estimate on the disc of Lemma~\ref{lem:frozen} for the first, the differentiated expansion for the second); and $A_0/(t\sigma_{\infty,-})-\hat\varphi=O(t^{-1}\ell^{-7/2})$ with derivatives $O(t^{-1-j}\ell^{-7/2})$, $j\le3$ (the factor and matching paragraphs of Proposition~\ref{prop:profile}). So $|D_1^{(j)}|\le D_0|t|^{-1-j}\ell^{-5/2}$ and Lemma~\ref{lem:inversion} with $\Lambda=\ell^{-5/2}$ gives $\psi_1^{(k)}=O(\delta^{-k}L^{-5/2})$, $k=1,2$: this is the term $|b|^2/(2\ell^2)$ of Corollary~\ref{cor:tree}(ii), and the exponent $5/2$ of the theorem is its exponent.

$D_2$: by (i), $|\varepsilon_-^{(j)}|\le\varepsilon_jt_1|t|^{-1-j}$, so $|D_2^{(j)}|\le D_0't_1|t|^{-2-j}\ell^{-1/2}$ and Lemma~\ref{lem:inversion} with $\Lambda(t)=t_1t^{-1}\ell(t)^{-1/2}$ (doubling, $\Lambda_0=2$) gives $\psi_2^{(k)}=O(t_1\delta^{1-k})$: bounded for $k=1$, $O(\delta^{-1})$ for $k=2$, inside the class.

The echo part: for $u<0$ the function $\hat e_u$ is $e^{itu}$ times $D_u=\rho_u(s)A_0(s/G)^{1/2}e^{\frac i2\mathcal H[g_0]}(1+\varepsilon_-)/(t\sigma_{\infty,-})$, and by Lemma~\ref{lem:frozen} ($\rho_u(s)\le\rho_u(s_*)s_*/s$ with the Cauchy bounds for the derivatives) $|D_u^{(j)}|\le D_0''\rho_u(s_*)s_*|t|^{-1-j}\ell^{-3/2}$, $j\le3$; Lemma~\ref{lem:inversion} with $\Lambda=\ell^{-3/2}$ gives a real $C^2$ function $\tilde e_u$ on $x\ne0$ with $|\tilde e_u^{(k)}(x)|\le C\rho_u(s_*)s_*|x|^{-k}L(|x|)^{-3/2}$, $k=1,2$, and $e_u(\delta)=\tilde e_u(\delta-|u|)$ is the cusp of weight $\rho_u$ at distance $|u|$ from the edge; $e_u$ is continuous and bounded by $\|D_u\|_1/2\pi=O(\rho_u(s_*)s_*)$ since $D_u=O(t^{-1}\ell^{-3/2})$ is integrable, and $\int_0^\delta|e_u'|\le C\rho_u(s_*)s_*\min\big(1,\,2\delta/|u|\big)$ (for $|u|\ge2\delta$ the derivative is bounded by $C\rho_us_*\cdot2/|u|$ on $(0,\delta)$; for $|u|<2\delta$ the integral of $|x|^{-1}L^{-3/2}$ over $|x|\le3\delta$ is at most $4L(3\delta)^{-1/2}\le4$). Summing, $e=\sum_{u<0}e_u$ converges uniformly, is continuous, and
\[
|e(\delta)-e(0^+)|\le Cs_*\Big(2\delta\sum_{u\ne0}\frac{\rho_u(s_*)}{|u|}+\sum_{0<|u|<2\delta}\rho_u(s_*)\Big)\le Cs_*\Big(\frac{4\delta}{1-X/s_*}\sum_k\Big(\frac{Xe^{2a}}{s_*}\Big)^k+\frac{(4\delta)^{\alpha(s_*)}}{1-X/s_*}\Big)=O(\delta),
\]
by Lemma~\ref{lem:dioph}(i) and Proposition~\ref{prop:lattice}(iii), with $\alpha(s_*)>1$. This is the structure of the echo part stated in (iii).

$D_3$ and $D_4$ (\emph{sketch density}): $A_a-A_0=M_a+O((\log t)^{-2})$ by (ii), and $m_-$ is bounded with the expansion \eqref{eq:mline}. The $O((\log t)^{-2})$ piece times $m_-^{(0)}$ is a sum over $u\le0$ of $e^{itu}$ times a function $O(t^{-1}\ell^{-5/2})$: its $u=0$ term is in the class by Lemma~\ref{lem:inversion} once its derivatives gain $t^{-j}$, and its $u<0$ terms are cusps at the echo points of the same type as $e_u$, with the same summability; the mirror term $M_am_-/(t\sigma_{\infty,-})$ is the sum over $v>2a$, $u\le0$ of $m_v\rho_ue^{it(v-2a+u)}/(t\sigma_{\infty,-})$, copies of the profile at the positions $v-2a+u\in\Lambda_a-2a$ inside the window, of which those within $2\delta$ of the edge have weight $O(L^{-3})$ (Proposition~\ref{prop:lattice}(iii); at a resonant support the words with $v+u=2a$ sit at the edge and are the $O(s^{-2})$ relative correction of Step~2), and those with $v-2a+u>0$ would sit outside the window and cancel against $D_4$, the far source through the kernel of $1/\tilde\sigma_a=m_+m_-/\tilde\sigma_\infty$, which is smooth on $(-2a,0]$ away from $\Lambda_a-2a$ with the same weights near $0$. The derivative bounds with the gain $t^{-j}$ for $A_a-A_0$, the cancellation, and the uniformity over the lattice are the points at which this step is a research sketch; the verification notes record them. Granting them, the inverse transforms of $D_3$ and $D_4$ are in the class, and $\psi=\psi_1+\psi_2+\psi_3+\psi_4$ satisfies $\psi^{(k)}=O(\delta^{-k}L^{-5/2})$, $k=1,2$.

Finally $f(a^-)=0$ (Theorem~\ref{thm:edge}) and $\varphi(0^+)=0$ give $e(0^+)+\psi(0^+)=0$, hence $f(a-\delta)-\varphi(\delta)=[e(\delta)-e(0^+)]+\int_0^\delta\psi'=O(\delta)+O(L^{-5/2})$, which is the pointwise law $\varphi(1+O(L^{-2}))$. This is (iii).

\emph{Step 4: the laws, (iv) (sketch density).} Proposition~\ref{prop:flux} is applied with $u=(\varphi+e+\psi)\chi$ near the right edge. The parts $\varphi$ and $\psi$ are as there. The echo part enters through the leak of $(xe)'$: the transform of $(xe_u)'$ is $O(\rho_u(s)\ell^{-3/2})$ with the phase $e^{it(a-|u|)}$, its high-pass at $a+\delta$ is $O(\rho_u\ell_T^{-3/2}/(T(|u|+\delta)))$ after one integration by parts, and paired with $g_{\mathrm{out}}(a+\delta)=-CL(\delta)^{1/2}+O(1)$ over $0<\delta<\delta_0$ it contributes $O\big(\rho_u(s_*)\ell_T^{-3/2}(\log(1/|u|))^{3/2}/T\big)$ to $B_T^+$, summable over $u$ by $\log(1/|u|)\le2a|w|+\log2$ and $\sum_k(X/s_*)^kk^{3/2}<\infty$; so the echo part contributes $O(T^{-1}\ell_T^{-3/2})$ to $B_T$ and nothing to $B_\infty=a|A_0|^2$. Proposition~\ref{prop:tail} is applied on the transform side: by (i)--(iii), $F(t)=e^{ita}\tilde F$ with $|F|^2=4|A_0|^2|E_a(t)|^2\cos^2(\dots)/(t^2\tilde\sigma_\infty)\,(1+O(1/\log t))+O(t^{-2}(\log t)^{-3/2})$ and $E_a=\sum_{u\le0}\rho_u(s)e^{itu}$; the non-oscillating part of $|E_a|^2$ is $\sum_u\rho_u^2=1+O(s^{-2})$, and the oscillating parts have the frequencies $u-u'\in\Lambda_a\setminus\{0\}$ with weights $\rho_u\rho_{u'}$, for which Step~2 of that proof applies term by term, the integration by parts against $\sin(t(u-u'))/(u-u')$ costing $1/|u-u'|<2e^{2a(|w|+|w'|)}$ (Lemma~\ref{lem:dioph}(i)), summable since $\alpha(s_*)>1$; the slowly varying coefficients $\rho_u(s(t))$ have derivatives $O(\rho_u(s_*)/t)$ by Lemma~\ref{lem:frozen}. The two applications are written at the density of the proofs they extend; the verification notes record them. With them, (iv) follows as in Corollary~\ref{cor:tree}(v), and the last statement integrates $\lambda'$ over the supports that are not entries.
\end{proof}

\paragraph*{Verification notes.} The proof above was rewritten so that every estimate of Step~1 is a lemma with its constants, and the rewriting changed three things in the statement. First, the error of the factors is $O(t_1/t)$, not $O(t^{-1/2})$: the earlier version split the lattice sum at $|u|=t^{-1/2}$ under $\alpha(s_1)\ge1$; the kernel form of the Hilbert transform (Lemmas~\ref{lem:symbolkernel} and~\ref{lem:hilbmod}) gives the error $e_k M_u/(|u|t^{k+1})$ for each frequency with all derivatives gaining, and the sum converges exactly when $\alpha(s_*)>1$. Second, the frequency $u=0$ of $\log w$, the constant term $\log(G/s)$, has a Hilbert transform that is not small in $1/t$ but slowly varying, $-\pi|b|^2/\ell^3$: it is a real phase of the factors, omitted in the earlier statement of \eqref{eq:mline}, and it enters the edge profile as a relative correction $O(\ell^{-3})$, inside the remainder class. Third, the edge law with two derivatives in the pointwise sense of Proposition~\ref{prop:tail} does not hold at a support where the echo lattice is dense: the minimizer has at every lattice point $u<0$ the cusp of its echo, with the derivative $\rho_u|x|^{-1}L^{-3/2}$ at distance $x$ from it, and the lattice accumulates at the edge; the correct statement separates the echo part $e$, whose weights within $\varepsilon$ of the edge sum to $O(\varepsilon^{\alpha})$, and the pointwise law $f(a-\delta)=\varphi(\delta)(1+O(L^{-2}))$ survives because $e(\delta)-e(0^+)=O(\delta)$. Three points of the proof remain at the density of a research sketch and are the next items of the verification: (1) in Step~2, the uniformity over the lattice points $v-2a\to0^+$ of the far-source feedback, which produces the mirror amplitudes $m_v$ and the $O(t^{-1/2})$; (2) in Step~3, the derivative bounds with the gain $t^{-j}$ for $A_a-A_0$, and the cancellation between the mirror terms that would land outside the window and the far source $D_4$ through the kernel of $1/\tilde\sigma_a$; (3) in Step~4, the two extensions, of the flux pairing of Proposition~\ref{prop:flux} and of the zero sums of Proposition~\ref{prop:tail}, to the echo part, which are written above with their estimates but not as separate lemmas. Items (1) and (2) are statements about the kernel of $1/\sigma_{a,+}$ on $(0,\varepsilon)$, where Lemma~\ref{lem:symbolkernel} and Proposition~\ref{prop:lattice}(iii) give the ingredients; item (3) is bookkeeping of the type of Corollary~\ref{cor:tree}. Below $a_\infty$, where the lattice is finite, none of the three arises and the theorem is Corollary~\ref{cor:tree}(ii), (v).


\begin{corollary}[the reduction, unconditional on the boundary law]\label{cor:unconditional}
Assume (H$_\infty$) and the inequality \eqref{eq:theinequality} at almost every support $a\ge a_Z$. Then Conjecture~\ref{conj:A} holds with the constant $c$ of \eqref{eq:theinequality} and the Riemann Hypothesis follows.
\end{corollary}
\begin{proof}
Theorem~\ref{thm:everysupport}(iv) gives $\lambda'=-2|A_0|^2$ at almost every $a\ge a_Z$, so \eqref{eq:theinequality} reads $\lambda'\ge-c\Tst\lambda$ almost everywhere on $[a_Z,\infty)$; with the absolute continuity of $\lambda$ this is Conjecture~\ref{conj:A} with $a_0=a_Z$, and Proposition~\ref{prop:AimpliesRH} applies.
\end{proof}

Three remarks. First, the theorem is about $\lambda'$, not about the onset: the asymptotics it establishes begin where the chain expansion converges with room, $s>Xe^{2a}$, which is a height $2\pi e^{Xe^{2a}}$, astronomically far above the horizon for $a\ge1$; the measured onset of the tail law, a few horizons at every support, is governed by the band the edge sees (Proposition~\ref{prop:spectral}), and its uniformity in $a$ is item~3 of the program, untouched here. Second, below $a_\infty$ the window's representation and the tree's represent the same function, and the theorem recovers Corollary~\ref{cor:tree}(ii) by a different route: the tree's effective symbol absorbs the echoes exactly from the horizon, the window's symbol does so only asymptotically, through the mirror chains and the slowly varying factor $(s/G)^{1/2}$, and the two agree on the constants because $\rho_0=1$ and $(s/G)^{1/2}-1=O(s^{-2})$. Third, the resonant supports, $2a\in\Lambda_a$: a chain from the far edge lands exactly on the near edge, with the weight $\rho_{2a}(L)=O(L^{-2})$ of a word of length at least two; it is a relative correction of that order to the edge amplitude, inside the remainder class, so the theorem holds there with the same constants, and the only supports it leaves out are the entries, where $\lambda$ has its soft kinks.

\begin{computation}[the mirror chains, and what the $K$-mode minimizers resolve]\label{comp:mirror}
\sloppy \texttt{rh\_mirror\_chains.py} and \texttt{rh\_band\_phase.py}, logs \texttt{mirror\_chains\_a0.6.log} and \texttt{band\_phase\_a*.log} in \texttt{data/}. The $K$-mode minimizer is a polynomial of degree $2K-1$ on $[-a,a]$: it resolves the edge to $\delta\approx a/(2K-1)^2$, heights of order $(2K-1)^2/a$, but the interior cusps only to heights of order $(2K-1)/a$. (i) At $a=0.6$, against the edge FEM of Computation~\ref{comp:tree06} (interior resolved to $34\,\Tst$), the $K$-mode transform ($K=48$, degree $95$, $(2K-1)/a=7.6\,\Tst$) agrees to $0.07\%$, $0.3\%$, $0.6\%$ at $2$, $3$, $5\,\Tst$ and departs at $7\,\Tst$ ($2.9\%$) to $15$--$20\%$ at $10$--$30\,\Tst$. (ii) On $81$ nulls at $10$--$30\,\Tst$, the residual $r+\theta_\infty-\kappa/t$ regressed on $(\alpha_d\sin td+\beta_d\cos td)/\tilde\sigma_\infty$: the FEM gives $\alpha_2=0.502$, $\alpha_3=0.635$, $|\beta_d|\le0.005$ against the echo form's $c_2=0.490$, $c_3=0.634$ (rms $0.139\to0.022$); the polynomial gives $\alpha_2=-0.003$, $\alpha_3=0.16$ (rms $0.055\to0.048$). The $K$-mode minimizers carry the edge alone above a few horizons, and above $(2K-1)/a$ ($3.4$, $2.9$, $2.4\,\Tst$ at $a=1.0$, $1.25$, $1.5$) their transforms say nothing about the pointwise structure of the minimizer; the envelope, a mean, they get right to the few per cent of $\sigma_{\mathrm{eff}}/\tilde\sigma_\infty-1$. On $3112$ nulls at $3$--$400\,\Tst$ of the $K=64$ minimizer at $a=0.8$ ($(2K-1)/a=5.1\,\Tst$, $(2K-1)^2/a=650\,\Tst$) the archimedean phase fits to rms $0.008$--$0.017$ from $10\,\Tst$ on, with $|\alpha_d|\le0.0011$ for the three entries, and the window's symbol phase to $0.10$--$0.16$; the local amplitude $\langle4t^2\tilde\sigma_a S^2\rangle/(-\lambda')$ over one period is $1.04\pm0.04$ at $47$--$100\,\Tst$ and $0.95\pm0.03$ at $200$--$400\,\Tst$; on $5755$ nulls of the $K=80$ minimizer at $a=1.0$ (edge resolved to $540\,\Tst$) the archimedean phase fits to rms $0.010$--$0.017$ from $30$ to $400\,\Tst$, while on $10$--$30\,\Tst$ the residual is $0.31$ and fits no echo form: between $(2K-1)/a$ and a few times that height the polynomial's interior error still reaches its transform. The $K=200$ minimizer at $a=1.0$ (\texttt{kmode\_K200/coefs\_1.0.json}; $\lambda_{200}=1.4361\times10^{-26}$, $a\lambda'=-2.4201\times10^{-24}$, against $1.4820\times10^{-26}$ and $-2.5169\times10^{-24}$ at $K=80$; interior resolved to $(2K-1)/a=8.6\,\Tst$) on the same $63$ nulls at $3$--$8\,\Tst$ (\texttt{band\_phase\_a1.0\_K200\_3-8.log}): the residual against the archimedean phase is $0.56$, the regression on the five entries gives $\alpha_2=0.38$, $\alpha_3=0.51$, $\alpha_4=0.11$, $\alpha_5=0.41$, $\alpha_7=0.32$ against $c_d=0.49$, $0.63$, $0.35$, $0.72$, $0.74$, all now with the sign of the echo form and $60$--$80\%$ of its size where $K=80$ gave signs at random, but the residual falls only to $0.54$: at $a=1$ the band of the window's symbol, with $87$ real zeros up to $33\,\Tst$, dominates these heights, and the echo form is not the right description there; what the $K=200$ run adds is that the interior structure the polynomial resolves does carry the entries' frequencies with the predicted signs. This is the edge of the minimizer, at heights where the sampling identity of Proposition~\ref{prop:defect} and the tail law were measured on these functions; it is not their interior. (iii) The window's symbol on the FEM nulls, $114$ at $5$--$34\,\Tst$: $\tilde\sigma_a$ has $11$ real zeros below $2.2\,\Tst$ and its band top is $2.9\,\Tst$ ($X=2.249$). The residual with the phase $\theta_a$ of $\tilde\sigma_a$ has rms $0.0365$ (tree form $0.0009$, archimedean phase $0.145$); regressed on $(p\sin+q\cos)(t(v-2a))/\tilde\sigma_\infty^2$ over the three two-step mirror words $v=d+d'>2a$ ($\log4$, $\log6$, $\log9$; $v-2a=0.186$, $0.592$, $0.997$), it drops to $0.0186$ with the amplitudes $0.190$, $0.514$, $0.348$ against the chain weights $\sum c_dc_{d'}=0.240$, $0.622$, $0.402$, a common factor $0.8$; by height band the residual falls from $0.046$ to $0.030$ ($5$--$10$ to $20$--$34\,\Tst$) and after the fit from $0.033$ to $0.013$, while $(X/s)^2$ falls from $0.49$ to $0.25$ and $(X/s)^3$, the three-step chains left out, from $0.34$ to $0.13$. The same regression on the tree's residual finds nothing ($0.002$, $0.001$, $0.002$).
\end{computation}


\subsection{The global and the local}\label{sec:globallocal}

Two objects in the companion work are global. The complete Weil form $W(q,q)$ of \cite{michaels-atlas} is the form of this paper on all of $C_c^\infty(\R)$, every support at once, and its positivity is the Hypothesis; the atlas organizes it through the Suzuki metric and its defect $E_N$ over the first $N$ zeros, and proves local support theorems, the latest being the Checkpoint~20 certificate on the odd sector up to $a=3/8$. The M\"obius energy of \cite{michaels-mobius},
\[
R(N)=\sum_{m,n\le N}\frac{\mu(m)\mu(n)}{\max(m,n)}=\int_1^N\frac{M(t)^2}{t^2}\,dt+\frac{M(N)^2}N,\qquad M(t)=\sum_{n\le t}\mu(n),
\]
is the Green energy of the M\"obius history to $N$, the norm of $\sum_{n\le N}\mu(n)n^{-s}$ on the critical line in $L^2(dt/(\tfrac14+t^2))$, and its growth is the Hypothesis: $R(N)\ll_\varepsilon N^\varepsilon$ if and only if RH, and a zero of real part $\beta_0>\tfrac12$ forces $R(N)>N^{2\beta_0-1-\delta}$ infinitely often \cite[Theorem~12.6]{michaels-mobius}. The floor $\lambda(a)$ is local, one window at a time. This section records what connects them, and in what sense the local statements of this paper tolerate windows that close late.

\begin{proposition}[the window form in the atlas]\label{prop:atlas}
The odd arithmetic operator of \cite[CP19 (0.5)]{michaels-atlas}, $A_a^-=C_a-\log(2\pi)-\Pi_a-K_{\alpha,a}-2|s_a\rangle\langle s_a|$ on $L^2_{\mathrm{odd}}(-a,a)$, is the operator of the form $Q$ of Section~\ref{sec:form}: its archimedean part is Weil's kernel $\kappa(t)=e^{-t/2}/(1-e^{-2t})$ in the form $C_a-\log(2\pi)-K_{\alpha,a}$, its prime part the shifts $\Pi_aq=\sum_{\log n<2a}b_n[q(\cdot+\log n)+q(\cdot-\log n)]$ with $b_n=\Lambda(n)/\sqrt n=c_n$, and its polar part $-2|s_a\rangle\langle s_a|$ with $s_a=\sinh(x/2)$; so $Q_a^-(q)=\langle q,A_a^-q\rangle=Q(q)$ on the odd window, and the Checkpoint~20 inequality $Q_a^-(q)>\tfrac{5278}{10^6}\|q\|_2^2$ for $0<a\le\tfrac38$ \cite[CP20 (0.1)]{michaels-atlas} is the statement
\[
\lambda(a)\ \ge\ 5.278\times10^{-3}\qquad(0<a\le\tfrac38),
\]
a certified lower bound on the floor, with an exact rational certificate. Against it, the $40$-mode minimum is an upper bound: $5.28\times10^{-3}\le\lambda(\tfrac38)\le\lambda_{40}(\tfrac38)=3.14\times10^{-2}$ (\texttt{floor\_grid\_K40.csv}), and $\lambda_{40}=6.5\times10^{-2}$ at $a=0.35$, $9.8\times10^{-3}$ at $0.4125$.
\end{proposition}
\begin{proof}
The identification is term by term: the Weil distribution's archimedean part has the kernel $\kappa$ with the local singularity $1/(2|t|)$, which is the kernel of $\log|D|$, so that $\log|D|-\log(2\pi)-K_\alpha$, $\alpha=\kappa-1/(2|t|)$, with the atlas's finite part and gamma correction, is the operator with the multiplier $\Ree\psi(\tfrac14+\tfrac{it}2)-\log\pi$ of Section~\ref{sec:form}; the prime shifts are the terms $-2c_n\cos(t\log n)$ of $\Psi$ in the position representation, and the polar term is $-2P(f)^2$. The bounds are those of the two documents.
\end{proof}

The number both sides read is $\delta_{\max}=\sup_\rho(\Ree\rho-\tfrac12)$.

\begin{theorem}[two readings of $\delta_{\max}$]\label{thm:tworeadings}
Write $\log^+x=\max(\log x,0)$.
\begin{enumerate}
\item[(i)] Unconditionally, $\displaystyle\limsup_{N\to\infty}\frac{\log^+R(N)}{2\log N}=\delta_{\max}$.
\item[(ii)] Under the hypotheses of Theorem~\ref{thm:plane}(ii) and (iii), $\displaystyle\limsup_{a\to\infty}\frac{\log^+(-\lambda(a))}{2a}=\delta_{\max}$.
\end{enumerate}
So, in the common variable $a=\log N$, the global energy of the M\"obius history to $N$ and the local floor of the window of half-length $\log N$ grow at the same exponential rate, $2\delta_{\max}$, and both rates vanish if and only if the Hypothesis holds.
\end{theorem}
\begin{proof}
(ii) is Theorem~\ref{thm:plane}. (i) If $\delta_{\max}=0$ then $R(N)\ll_\varepsilon N^\varepsilon$ by \cite[Theorem~12.6(iii)]{michaels-mobius}, so the limit is $0$. If $\delta_{\max}>0$, every zero of real part $\beta_0>\tfrac12$ gives $\limsup\log R(N)/\log N\ge2\beta_0-1-\delta$ for all $\delta>0$ by \cite[(47)]{michaels-mobius}, and the supremum over the zeros gives $\ge2\delta_{\max}$. For the upper bound put $\Theta=\tfrac12+\delta_{\max}$; the classical bound $M(x)\ll_\varepsilon x^{\Theta+\varepsilon}$ (Perron's formula with $1/\zeta(s)\ll|t|^\varepsilon$ on $\Ree s\ge\Theta+\varepsilon$, the Borel--Carath\'eodory argument of \cite[\S14.2]{titchmarsh} in the zero-free half-plane, as in \cite[\S14.25]{titchmarsh}) gives $R(N)\le N^{-1}M(N)^2+\sum_{k<N}M(k)^2k^{-2}\ll_\varepsilon N^{2\Theta-1+\varepsilon}=N^{2\delta_{\max}+\varepsilon}$.
\end{proof}

Two remarks. First, the detection scale. The window of half-length $a$ sees a zero $\rho_0=\tfrac12+\delta_0+i\gamma_0$ only when its quartet term outweighs the on-line zeros: in the proof of Theorem~\ref{thm:plane}(iii) the test function $\sinh(\delta_0u)\cos(\gamma_0u)\phi(u/a)$ has $\lambda(a)\le-4c_\phi e^{2\delta_0a}(1+o(1))+O(a^2)$ with $c_\phi\to1/(8\delta_0)$, so the floor is negative from a support $a_{\mathrm{det}}=O\big(\delta_0^{-1}\log(1/\delta_0)\big)$ on, which is exponentially late in $1/\delta_0$ in the variable $N=e^a$; and the M\"obius energy exceeds its on-line growth $C_M\log N$ only once $N^{\delta_0}\gg|\rho_0\zeta'(\rho_0)|\sqrt{C_M}$ \cite[Remark~15.3]{michaels-mobius}, the same scale. The two readings close at the same time, and a floor that is positive, or a M\"obius budget that is tame, up to any finite scale certifies nothing beyond it: this is the sense in which a window can take an incredibly long time to close, and it is the only sense in which the Hypothesis is harder than a finite computation. Second, the atlas's negative-witness transfer, $E_N\ge(1+\delta/6S(q,q))^2>1$ for $N\ge N_0$ once an odd test $q$ has $Q(q)=-\delta<0$ \cite[Current mathematical position]{michaels-atlas}, is made quantitative by Theorem~\ref{thm:plane}: the witness at support $a$ is the minimizer, with $\delta=-\lambda(a)\|f\|^2$ growing like $e^{2\delta_{\max}a}$; what the transfer to the Suzuki defect needs beyond this is the Suzuki norm $S(f,f)$ of the minimizer, which is not computed here.

The local statements of this paper tolerate late windows in a precise sense.

\begin{proposition}[late windows are allowed]\label{prop:late}
Let $e\ge0$ be locally integrable on $[a_Z,\infty)$. If
\[
\lambda'(a)\ \ge\ -\big(c\,\Tst(a)+e(a)\big)\,\lambda(a)\qquad\text{for almost every }a\ge a_Z ,
\]
then $\lambda>0$ on $[a_Z,\infty)$ and the Riemann Hypothesis holds. Under (H$_\infty$) the hypothesis is the inequality $2|A_0(a)|^2\le(c\Tst(a)+e(a))\lambda(a)$ at almost every support (Theorem~\ref{thm:everysupport}). Conversely, if the Hypothesis holds then $-\lambda'/\lambda$ is locally integrable on $[a_Z,\infty)$; so, given the absolute continuity of $\lambda$ and $\lambda(a_Z)>0$,
\[
\text{RH}\iff\lambda>0\text{ on }[a_Z,\infty)\iff-\lambda'/\lambda\in L^1_{\mathrm{loc}}[a_Z,\infty).
\]
\end{proposition}
\begin{proof}
Put $g=c\Tst+e$ and $u(a)=\lambda(a)\exp\int_{a_Z}^ag$, locally absolutely continuous with $u'=e^{\int g}(\lambda'+g\lambda)\ge0$ a.e., so $u$ is non-decreasing and $\lambda(a)\ge\lambda(a_Z)e^{-\int_{a_Z}^ag}>0$; Theorems~\ref{thm:zhu} and~\ref{thm:weil} conclude as in Proposition~\ref{prop:AimpliesRH}. Conversely, if $\lambda>0$ on $[a_Z,\infty)$ then $\log\lambda$ is absolutely continuous on every compact interval, $\lambda$ being absolutely continuous and bounded below there, so $(\log\lambda)'=\lambda'/\lambda\in L^1_{\mathrm{loc}}$; and if $\lambda$ first vanishes at some $a_0>a_Z$ then $\int_{a_Z}^{a}|\lambda'|/\lambda\ge\log\lambda(a_Z)-\log\lambda(a)\to\infty$ as $a\to a_0^-$, so $-\lambda'/\lambda$ is not integrable near $a_0$.
\end{proof}

The proposition says where the content of Conjecture~\ref{conj:A} lies. Positivity of the floor for all $a$ is equivalent to the local integrability of $-\lambda'/\lambda$ and says nothing about a rate; the conjecture, and the inequality \eqref{eq:theinequality}, assert the rate $c\Tst$, and a proof of that rate is what the structure of the minimizer might supply. Windows at which the rate is exceeded are permitted, as many as one likes, provided the excess is integrable: the entries, where the floor has its soft kinks and Theorem~\ref{thm:everysupport} does not apply, are countable and cost nothing; a support at which the onset of the tail law is late costs its excess $e(a)$ over the interval of lateness, and the integration pays it. What may not happen is a non-integrable excess, which is a sign change of the floor, which is a zero off the line.

Finally, the identification of Proposition~\ref{prop:atlas} places this paper's results inside the atlas's objects: the floor of the odd window is the lowest point of the atlas's $Q_a^-$; the edge law, the echo tree, the boundary law and the reduction describe the minimizer of that form at every support, where the atlas certifies its sign up to $a=3/8$; and the all-support comparison that the atlas leaves open, the sign of the complete form on every window, is the inequality \eqref{eq:theinequality} in the form of Proposition~\ref{prop:late}.


\subsection{Two transfers, and the blind spot}\label{sec:transfers}

Section~\ref{sec:globallocal} left two connections open, the transfer of the floor's negative value to the atlas's Suzuki defect, and the relation of the atlas's paired-prime matrices to the echo tree; and it raised the question of blind spots: at which heights, and at what order in the distance from the line, does a window see a zero off the line. The two transfers are done here, each with the quantity it leaves uncontrolled named; the blind spot is a theorem, and its map is computed.

\begin{proposition}[the witness in the Suzuki metric]\label{prop:suzuki}
In the notation of \cite[Appendix~B and CP9, \S B]{michaels-atlas}: $S(f,g)=\langle L_0f,L_0g\rangle$ is Suzuki's metric, positive definite on the form domain, with $\|L_0f\|_2\le\pi^{-1/2}M_A\|f'\|_1$ for $\operatorname{supp}f\subseteq[-A,A]$ (B.7), $M_A$ the local bound of Suzuki's feature; $E_N$ is the Suzuki defect on the determining ladder of the first $N$ zeros, non-decreasing in $N$, zero under RH; and the transfer (CP9 (D1)) takes a compact smooth odd $q$ with $W(q,q)=-\eta<0$ to $E_N\ge(1+\eta/3S(q,q))^2$ for $N\ge N_0(q)$. Let $a$ be a support with $\lambda(a)<0$, $f$ the minimizer, $\|f\|=1$, $V_a=\|f'\|_1$ its total variation. Then for every $\varepsilon>0$ there is $N_0$ with
\[
E_N\ \ge\ \Big(1+\frac{\pi(1-\varepsilon)\,|\lambda(a)|}{3\,M_{a+\varepsilon}^2\,V_a^2}\Big)^2\qquad(N\ge N_0).
\]
Under the hypotheses of Theorem~\ref{thm:plane}(iii), $|\lambda(a)|\ge e^{2\delta_{\max}a}/(2\delta_{\max})\,(1+o(1))$ at every large support, so the permanent floor of the defect grows with the support like $e^{2\delta_{\max}a}/(M_a^2V_a^2)$. The two quantities not controlled here are Suzuki's constant $M_a$ and the variation of the negative-branch minimizer, and the bound (B.7) is in any case far from sharp: under RH, $S(f,f)=W(f,f)=\lambda(a)$, of size $e^{-c\Tst}$, while $M_a^2V_a^2/\pi$ is of order one, so the quantity to compute is $S(f,f)$ itself, through Suzuki's feature along the critical line; on the computed branch $V_a=4\sup|f|$ exactly, with $\sup|f|=1.46$, $1.42$, $1.40$, $1.38$ at $a=0.6$, $0.8$, $1.0$, $1.25$ (Computation~\ref{comp:blind}).
\end{proposition}
\begin{proof}
Let $f_\varepsilon=f*\phi_\varepsilon$ with $\phi_\varepsilon$ an even mollifier of width $\varepsilon$: odd, smooth, supported in $[-a-\varepsilon,a+\varepsilon]$, with $\|f_\varepsilon'\|_1\le\|f'\|_1$, and $f_\varepsilon\to f$ in the form norm $\int(1+\log(2+|\xi|))|\hat f|^2$ (dominated convergence, $f$ being in the form domain), so $Q(f_\varepsilon)\to\lambda(a)$ and $\|f_\varepsilon\|\to1$. By (B.7), $S(f_\varepsilon,f_\varepsilon)\le M_{a+\varepsilon}^2V_a^2/\pi$. The transfer with $\eta=-Q(f_\varepsilon)\ge(1-\varepsilon)|\lambda(a)|$ for $\varepsilon$ small gives the bound. The growth is Theorem~\ref{thm:plane}(iii); on the computed branch every minimizer is unimodal on each half-window (Section~\ref{sec:conj}), so $V_a=4\sup|f|$.
\end{proof}

\begin{proposition}[the paired-prime matrices and the echo tree]\label{prop:paired}
Let $\Pi_aq=\sum_{\log n<2a}c_n[q(\cdot+\log n)+q(\cdot-\log n)]$ be the atlas's prime operator on the odd window \cite[CP19 (3.3)]{michaels-atlas}, and $P_a=\sum_nc_nP_{a,\log n}$ its paired-prime matrix on the forced columns, $c^*P_ac=\langle W_ac,\Pi_aW_ac\rangle$.
\begin{enumerate}
\item[(i)] For every $q$ in the odd form domain, $\langle q,\Pi_aq\rangle=2\sum_{\log n<2a}c_n\,h_q(\log n)$ with $h_q(x)=\int q(y)q(y+x)\,dy$ the autocorrelation: the paired-prime form is the prime part of $Q$, $Q(q)=\frac1{2\pi}\int|\hat q|^2\Psi_\infty-\langle q,\Pi_aq\rangle-2P(q)^2$, and, with the cutoff $\chi_T$ of Proposition~\ref{prop:defect}, its part above $T$ is the sampling defect of the zeros above $T$ against their density.
\item[(ii)] At a support with a finite tree, on the minimizer, the part of $\langle f,\Pi_af\rangle$ carried by the frequencies above $T$ is $(M_\chi(T)-1)$ times the density integral, and by Corollary~\ref{cor:tree}(vi)
\[
M_\chi(T)-1=\frac{\langle e^TC_ae/\tilde\sigma_\infty\rangle_T}{\langle\sigma_{\mathrm{eff}}/\tilde\sigma_\infty\rangle_T}\,(1+o(1)),\qquad e=(1,\rho),\quad C_a=\begin{pmatrix}0&b^T\\ b&C\end{pmatrix},
\]
the contraction of the tree's weighted adjacency, edges included, with the amplitude vector: $e^TC_ae=2b^T\rho+\rho^TC\rho$.
\item[(iii)] In the periodic model of Proposition~\ref{prop:lattice}, with $E(\tau)=\sum_{p\in S^\circ\cup\{a\}}e_pe^{-i\tau(a-p)}$, the mean over $\tau$ of $2\cos(\tau d)|E(\tau)|^2$ is $\sum_{|p-q|=d}e_pe_q=e^TC^{(d)}e$, $C^{(d)}$ the adjacency of the step $d$ on the echo set. So the paired-prime matrix of the entry $n$, compressed to the span of the edge profile and its echoes, is the adjacency of the step $\log n$, and $P_a$ compressed to that span is the tree's weighted adjacency $C_a$: the atlas's ``point of contact'' is the echo tree.
\end{enumerate}
\end{proposition}
\begin{proof}
(i) is the prime term of \eqref{eq:Q} in the position representation, $-\frac1{2\pi}\int|\hat q|^2\,2c_n\cos(t\log n)\,dt=-c_n\langle q,q(\cdot+\log n)+q(\cdot-\log n)\rangle$, and Proposition~\ref{prop:defect}. (ii) is Corollary~\ref{cor:tree}(vi) with its proof, where $R+\tilde\sigma_\infty(|\rho|^2-1)=e^TC_ae-\dots$ is the mean of $\sum_d2c_d\cos(\tau d)|E|^2$. (iii) is the computation in the proof of Corollary~\ref{cor:tree}(iv).
\end{proof}


\begin{proposition}[the band the edge sees: support and mass]\label{prop:edgeband}
Let $C$ be the adjacency of the interior lattice $S^\circ$ restricted to the component containing the first-generation points, $\mu_b$ its spectral measure at the edge's coupling $b$, a vector with finite support, and $m_k=\langle b,C^kb\rangle/|b|^2$.
\begin{enumerate}
\item[(i)] $\sup\operatorname{supp}\mu_b=\rho(C)$: the edge's band reaches the top of its component's band. At a non-resonant support the closures of the two edges are disjoint point sets (a common point would put $2a$ in $\Lambda_a$), so that component is the interior of the right edge's closure, one component in every computed case from $a=0.9$ on, while just above $a_\infty$, at $a=0.85$, the first generation still splits into two components within $10^5$ points and chains of $9000$ steps (\texttt{rh\_lattice\_connect.py}, logs \texttt{lattice\_connect*.log}); its truncations give $\rho(C)\ge1.94$, $2.93$, $3.94$, $4.84$, $5.52$, $5.83$, $6.52$ at $a=1.0$, $1.25$, $1.5$, $1.75$, $2.0$, $2.25$, $2.5$, still rising with the truncation at $a\ge1.5$ and within $2\%$ of $2a+\log5$ at $a\ge2$; and if the closure is the full lattice $\Sigma$ of the window, as the density of the echo set suggests beyond $a_\infty$, Proposition~\ref{prop:confined} gives $\rho(C)\ge\bar\rho(a)=\sum_d2c_d(1-d/2a)$, which exceeds $2a+\log5$ from $a=1.75$ on and grows like $4e^a/a$: the edge's band then outruns the horizon.
\item[(ii)] For every even $k$ and every $s>0$, $\mu_b([s,\infty))\le m_k|b|^2/s^k$, and $m_k$ is exact on the lattice truncated at depth $g$ for $k\le2g-2$ (Proposition~\ref{prop:spectral}).
\item[(iii)] $m_1=|b|^{-2}\sum_{p^j<e^{2a}}2(j-1)(\log p)^3p^{-j}$, exactly, with $|b|^2=\sum_{p^j<e^{2a}}(\log p)^2p^{-j}\sim2a^2$; so $m_1\to0$ like $C_1/2a^2$, $C_1=\sum_p2(\log p)^3/(p-1)^2=4.6315$: the mean of the edge's measure tends to zero while its support tends to infinity.
\item[(iv)] The uniformity of the onset is therefore a statement about mass, not support: that $\mu_b([2a+\log\kappa,\infty))\le\eta|b|^2$ for some $\kappa$, some $\eta<1$ and all $a$, which by (ii) follows from $m_k\le\eta(2a+\log\kappa)^k$ for one even $k$ and all $a$, and which no bound holding for all $k$ can give, since $m_k^{1/k}\to\rho(C)$.
\item[(v)] For $k=2$ it holds with $\eta=\tfrac5{12}+o(1)$: exactly,
\[
m_2|b|^2=\|Cb\|^2=\sum_q\Big(\sum_{(m,m')\to q}c_mc_{m'}\Big)^2,
\]
the inner sum over the ordered pairs of entries whose two-step walk from the edge lands at $q$, which is $a-\log(mm')$ when $mm'<e^{2a}$ (inward) or $a-\log(m/m')$ when $m'<m$ (outward), so that the walks are grouped by the rational $mm'$ or $m/m'$; and
\[
m_2\le\frac5{12}\,(2a)^2\,\big(1+O(1/a)\big),\qquad\text{hence}\qquad\frac{\mu_b([2a+\log\kappa,\infty))}{|b|^2}\le\frac5{12}\Big(\frac{2a}{2a+\log\kappa}\Big)^2\big(1+O(1/a)\big)
\]
for every $\kappa\ge1$: the mass of the edge's measure above any fixed number of horizons is at most $\tfrac5{12}+o(1)$ of its total, uniformly in the support. Computed exactly, $m_2/(2a)^2$ rises from $0.23$ at $a=1$ to $0.39$ at $a=5$ (Computation~\ref{comp:edgemoments}).
\end{enumerate}
\end{proposition}
\begin{proof}
(i) For a bounded self-adjoint operator with nonnegative matrix elements on a connected graph with positive weights, the local growth rate $r_x=\lim_n\langle\delta_x,C^{2n}\delta_x\rangle^{1/2n}$, the top of $|\operatorname{supp}\mu_{\delta_x}|$, is the same at every vertex: $(C^{2n+2m})_{xx}\ge(C^m)_{xy}^2(C^{2n})_{yy}$, one term of a nonnegative sum, gives $r_x\ge r_y$; the union over $x$ of the supports of the $\mu_{\delta_x}$ is the spectrum, whose top is $\rho(C)$ for a nonnegative operator ($\langle v,Cv\rangle\le\langle|v|,C|v|\rangle$); so $r_x=\rho(C)$ for all $x$, and $\langle b,C^{2n}b\rangle\ge b_x^2(C^{2n})_{xx}$ at a point $x$ with $b_x>0$ gives $\sup\operatorname{supp}\mu_b\ge\rho(C)$, the reverse being trivial. The two closures are disjoint at a non-resonant support since $a+u=-a+u'$ with $u,u'\in\Lambda_a$ gives $2a=u'-u\in\Lambda_a$; the truncated top eigenvalues are lower bounds for $\rho(C)$, the restriction of a nonnegative operator to a subgraph having the smaller norm; the bound $\rho(C_\Sigma)\ge\bar\rho(a)$ is Proposition~\ref{prop:confined}, with $\bar\rho(1.75)=5.65>5.11=2\cdot1.75+\log5$ (Computation~\ref{comp:confined}). (ii) is Markov's inequality for $\nu^k\ge0$ and Proposition~\ref{prop:spectral}. (iii) Two first-generation points $a-\log m$, $a-\log m'$ are adjacent when $m/m'$ is an entry, which for prime powers forces $m=p^j$, $m'=p^i$ with $i<j$ (or the reverse) and the weight $c_{p^j}c_{p^i}c_{p^{j-i}}=(\log p)^3p^{-j}$; summing over the ordered pairs gives $\langle b,Cb\rangle$, and $\sum_{j\ge2}(j-1)p^{-j}=(p-1)^{-2}$; the denominator is Mertens' $\sum_{p\le x}(\log p)^2/p\sim\tfrac12(\log x)^2$ at $x=e^{2a}$. The formula reproduces the computed $m_1$ at every support of Computation~\ref{comp:moments} to four digits. (iv) is (ii) and (i). (v) The landing points are as stated, two-step walks landing at the same point being those with the same rational. With $N_q$ the number of walks landing at $q$, $(\sum_{(m,m')\to q}c_mc_{m'})^2\le N_q\sum_{(m,m')\to q}c_m^2c_{m'}^2$; $N_q=2$ at an inward point $mm'$ with two distinct primes (the swap), $N_q=1$ at an outward point $m/m'$ with distinct primes (the pair is determined by the rational), and $N_q\le4a/\log2$ at the points $p^k$ of a single prime, whose walks have total squared weight at most $\sum_p(\log p)^4/(p-1)^2=O(1)$. So, with $B(y)=\sum_{\log m'\le y}c_{m'}^2$,
\[
m_2|b|^2\le2\sum_mc_m^2B(2a-\log m)+\sum_mc_m^2B(\log m)+O(a)=\int_0^{2a}\big[2B(2a-y)+B(y)\big]\,dB(y)+O(a),
\]
and Mertens' theorem, $B(y)=\tfrac12y^2+O(y)$, gives $\int_0^{2a}[(2a-y)^2+\tfrac12y^2]\,y\,dy\,(1+O(1/a))=\tfrac{10}3a^4(1+O(1/a))$, while $|b|^2=B(2a)=2a^2(1+O(1/a))$; (ii) with $k=2$ gives the mass bound.
\end{proof}

\begin{theorem}[the blind spot]\label{thm:blind}
Let $f$ be odd, supported in $[-a,a]$, with $\|f\|_1<\infty$, and $S(z)=\int_0^af(u)\sin(zu)\,du$, entire of exponential type $a$ with $|S^{(k)}(z)|\le a^k\|f\|_1e^{a|\Imm z|}$.
\begin{enumerate}
\item[(i)] For real $\gamma$ and $\delta$,
\[
16\,\Ree S(\gamma-i\delta)^2=16S(\gamma)^2-16\delta^2\big(S'(\gamma)^2+S(\gamma)S''(\gamma)\big)+R,\qquad |R|\le C\,a^4\delta^4\|f\|_1^2e^{2a|\delta|},
\]
with an absolute constant $C$: the quartet of a zero at distance $\delta$ from the line contributes, against a double zero at its height, a correction of second order in $\delta$ and even in $\delta$. No functional $\sum_\rho q_f(\gamma_\rho)$ of the explicit formula, for any test function on any window, sees the distance of a zero from the line at first order; nor does the M\"obius energy, in which a quartet enters $M(x)$ through $x^{1/2}\cosh(\delta\log x)$.
\item[(ii)] Let $\Lambda_Z(a)=\inf_{\|f\|=1}\sum_{\rho\in Z}q_f(\gamma_\rho)$ for a configuration $Z$ in the strip, symmetric under $\rho\mapsto1-\rho$ and $\rho\mapsto\bar\rho$ with $\sum|\gamma_\rho|^{-2}<\infty$; $\Lambda_Z(a)=\lambda(a)$ when $Z$ is the zeros of $\zeta$. If $Z$ has a double zero at height $\gamma$ and $Z_\delta$ is $Z$ with it replaced by the quartet at distance $\delta$, then, with $f$ a minimizer for $Z$,
\[
\Lambda_{Z_\delta}(a)\le\Lambda_Z(a)-16\delta^2\big(S'(\gamma)^2+S(\gamma)S''(\gamma)\big)+O(\delta^4):
\]
the floor is a function of the configuration with the single-zero sensitivity $\delta_c(\gamma)=\big(\lambda/16S'(\gamma)^2\big)^{1/2}$ at a null $\gamma$ of the minimizer's transform.
\item[(iii)] Above the onset of the sharp form (Proposition~\ref{prop:sharp}), where $S=-|A_0|\cos(ta+\theta)/(t\tilde\sigma^{1/2})(1+o(1))$ with $2|A_0|^2=-\lambda'$,
\[
\delta_c(\gamma)^2\ \ge\ \frac{\gamma^2\,\tilde\sigma(\gamma)}{8a^2}\cdot\frac{\lambda}{-\lambda'}\,(1+o(1))\ \ge\ \frac{\gamma^2\tilde\sigma(\gamma)}{8a^2c\,\Tst}\,(1+o(1))
\]
under $-\lambda'\le c\Tst\lambda$, which exceeds $\tfrac14$ at every support computed from the onset on: above the onset the window is blind, and below the horizon it resolves the zeros to $e^{-c\Tst/2}$ (Computation~\ref{comp:blind}).
\end{enumerate}
\end{theorem}
\begin{proof}
(i) Expand $S(\gamma-i\delta)=\sum_k(-i\delta)^kS^{(k)}(\gamma)/k!$, absolutely convergent with the stated bounds; in $S(\gamma-i\delta)^2=\sum_{k,l}(-i\delta)^{k+l}S^{(k)}S^{(l)}/(k!\,l!)$ the terms with $k+l$ odd are imaginary and drop from the real part, $k+l=0$ gives $S^2$, $k+l=2$ gives $-\delta^2(S'^2+SS'')$, and $k+l\ge4$ is bounded by $\|f\|_1^2\sum_{k+l\ge4}(a\delta)^{k+l}/(k!\,l!)\le C(a\delta)^4\|f\|_1^2e^{2a|\delta|}$. The quartet's total is $8[S(\gamma-i\delta)^2+S(\gamma+i\delta)^2]=16\Ree S(\gamma-i\delta)^2$ by $S(\bar z)=\overline{S(z)}$ and $S(-z)=-S(z)$, and a double zero at $\pm\gamma$ contributes $2\cdot2\cdot4S(\gamma)^2$. For $M(x)$, a zero $\rho$ enters through $x^\rho/(\rho\zeta'(\rho))$ and its mirror through $x^{1-\bar\rho}$, whose moduli are $x^{1/2\pm\delta}$. (ii) $\Lambda_{Z_\delta}(a)\le\sum_{\rho\in Z_\delta}q_f(\gamma_\rho)=\Lambda_Z(a)+16\Ree S(\gamma-i\delta)^2-16S(\gamma)^2$ and (i). (iii) $|S'|\le a|A_0|/(\gamma\tilde\sigma^{1/2})(1+o(1))$ from the sharp form, and $\lambda/16S'^2\ge\gamma^2\tilde\sigma\lambda/(16a^2|A_0|^2)=\gamma^2\tilde\sigma\lambda/(8a^2(-\lambda'))$.
\end{proof}

What this settles about blind spots. There is no first-order detector: every quantity built from the zeros through the explicit formula, the floor and the M\"obius energy included, is even in the distance from the line, so a search for a quantity that sees an off-line zero at first order is over before it starts. The floor's sensitivity is second order with no suppression by the size of the floor: below the horizon the minimizer hides from the zeros, its transform crosses them with slopes $S'(\gamma)$ of order $10^{-3}$ to $10^{-2}$ at the first zeros while $\lambda$ is $e^{-c\Tst}$, and the sensitivity $\delta_c=(\lambda/16S'^2)^{1/2}$ is $e^{-c\Tst/2}$, $3.5\times10^{-12}$ at $a=1$ for the first zero: no blind spot below the horizon, at any support. Above about one and a half horizons $\delta_c$ exceeds $\tfrac12$ and the window is blind, at every support; the M\"obius energy, with its weight $1/|\rho|^2$, is blind in the same place. The two readings of Theorem~\ref{thm:tworeadings} therefore see the same zeros and miss the same zeros, and no functional of either kind sees the zeros above its scale. For the program this closes the search for a branch-separating quantity below the sign change: at supports where the floor is positive, every such quantity differs between the branches by a second-order term that the floor itself already carries with the sharpest coefficient, and the branch is decided only at the sign change. The Hypothesis is therefore not a matter of detection at any finite scale but of structure, which is the reduction of Theorem~\ref{thm:reduction} in the form of Proposition~\ref{prop:late}.

\begin{computation}[the blind spot]\label{comp:blind}
\sloppy \texttt{rh\_blind\_spot.py}, log \texttt{blind\_spot.log} in \texttt{data/}. On the $K$-mode minimizers (unit norm) at $a=0.6$, $0.8$, $1.0$, $1.25$ ($K=48$, $64$, $80$, $140$), the transform through spherical Bessel functions at $60$ digits with the full-precision coefficients. (i) The second-order formula: at the first zero and at the zero nearest five horizons, the exact quartet difference $16\Ree S(\gamma-i\delta)^2-16S(\gamma)^2$ against $-16\delta^2(S'^2+SS'')$ has ratio $1.0000$ at $\delta=10^{-3}$ and $10^{-2}$ and $0.996$--$1.005$ at $\delta=0.1$, at every support. (ii) The sensitivity $\delta_c(\gamma)=(\lambda/16S'(\gamma)^2)^{1/2}$, with the slopes of the transform at the zeros:

zero & $S'(\gamma)$ & $\delta_c$ & $S'(\gamma)$ & $\delta_c$ & $S'(\gamma)$ & $\delta_c$\\
\midrule

$\gamma_2=21.02$ & $1.9\times10^{-3}$ & $0.10$ & $6.3\times10^{-4}$ & $4.9\times10^{-5}$ & $3.3\times10^{-4}$ & $9.1\times10^{-11}$\\

$\gamma\approx\Tst$ & $1.9\times10^{-3}$ & $0.10$ & $1.1\times10^{-5}$ & $2.8\times10^{-3}$ & $-6.7\times10^{-10}$ & $4.5\times10^{-5}$\\
$\gamma\approx2\Tst$ & $-1.3\times10^{-5}$ & $15$ & $-2.2\times10^{-8}$ & $1.4$ & $1.5\times10^{-14}$ & $2.0$\\

\noindent The sensitivity crosses $\tfrac12$ between $1.2$ and $1.5\,\Tst$ at $a=0.6$, between $1.1$ and $2\,\Tst$ at $0.8$, and between $1.0$ and $2\,\Tst$ at $1.0$; above, it is $10$--$100$ from five horizons on, the slopes being those of the edge's cosine; below, it falls like $e^{-c\Tst/2}$, from $10^{-2}$ at $a=0.6$ to $10^{-12}$ at $a=1.0$ for the first zero; at $a=1.25$ the first zero has $\delta_c=2\times10^{-24}$. (iii) The minimizer in position space: $\sup|f|=1.462$, $1.422$, $1.400$, $1.384$, attained at $u=0.221$, $0.226$, $0.229$, $0.232$ from the origin at the four supports, a position that does not move with the support, and the total variation is $4.00\sup|f|$ in every case.

\begin{computation}[the second and fourth moments of the edge's measure to $a=5$]\label{comp:edgemoments}
\sloppy \texttt{rh\_edge\_moments.py}, log \texttt{edge\_moments.log} in \texttt{data/}. The two-step and three-step walks from the edge grouped exactly by their rational landing point, over the entries below $e^{2a}$: $m_2|b|^2=\|Cb\|^2$ and $m_4|b|^2=\|C^2b\|^2$ as finite sums, with no truncation. At $a=1.0$--$2.5$ the values reproduce every digit of the Lanczos moments of Computation~\ref{comp:moments}. The Chebyshev bounds are on the mass of $\mu_b$ above five horizons, $s=2a+\log5$:
\begin{center}\small
\begin{tabular}{@{}rrrrrrrrr@{}}
\toprule
$a$ & entries & $|b|^2$ & $m_2$ & $m_2/(2a)^2$ & $m_2/s^2$ & $m_4$ & $m_4^{1/4}/|b|$ & $m_4/s^4$\\
