$1.5$ & $12$ & $4.003$ & $2.650$ & $0.294$ & $0.125$ & $15.4$ & $0.99$ & $0.034$\\
$2.0$ & $24$ & $7.073$ & $5.154$ & $0.322$ & $0.164$ & $62.2$ & $1.06$ & $0.063$\\
$2.5$ & $47$ & $11.159$ & $8.708$ & $0.348$ & $0.199$ & $179$ & $1.10$ & $0.094$\\
$3.0$ & $98$ & $16.711$ & $12.929$ & $0.359$ & $0.223$ & $399$ & $1.09$ & $0.119$\\
$3.5$ & $209$ & $23.145$ & $18.007$ & $0.368$ & $0.243$ & $778$ & $1.10$ & $0.142$\\
$4.0$ & $465$ & $30.578$ & $23.980$ & $0.375$ & $0.260$ & & &\\
$4.5$ & $1068$ & $38.992$ & $30.841$ & $0.381$ & $0.274$ & & &\\
$5.0$ & $2532$ & $48.466$ & $38.521$ & $0.385$ & $0.286$ & & &\\
\bottomrule
\end{tabular}
\end{center}
\noindent $m_2/(2a)^2$ rises towards the asymptotic bound $5/12=0.417$ of Proposition~\ref{prop:edgeband}(v) and stays below it at every support; the Chebyshev bound with $k=2$ on the mass above five horizons rises from $7\%$ to $29\%$ and tends to $5/12$. The fourth root $m_4^{1/4}/|b|$ settles at $1.10$ from $a=2.5$ on, so the fourth-moment bound tends to $(1.10/\sqrt2)^4=0.36$, no better; and the roots $m_k^{1/k}/|b|$ increase with $k$ (to $1.2$ and $1.3$ at $k=6,8$ where exact), so the moments cannot push the uniform bound below about a third. What they establish is that the edge's measure keeps at least two thirds of its mass below any fixed number of horizons at every support; what they cannot see is how much smaller the mass above is (at $a\le1.25$, where the measure is resolved, it is $10^{-6}$ to $10^{-4}$), which is a question about the shape of $\mu_b$, not its moments.
\end{computation}


\subsection*{Where the paper rests}
The main theorem is the reduction: under (H$_\infty$), the Riemann Hypothesis follows from the inequality \eqref{eq:theinequality} at almost every support, with any locally integrable excess, and every other link is proved. The inequality is not proved, and the paper does not claim it. Its remainder in the dilation identity is the prime part $D_P$, whose sign is not fixed; a proof would have to show that the minimality of $f$ fixes it against $\Tst\lambda$, and the arithmetic in which this would have to be done is that of the moments of the edge's measure, where the second moment is now bounded uniformly in the support and the finer structure is not.

\section{Relation to the literature, and acknowledgement}\label{sec:lit}

Suzuki \cite{suzuki-screw} organizes the Weil form on $[-a,a]$ as a family of self-adjoint operators and conjectures that an operator with the zeros as spectrum is their limit as $a\to\infty$; Conjecture~\ref{conj:A} is a differential inequality for the ground state of that family. Zhu \cite{zhu} certifies both parity sectors at $a=0.8$; his Landau--Widom decay law is fitted to the even-sector floor, calibrated on $L\le1.2$ and validated at $L=1.4,\dots,2.0$, and his exploratory $950$-mode value at $L=1.19$, between $10^{-48}$ and $10^{-46}$, is for the full space of test functions, i.e.\ the even floor; the odd floors of Section~\ref{sec:grid} interpolate to $\lambda(1.19)\approx3\times10^{-44}$, and the even floor is already three orders below the odd one at $a=0.8$ (Theorem~\ref{thm:zhu}), so the two are consistent. Comparisons between his law and the odd $\Phi'$ are cross-sector and are labelled so. Connes--Consani \cite{connes-consani} treat the range $a\le\tfrac12\log2$ where only the archimedean place acts; the wall of Proposition~\ref{prop:wall} is that place acting alone at the edge for every $a$. The atlas \cite{michaels-atlas} contains an exact-rational certificate of the odd floor to $a=3/8$ with constant $1/200$, inside Zhu's range, and the first application of Theorem~\ref{thm:edge} to this operator. The folds paper \cite{michaels-folds} computed the floors to $a=1.1$, the deleted-form failure points and the symbol's negative set, and described the relay as a sawtooth, which Computation~\ref{comp:relay} measures; the M\"obius companion \cite{michaels-mobius} treats the Green-energy side of the same program.

Computation~\ref{comp:zeros} is the converse use of the explicit formula: the form was built from the primes and the archimedean symbol, and evaluating the minimizer on the actual zeros returns the floor and its derivative; with Computations~\ref{comp:identity} and the Fourier-side check the identity has now been measured from the matrices, from the symbol and from the zeros. An independent referee report on the first version of this paper identified the gaps corrected here: the identity had been asserted for the exact form on numerical grounds (now Theorem~\ref{thm:identity} for the $K$-mode form and Proposition~\ref{prop:dini} for the exact one), Conjecture~\ref{conj:A} had been phrased in terms of $\Phi$ and so presupposed $\lambda>0$, the abstract overstated several computations, the resolution threshold had been misattributed, the scaling identity carried a sign error, \eqref{eq:soft} lacked the constant $1-\log2$, and Computation~\ref{comp:grid} misdescribed its data; the report's own recomputations of every table from the data files are gratefully acknowledged.

\section*{Reproducibility}
{\sloppy All scripts and data are in \cite{michaels-repo}: \texttt{rh\_weil\_odd.py}, \texttt{rh\_weil\_arb.py} (python-flint 0.9.0), \texttt{rh\_edge\_fem.py} (numpy 2), \texttt{rh\_soft\_kink.py}, \texttt{rh\_floor\_grid.py}, \texttt{rh\_arb\_grid.py}, \texttt{rh\_dilation.py} (\texttt{python3 rh\_dilation.py a K [bits] [noprimes]}), \texttt{rh\_hadamard.py}, \texttt{rh\_fourier\_check.py} (the Fourier-side check of Computation~\ref{comp:identity}), \texttt{rh\_bv\_check.py}, \texttt{rh\_zero\_side.py} (Computation~\ref{comp:zeros}, with the zeros in \texttt{data/zeros\_6000.txt}), \texttt{rh\_tail\_law.py} and \texttt{rh\_alignment.py} (Computations~\ref{comp:tail} and~\ref{comp:align}), \texttt{rh\_sum\_rule.py} and \texttt{rh\_cutoff\_identity.py} (Computations~\ref{comp:tailid}, \ref{comp:pairing} and~\ref{comp:envelope}), \texttt{rh\_wiener\_hopf.py} (Computation~\ref{comp:whphase}), \texttt{rh\_echo\_tree.py} (Computation~\ref{comp:tree}), \texttt{rh\_sampling\_defect.py} (Computation~\ref{comp:defect}), \texttt{rh\_band.py} (Computation~\ref{comp:band}), \texttt{rh\_lattice\_band.py} (Computation~\ref{comp:confined}), \texttt{rh\_spectral\_measure.py} (Computations~\ref{comp:spectral} and~\ref{comp:moments}), \texttt{rh\_plane.py} (Computation~\ref{comp:plane}), \texttt{rh\_mirror\_chains.py} and \texttt{rh\_band\_phase.py} (Computation~\ref{comp:mirror}), \texttt{rh\_blind\_spot.py} (Computation~\ref{comp:blind}), \texttt{rh\_lattice\_connect.py} and \texttt{rh\_edge\_moments.py} (Proposition~\ref{prop:edgeband}, Computation~\ref{comp:edgemoments}), and the data \texttt{floor\_grid\_K40.csv}, \texttt{floor\_grid\_K72.csv}, \texttt{floor\_grid\_arb.csv}, \texttt{boundary\_law.csv}, \texttt{relay\_scan.csv} and the folder \texttt{data/} (edge-FEM outputs and dilation logs behind Computation~\ref{comp:hadamard}).\par}
